**Seasoning.** As of `dates[n]`, fixings with j ≤ n are realised at their closes (the as-of close included). The seasoned product's time origin is the as-of date: the remaining fixings sit at t − n/252 and the realised part enters through state inputs whose defaults reproduce the fresh product bit for bit.
- Variance products: `reference_fixing` (the last realised close, a constant first column, so a spot bump never moves a realised fixing), `realised_sum_sq` (capped Σr²) and `realised_count` (N counts the whole life). Variance swap: `inception`, so that A = 1/(t_n − inception). KO variance swap: τ = realised_count + the stopping index of the remaining returns. VKO put: survives iff realised_sum_sq + Σ_future r² < vol_ko² N / A.
- Cliquet: `reference_fixing` (the running period's start close) and `accrued` = Σ clip(r_i, LF, LC) over the realised periods.
- Autocall / Phoenix: the remaining dates, coupons (a growing coupon keeps its original index), non-call periods and monitoring dates are re-indexed; `knocked_in` (American knock-in only) sets the breach on every path; `memory_coupons` is added to the memory catch-up of the next paid coupon.

`season` returns the seasoned product or `Settled` — a trade settles when a knock-out is realised (KO swap: N_var [(A/N) Σ_{i≤τ} r_i² − K² τ/N] at T), a certain vol knock-out is realised (0), an autocall event is realised (coupon with memory + 1 + c_i at T_i), or every fixing is realised. `Settled` is deterministic cash: amount · DF(pay_time) while unpaid, 0 once paid, stderr 0. Cash-flow convention: a flow dated d is a realised flow for every as-of date ≥ d and belongs to no seasoned value from d on (ex-coupon at the payment date's close), so the P&L of (d₀, d₁] is V(d₁) − V(d₀) + Σ flows. At age 0 the trade-date close is fixing 0: bit-identical to the fresh product at a spot equal to that close, but held fixed under spot bumps. Unsupported (errors naming the class): continuous knock-in, the smart gap, simulation-grid swaps, other classes; an already-seasoned product is rejected.

Every product `season` returns carries `seasoned=True` (its dates count from the as-of date) and is refused by a second `season` — always season the fresh trade on the full history. Its discount, and `Settled.value`, use the as-of curve: the `discount` passed (anchored at the as-of date), by default the product's curve rolled forward, DF(e+τ)/DF(e) (`DiscountCurve.rolled`). `Settled.as_product()` (`SettledCash`) hands the known cash to the pricing machinery. Any gap convention, fixed or smart, raises `NotImplementedError` (history events are decided on the term-sheet levels). `hedge_state` reads the same realised state (the realised reference close starts the first period, the realised sums are added, the VKO compares with its whole-life budget, a realised knock-in breaches every path, realised memory coupons are carried), checked against the fresh product on the full path to 1e-12 for seven seasoned products.

**Tests** (`tests/test_seasoning.py`): age 0 bit-identical for the six book products; explicit defaults equal the fresh product; half-way variance swap under Black–Scholes 0.0184729 ± 2.5·10⁻⁵ against the closed form 0.0184426 (z +1.20; with spot 3 % off the reference, z +1.20); a realised KO breach settles (strictness edge included); a seasoned KO swap with B → ∞ equals the seasoned variance swap path by path; an American knock-in is carried (path by path equal to the European note at 100 %); the cliquet against a brute force to 2·10⁻⁶; the VKO budget; Phoenix memory-coupon accounting on synthetic paths.

## 7. Risk and analytics (M5 — detailed)

### 7.1 General machinery (`risk/engine.py`)
- `BumpSpec(name, apply, size, scheme="central"|"forward")` and a `RiskEngine` pricing base and bumped states under common random numbers, returning `Sensitivity(value, stderr)` with the stderr of the *difference*, path by path.
- Bumps that change the surface or a model parameter recalibrate through `LeverageCache.get_or_calibrate` (each is a cache entry; the second run of a ladder is free); bumps of S0 or the factor state do not.
- Surface perturbations are an additive layer on `ImpliedSurface` (δσ(k,T) in implied vol), with the no-arbitrage checks re-run; on failure halve the bump, retry, report.
- Default bump sizes: delta/gamma 1% of spot in log space (central three-point); vega 1 vol point; forward-variance buckets +1 vp of the bucket's forward VS vol; model parameters 5% relative (ν, k1, k2), 0.05 absolute (θ, correlations) with the PSD check re-run. Configurable.

### 7.2 Delta and gamma regimes (`risk/greeks.py`)
Smile in `k = ln(K/S)`, `Δ = ln(S0_new/S0_old)`, `s_T` = ATM skew dσ/dk at maturity T from the surface.
- `"model"`: bump S0, L and factors fixed — default, the hedger's delta.
- `"sticky_strike"`: `σ_new(k,T) = σ_old(k + Δ, T)`.
- `"sticky_moneyness"`: `σ_new(k,T) = σ_old(k, T)`.
- `"sticky_skew"`: `σ_new(k,T) = σ_old(k,T) + s_T Δ` (ATM vol slides along the old smile; shape re-centred with the same skew and curvature).
- `"sticky_local_vol"`: `σ_new(k,T) = σ_old(k,T) + 2 s_T Δ` (Derman's sticky-implied-tree regime, SSR = 2; reference only).
All but "model" rebuild the surface, recalibrate L (cached), reprice under CRN. Report all five deltas and gammas side by side. Tests: coincide in BS; ATM vanilla sticky_strike vs sticky_skew agree to first order (report the curvature term); ATM-vol shift per unit Δ ordered 0 < s_T < 2 s_T; the "model" shift equals `SSR_T · s_T` (§4.4, lands with M7).

### 7.3 Vega, theta (`risk/greeks.py`)
- Parallel vega: +1 vp at every (K,T); variants `"recalibrated"` (default) and `"sticky_leverage"` (L held, ξ₀ moves with the strip); the difference is the leverage vega, a study quantity.
- Theta: t + 1 business day, surface held in (K, absolute expiry), factors at zero, forward rolled; only for products with no fixing in the roll window. Split into carry (rates/divs), pure decay (surface held in time-to-maturity) and roll-down. BS test: θ + ½σ²S²Γ + rate terms = 0.

### 7.4 Vega by maturity — desk "wave" convention (`risk/ladders.py`, `vega_T`)
Pillars: the surface's expiry pillars (default 1m, 2m, 3m, 6m, 9m, 1y, 18m, 2y, 3y). `tent_i(T)` linear 0 → 1 → 0 over (T_{i−1}, T_i, T_{i+1}), flat beyond the ends. Pillar bump i: `σ → σ + 0.01·tent_i(T)`; check the calendar condition on every ramp (`(σ+0.01)² T_i ≤ σ² T_{i+1}`, i.e. `T_{i+1}/T_i ≥ 1.10` at σ = 20%; raise for pillar sets that violate it). Wave j = all pillars with T_i ≤ T_j bumped together; wave n = parallel vega. Report cumulative vega per wave and the projection at T_j = wave_j − wave_{j−1}; projections sum to the parallel vega. Recalibrated (default) and sticky-leverage variants; units % of notional per vol point. Tests: projections equal the single-pillar tent ladder within 2 stderr; wave_n equals parallel vega within 2 stderr. `RiskReport` docstring states that (i) projections equal single-pillar bumps only to first order (recalibration and vega convexity add a cross term), (ii) an expiry between pillars shows vega in both neighbouring projections in proportion to distance.

### 7.5 Forward-variance vega ladder (`risk/ladders.py`, `fwd_var_ladder`)
Buckets monthly to 1y, quarterly to 3y. Bump `ξ₀^T → (1+ε)ξ₀^T` on the bucket; propagated to the surface as `dW(T) = ε ∫_{bucket∩[0,T]} ξ₀` added to `w(k,T)` for every k (parallel shift in total variance per maturity; skew preserved in total-variance terms). ε sized so the bucket's forward VS vol rises 1 vp; units % of notional per vol point of bucket forward VS vol. Variants recalibrated / sticky-leverage. Tests: ladder sums to the parallel forward-variance bump (both variants, 2 stderr); flat and analytic for a plain variance swap. Second-order (diagonal convexity per bucket, three-point) on request.

### 7.6 Skew and curvature risk (`risk/ladders.py`, `skew_T`, `curvature_T`)
Skew bump i: `σ → σ + s·k·tent_i(T)`, s sized so `σ(ln 0.9) − σ(ln 1.1)` at T_i rises 1 vp (rotation around ATM); units per vol point of 90/110 skew. Curvature bump i: `σ → σ + c·k²·tent_i(T)`, c sized so `[σ(ln 0.9)+σ(ln 1.1)]/2 − σ(0)` rises 1 vp; units per vol point of 90/110 butterfly. No-arbitrage checks as §7.1; both variants. Tests: ATM vanilla zero skew vega, 90% put positive; 90/110 risk reversal zero curvature vega, strangle positive; `skew_T` sums to a global rotation within 2 stderr.

### 7.7 Cross-Greeks (`risk/greeks.py`)
Vanna as both `∂vega/∂ln S` and `∂delta/∂σ` (equal only in BS; the LSV difference is informative); volga `∂vega/∂σ`; charm and veta (one-business-day rolls, surface in (K, absolute expiry)); rho (+1 bp rates); repo/dividend delta (+1 bp in q). Cross terms `∂delta/∂ρ_SX1`, `∂delta/∂ρ_SX2`, `∂delta/∂ν` (recalibrated): the hedge ratio's own dependence on the vol-sto parameters.

### 7.8 Spot-shift profiles (`risk/profiles.py`)
S0 grid −30% to +30% in 2.5% steps; price, model delta/gamma/vega per shift under "model" dynamics (other regimes optional, recalibrated per shift, cached); gamma profile as second difference. Cliquet gamma profile versus the accumulated sum at an intermediate date, with the Bachelier call-on-remaining-capped-sum decomposition of the original study as an *approximate* cross-check: the sum of capped monthly returns is not Gaussian, so the formula was never exact under BS (owner's correction at the M5 review); the exact BS reference is the independent-legs simulation, and both the BS and the LSV deviations are reported (§7.15).

### 7.9 Model-parameter sensitivities (`risk/volsto_sens.py`)
Partials to each of (ν, θ, k1, k2, ρ12, ρ_SX1, ρ_SX2) with recalibration; ν also sticky-leverage. Central differences, PSD check on correlation bumps. One table per product.

### 7.10 Product-specific risks (`risk/product_risk.py`)
- Fixing risk: forward-start and cliquet delta/gamma/vega at T1 − 1d and T1 + 1d (state held), reported as the jump; per fixing for cliquets.
- Barrier risk: `∂price/∂B` for KO var and for the spot-barrier knock-in put of the autocall (M6 Part 3; owner's clarification at the M5 review — the M5 implementation covered the VKO knock-in variant, which stays as built); delta and gamma within ±5% of the barrier at 0.5% steps; VKO `∂price/∂H` (vol points of the vol barrier) and `∂P(KO)/∂ln S`.
- Realised-variance exposure: expected dollar-gamma-weighted variance per fixing period along the path; flat and equal to notional for a variance swap.

### 7.11 Precision options (`risk/estimators.py`)
Likelihood-ratio delta and vega for discontinuous payoffs (barriers, KO var, VKO, digitals) with the bump estimate as cross-check (flag > 3 stderr disagreement); conditional Greeks at a future date by regression of payoff and CRN bumps on a polynomial basis in (ln S_t, X¹_t, X²_t) — the hedger's engine; control variate on the difference (BS Greek at market vol for vanilla-like products), with variance reduction reported.

### 7.12 P&L attribution (`risk/attribution.py`)
`explain(product, state_0, state_1)`: sequential CRN revaluation in the order spot (delta, gamma), surface (parallel vega, vega-T waves, skew/curvature), model parameters, factor state; residual = actual minus sum. States carry S0, surface, model params, factor values, date. Tests: pure spot move residual at the gamma-cubed level (report); pure parallel vega move residual within 2 stderr.

### 7.12.1 Sticky-leverage attribution (M10 Part 3, 2026-09-16)

`explain(..., mode="sticky_leverage", product_1, product_theta)` prices every intermediate state and every Greek on state_0's leverage **held in forward log-moneyness** (builder mode `"frozen_leverage"`, the builder based on state_0) with each state's ξ₀ and parameters — no calibration between the endpoints. The Greeks are model Greeks with the leverage frozen at state_0, not recalibrated Greeks (they equal the M5 sticky-leverage vega at the same spot). Spot and rate moves are exact: the refit leverage after a sticky-moneyness move equals L₀ in k to 3·10⁻¹¹ (cached pairs, 2026-09-16). The builder's existing `"sticky_leverage"` mode re-anchors L₀ in spot, which would book 0.077 ± 0.001 (synthetic, +2 %) and −3.38 ± 0.11 (SPX 2F, +1 %, on a 186 call) as recalibration; it is unchanged because the spot profiles rely on its meaning.

The approximation shows in the final `recalibration` step only: V(state_1, recalibrate) − V(state_1, frozen L₀), paired CRN on the end product, its own bucket (explained = actual, so the total residual stays the Greek residual). On a pure spot move it is 0 ± 0 (synthetic) and 5·10⁻¹⁴ ± 1.3·10⁻¹³ (real pair). `product_1` (seasoned to date 1) replaces `product.aged(dt)` in the time step; the theta prices `product_theta`, the product seasoned with the held spot as its day-1 close (theta at state_0 cannot use `product_1`, whose day-1 fixing is S₁). **The rates step** is explained by the first-order P&L of the day's *actual* curve moves — `rho` along the rate-curve move and `repo` along the dividend-curve move, each [P(state_0 with that curve moved h of the way to state_1's) − P(state_0)] / h with h = `CURVE_MOVE_H` = 0.1 (paired CRN, one pricing per curve that moved; the blend (1−h) ln DF₀ + h ln DF₁ is exact on the union of the two pillar grids; h = 0.01 was pure noise on knock-out products). The first version explained it with the mean zero rate as a parallel shift, which is wrong on real curves: on 2022-10-28 the 7-day implied SPX dividend yield fell from 49.6 % to −0.35 % while the 6m yield moved from 3.4 % to 1.3 % (a short-end artefact of the HDN implied forwards; the importer is unchanged), and the rates-step residuals carried almost the whole book residual (Σ|residual| over 16 attributed rows 4.56 → 2.85, 2.42 → 0.44 without the 2-day variance swap). Cross terms stay in the residual by design — notably spot × carry: the 2-day variance swap's 2.4 vol-point residual on 2022-10-28, a day SPX rose 2.5 %. A model parameter counts as moved when |p₁ − p₀| > 1e-12 · max(1, |p₀|) (a ν jitter at the cap cost 12 pricings before). The one-sided difference is P′ + (h/2)P″ + O(h²), so the rates bucket carries h/2·P″ (5 % of the move's curvature) and the rates residual keeps (1−h)/2·P″ — systematic, with the sign of P″ (positive on every variance-swap row of 2022-10-27..11-01), and added to the spot × curve cross term. Removing the first-order bias costs one pricing per moved curve: the central difference multiplied barrier-product stderrs by up to 34 (autocall) and 229 (Phoenix) on those rows, and the half-step Richardson extrapolation was unbiased at equal noise on smooth products but 2.4–3.0× noisier on knock-out ones (where the bias is below 1.8 stderr); neither removes the residual's P″/2, so the one-sided scheme is kept.

**Standard errors.** Every step's actual is a paired CRN difference with its stderr. The P&L's stderr is that of the direct paired difference V(state_1, product_1) − V(state_0, product), whose two prices are already in the memo — not the root sum of squares of the steps' errors, which ignores their covariance (0.72–1.79× the paired value on the 2022 SPX rows; checked over 24 seeds: seed spread / reported stderr 0.93). Theta's carry — [P(aged) − P] at the actual curves minus the same difference at zero rates — and its roll-down, P(aged, rolled) − P(aged, held), are paired combinations on the engine's common random numbers, built from the prices theta already has (five distinct pricings in all), so their errors include the covariance (the earlier root sum of squares was ≈ 1.75× too wide for the time group and 43× for the carry on the test case); `explain`'s decay and carry buckets and the backtest rows carry these paired errors. `Explain.bucket_stderrs` gives each explained bucket's paired error from the per-path sum of its items and `Explain.group_stderrs` the paired errors of the groups spot, rates, surface, params, time, Greeks and explained, both at no pricing cost; `residual_paired` is false when some explained item has no per-path combination, and the residual's error then falls back to a root sum of squares (flagged). The residual's stderr is paired per path too (the total's per-path difference minus each explained item's per-path combination times its move), at no pricing cost; the earlier approximation was a median 3.5× too wide. With paired residual stderrs the variance-swap residuals on 2022-10-27..11-01 are 50–118 stderr (the rates convexity and the spot × curve cross term of the HDN short-end dividend artefact), and a few other rows sit at 2–4 stderr. `Explain.buckets()` sums to the total. A settled end (`product_1` a `Settled`) is priced as its cash on the end state's curve and its recalibration bucket is zero; `product_theta` is accepted only with `product_1`.

**Cost** (`attribution_cost`, a dry run counting calibrations and pricings). Per book product per date at production paths: sticky/parallel 2 calibrations (0 intermediate) and 15 pricings; sticky/ladders 2 and 52; recalibrate/parallel 11 and 14; recalibrate/ladders 48 and 51. In a backtest state_0 is the previous date's end state, so sticky mode adds one calibration per date. Synthetic LSV check (10⁴ paths): spot 0.61978 ± 0.00237 (explained 0.61993), rates 0.01463 ± 0.00007 (0.01386), surface 0.11396 ± 0.00095 (0.11350), params −0.02019 ± 0.00141 (−0.02196), recalibration 0.15161 ± 0.00126, total 0.87978 ± 0.00343, residual 0.00285 ± 0.00023 — 2 calibrations, 12 pricings.

### 7.13 RiskReport and budget
One object per product with every sensitivity, stderr, bump specs, cache keys; `to_dataframe()`, `to_excel()`. A full report is ≈ 80–100 recalibrations; print count and wall clock — it is the viewer precompute budget. Owner decision (M6 Part 0 review): the 8·10⁵ budget run is skipped; the 2·10⁵ count with the 4× per-calibration scaling (33 s → 130–140 s) is the M9 estimate. **Measured (2026-09-13, `scripts/m5_budget.py`, 1y ATM call, 1F reference LSV, 2·10⁵ particles and paths, seed 2024, default configuration — 9 pillars × 3 ladders, 20 forward-variance buckets × 2 variants, 5 regimes, 7 parameters, cross-Greeks, theta):** 89 recalibrations (76 cache misses), 134 pricings, 127 sensitivities, 80 min wall clock on a machine shared with other calibration jobs (about 50 min idle: 33–40 s per calibration plus 8 s per pricing at 2·10⁵ paths). At 8·10⁵ particles the calibrations scale 4× (130–140 s each): ≈ 3–3.5 h per product-state for the viewer precompute, dominated by the 76 distinct calibrations, which are shared across products at the same state (the study cliquet's two forward-variance ladders on the same states added 0 recalibrations and 13 min of pricing). Cliquet forward-variance ladders (study structure, 1y, % of notional per vol point of bucket forward VS vol): recalibrated sum −0.142 ± 0.001 (parallel −0.144 ± 0.001), sticky-leverage sum −0.034 ± 0.000 — the recalibrated ladder is front-loaded (−0.028 in the first month falling to −0.007 at 1y) and 4× the sticky one; the buckets beyond the 1y maturity carry nothing. The M5 budget runs found and fixed two report-path defects under the LSV builder (the unfloored ATM-skew shift of the sticky-skew / sticky-local-vol regimes and the `"model"` mode of a perturbed state), §7.15.

### 7.14 Smile-dynamics analytics (`analytics/smile_dynamics.py`, `analytics/var_decomp.py`)
As §4.4 and §15 Part 1: numerical SSR (LSV joint-bump, pure SV), eq. 12.52 decomposition, short-horizon regression estimator; vols of ATMF vols (eq. 12.56); conditional smile after a spot move at horizon t; vol-of-vol term structure; Var(V) decomposition (closed form pure SV, regression estimator LSV).

### 7.15 M5 implementation notes (2026-09-13)

Layout: `risk/engine.py` (`RiskState`, builders `LSVBuilder` / `LVBuilder` / `BSBuilder`, `RiskEngine` with `perturbed_state` (halve-and-retry on the arbitrage checks, achieved size reported), `combination` / `paired` (per-path CRN estimator with the stderr of the difference), `Sensitivity`, `product_key`), `risk/greeks.py` (five delta regimes, vega variants, theta split, cross-Greeks), `risk/ladders.py` (`vega_T`, `fwd_var_ladder`, `fwd_var_convexity`, `skew_T`, `curvature_T`), `risk/profiles.py`, `risk/volsto_sens.py`, `risk/product_risk.py`, `risk/estimators.py`, `risk/attribution.py`, `risk/report.py` (`RiskReport`, `risk_report`); `scripts/m5_budget.py`; tests `tests/test_risk_{greeks,ladders,profiles,product,estimators,attribution,report}.py` (fast on the Black–Scholes bed and local vol; slow: LSV budget report, control variate under LV).

Deviations from the text above and measured findings:
- **Bumps** are `SurfacePerturbation` layers (`parallel`, `tent`, `skew_tent`, `curvature_tent`, `shift_k`, `atm_shift`, `total_variance`, `roll`, `table`, `composite`) plus `RiskState.with_spot / with_params / with_rate_shift / with_x0`; there is no separate `BumpSpec` class — the bump specification lives in the `Sensitivity` (size, scheme, states, extras). Surface bumps are hashed into the leverage-cache key (`CalibrationSpec.perturbation`).
- **Forward-variance ladder sizing (§7.5).** The ξ₀-scaling `ε = [(σ_b+0.01)² − σ_b²]/σ_b²` moves the log-contract strip by a smile-dependent factor, 1.17 on the reference surface (K_var 6.0% against an ATM variance of 4.0%): the first monthly bucket of a 1y variance swap measured 0.04975 ± 0.00056 against the naive 0.0434 (−14%). `bucket_epsilon` now solves ε on the strip (two secant steps) so the bucket's forward VS vol rises exactly 1 vp, and the analytic reference of `varswap_bucket_sensitivity` is the model-independent strip difference `N · DF · [(σ_b+0.01)² − σ_b²] · Δt_b/T`.
- **Skew / curvature profile (§7.6).** `s·k` and `c·k²` break the butterfly / calendar checks at the short pillars; the implemented profile is `κ(k) = k_cap tanh(k/k_cap)` (k_cap = 0.5, linear near the money, saturating in the wings) with the same 90/110 normalisation; the checks still halve the skew bump once from 1y and the curvature bump 2–4 times (achieved sizes reported, sensitivities per requested unit).
- **Parameter boundaries (§7.9).** θ = 0 of the one-factor model admits no central bump: the difference is one-sided towards the admissible side (`scheme` records `forward` / `backward`), halving only when neither side is admissible (|ρ| > 1, PSD).
- **Cliquet gamma profile (§7.8).** The local-linear regression carries the ½h²m″ smoothing bias (+0.1% of notional at h = 0.03 where m″ reaches 3–4 per unit²); the value is corrected with the stencil second difference (the calibration's device). The Bachelier call-on-remaining-capped-sum is *not* exact in Black–Scholes: the Gaussian-sum step is an approximation even with independent legs (the remaining sum is bounded above by the caps with an open left tail). Measured against the exact independent-legs reference `bs_cliquet_value_mc`: the Bachelier value exceeds the true conditional value by up to 0.18% of notional at the 6m fixing of the study cliquet — 20–25% of the value around A ≈ 0 (value ≈ 1% of notional), 4% at the top of the range. The regression reproduces the exact reference within 1.5% of the value.
- **Fixing risk (§7.10).** "State held" legs are built explicitly (before: the same forward start with T1 = 1d; after: the vanilla at k·S₀; cliquets: the remaining structure with zero fixed returns), not through `aged()`, which raises for fixings inside the roll window. Black–Scholes: the forward-start straddle's delta jumps 0 → 0.116 (per unit S₀, notional 100) and gamma 0 → 0.039 with vega unchanged (0.0011 jump) — the vega-to-delta conversion ratio (delta jump / vega before) is 0.15.
- **Barrier risk (§7.10).** The library had no spot-barrier knock-in put at M5; the M5 implementation covered the VKO's knock-in variant (`knock_in=True`) through `∂price/∂H`, which stays as built; the owner clarified at the review that the §7.10 KI put is the autocall's spot-barrier knock-in put, delivered with M6 Part 3. `∂price/∂B` for the knock-out variance swap is central at ±0.5% of the barrier; `∂P(KO)/∂ln S` comes from the `"ko"` statistic leg; the ±5% profile in 0.5% steps is the model-regime spot profile.
- **Realised-variance exposure (§7.10).** Defined pathwise: the bucket's period log-returns are rescaled by 1 ± ε on the simulated paths (later log-spots shifted), the symmetric second difference E[P₊ + P₋ − 2P] is the dollar-gamma-weighted variance of the bucket (first-order spot effect cancelling), normalised by 2ε² E[Σ_b r_i²]/(T − t₀). Exactly N · DF(T) in every bucket for a daily variance swap (identity; tested within 3 stderr, flat to 1%).
- **Likelihood ratio (§7.11).** The exact first-transition score has variance ∝ 1/δ₀ and is useless on the 1/1460 first step; `lr_delta` aggregates the spot normals over the first week (score of the constant-coefficient approximation: exact under Black–Scholes and for τ = δ₀, O(τ) bias under LSV that the bump cross-check bounds); `lr_vega` is the per-step parallel instantaneous-vol score on a record-all-steps grid (frozen coefficients; the comparator is the sticky-leverage bump vega). Digital call (BS, 2·10⁵ paths): LR delta 0.02783 ± 0.00026 vs bump 0.02810 ± 0.00031 (z = −0.67), both within 3.5 stderr of the analytic value; LR vega within 3.5 stderr.
- **Conditional Greeks (§7.11).** The per-path CRN differences are normalised by S_t/S₀ (tangent-process form: exact for spot-homogeneous dynamics, the leverage-held-in-spot approximation for the LSV); the raw S₀ targets are kept for the t = 0 Greeks. A quadratic basis cannot follow the vanilla delta's sigmoid (RMSE 0.05 against the Black–Scholes delta at 6m); the default is cubic (RMSE 0.02–0.03; conditional value within 4% of the analytic price).
- **Control variate (§7.11).** The Black–Scholes control at the market implied vol of (K, T) runs on the engine's grid with the same normals (column 0 of the draws is the spot normal in every kernel); it needs the LV/LSV engine (under `BSBuilder` the control is the estimator). Measured under local vol on the reference surface (40k paths, 1y options): the control reduces the **vega** variance by 10× (ATM call, β = 0.76) and 2.9× (90% put, β = 1.3), but leaves **delta** and **gamma** essentially uncontrolled — VR 1.1–1.3 for the delta under the model, sticky-moneyness and sticky-strike moves alike, with β < 0 for the ATM call (the local vol held in spot makes the up path run at a lower vol than the down path, so the pathwise LV difference anti-correlates with the Black–Scholes one on the deep in-the-money paths) and β ≈ 0.04 for the gamma. The control is a vega device under smile dynamics.
- **Attribution (§7.12).** The spot step is the sticky-moneyness move of the state's own surface configuration (SSVI in k); the time step is explained by decay + carry (the roll-down sits in the surface step since `state_1` carries the observed end surface); `detail="parallel"` (parallel vega × mean ATM change) or `"ladders"` (tents × per-pillar ATM changes, skew and curvature ladders × 90/110 changes). Black–Scholes 1y ATM call: pure +2% spot → residual −1.4·10⁻⁴ against the analytic third-order term −4.6·10⁻⁴ (stderr 1.0·10⁻³, delta term 1.109, gamma term 0.038); pure +0.5 vp → residual 2.8·10⁻⁵ (stderr 1.6·10⁻³).
- **Engine details.** The pricing memo is keyed by the product's attributes at full precision (`product_key`; reprs round floats), the calibration key and the mode; products are rebound to the state's rate curve (rho moves discounting); `BSBuilder` counts only `recalibrate`-mode builds; a `Portfolio` product (weighted legs) was added to `products/base.py`; `openpyxl` moved into the core dependencies for `RiskReport.to_excel`. `bergomi.py` / `lsv.py` gained the initial factor state `x0` (numerics unchanged with `x0 = None`; code tag kept at `m4b`, guard refreshed).
- **Budget (§7.13).** See the table below (filled by `scripts/m5_budget.py`).


---

## 8. Hedging framework

Port the cliquet and FVA hedging studies onto a generic engine:
- `HedgeInstrument`: spot, vanilla (strike, maturity), variance swap, forward variance swap, forward-start straddle; each priced under the **pricing model** at rebalancing dates using the conditional-pricing regression (§4.4) or nested MC (configurable, regression default).
- `Hedger(strategy, pricing_model, world_model, schedule)`: paths are generated by the world model (may differ from the pricing model: different ω, θ, ρ, or a different model class); at each rebalancing date the strategy rebalances hedge quantities computed under the pricing model (delta, cap-call strip, net-sized forward variance swaps, etc.); P&L accumulated to maturity with transaction costs (bid/ask in vol points on vanillas and var swaps, bps on spot).
- Output: P&L distribution (mean, std, quantiles, worst paths), regime breakdown (by realised vol, by realised skew), tables and figures in the study format.
- First regression targets: cliquet strategy ranking and the "delta + q-weighted cap calls + net-sized var swap, monthly" result with mean P&L within ±0.2% of notional across regimes.

### 8.1 M8 machinery as built (2026-09-15; `volsto/hedging/`; the owner's detailed M8 spec of 2026-09-14, Parts 1–4; the studies are M8b)

**Accounting (time-0 money).** Every value is discounted to `t = 0`: a product's conditional value at a rebalancing date is `V_k = E[total discounted payoff | F_k]` (past cash flows included, realised payoff at maturity), so the product leg `Σ_k (V_{k+1} − V_k) = payoff − V₀` **exactly on every path** whatever the regression error, discounted values are martingales under the pricing model and no funding leg is needed; a hedge position `q_k` earns `q_k (I_{k+1} − I_k)`. The spot instrument is the total-return asset `S₀ S_t / F(t)` (a martingale), delta `S₀/F(t)` per unit spot.

**Conditional pricing by regression** (`hedging/pricing.py`, `ConditionalPricer`): the pricing model is simulated once on the union grid of the rebalancing dates and every object's fixings (with its CRN spot bumps `S₀ e^{±h}`, leverage held in spot — the `"model"` delta — and one extra simulation per target bump); at each date each object's value / delta / gamma / bump differences are regressed on the **spline-tensor basis** `hedge_basis` — a cubic truncated-power spline in `ln S_t` with 8 interior knots at the alive paths' quantiles, tensored with the *linear* terms of the other features, plus their additive quadratic (cross) terms: `(4 + n_knots)(1 + d) + d(d + 1)/2` columns, 12 / 25 / 39 / 54 for `d = 0 … 3` other features — in the object's **hedge state** (`hedging/state.py`: `(ln S_t, X_t)` plus the product's own path-dependent state: accrued variance and the in-period log return `u`, accumulated cliquet sum, the started leg's log return `u_start = ln(S_t/S_{T1})` after the forward start, knock-in status and memory of an autocall, alive flags with the settled value on terminated paths — registered per product class, an unregistered class regresses on spot and factors only with a note; a state column constant across the pricing paths at a date is dropped). The normal equations carry a ridge of `1e-3 × n_alive` (a basis direction supported by fewer than ~0.1% of the paths is shrunk, one supported by 10% biased by ~1%) and the standardised features are clipped to the pricing paths' `[0.1%, 99.9%]` range at evaluation (the fitted function is held constant beyond the tails it was fitted on). *Measured:* a global cubic polynomial (the §7.11 basis) gave a daily Black–Scholes delta-hedge P&L std 3.3× the exact-delta bound — it cannot follow the near-step delta close to expiry; the spline brings it to the bound (ratios in the test table below); the full spline ⊗ quadratic tensor (72 columns at `d = 2`, 252 at `d = 5`) overfitted the noisy sensitivity targets in the sparse corners of the state space (sixth-order cross terms: single-path fitted vegas of ±40 against a true 0 on the study cliquet's net portfolio, a variance-swap quantity of 13 000 on one world path beyond the fitted range where the spline tail turned the swap's fitted vega negative) — the linear interaction, the ridge and the clipping remove both.

**Delta and gamma by hybrid CRN bumps** (the history held). The plain bumped path scales the whole path, past fixings included, and measures the homogeneity direction (the cliquet's cap-call strip had a zero bump delta inside every period), while the derivative of the fitted value in `ln S_t` is noise one day into a period (measured on the study cliquet's strip at the first monthly rebalance: fitted-gradient delta std 0.43 against a true 0.004, correlation 0 with the closed form — the static strip hedge then left the cliquet 30× *more* volatile than delta only). The delta target at `t` is the CRN payoff difference on **hybrid paths** — the base path up to and including the column of `t` spliced to the future of the bumped path **re-anchored** at the base spot times `e^{±h}` (a §7.2 delta-regime bump, kind `spot`, re-anchored at the base spot times its own spot ratio — re-anchoring it at the base spot removed the spot move from the hybrid and drove the regime delta to ~0, fixed at the M8 acceptance with `test_regime_delta_bump_reanchors_at_the_bumped_spot`: the sticky-strike regime delta at `t = 0` equals the M5 `delta_gamma` estimator) (the bumped path's increments from the base state: exact where the increments do not depend on the level, second order otherwise; cumulative accumulators rebased at the splice, the splice step's squared log-return kept consistent for the continuous-monitoring check) —, gamma the second difference; both are regressed as derivatives in `ln S_t` (dollar delta / gamma — flat across the range for a returns-type payoff, so the constant tail extrapolation holds on the world's crash paths: a per-unit-spot delta `∝ 1/S_prev` clipped at the 0.1% quantile was 2–3× too small on the local-vol world's −70…−90% paths, which then carried the whole hedged variance of the static replication) and converted per unit spot at evaluation. For a state-free object the hybrid is the plain bumped path. The CRN spot bumps are the **`"model"` regime of every model class** (`volsto.risk.engine.model_regime_spot_bump`): the LSV re-anchors its leverage so `L(t, S)` stays fixed in spot, Black–Scholes keeps its vol, and the pure local vol holds `σ_loc(t, S)` in absolute spot (`LocalVolSurface.reanchored`) — *not* `LocalVol.bump(spot=)`, which keeps the local vol indexed in `k` (the sticky-local-vol move): measured on the placeholder surface's 1y ATM call the hedger's LV "model" delta at `t = 0` was 0.69 under the `k`-held bump and is 0.31 under the spot-held one, equal to the M5 `delta_gamma` model delta within stderr and below the sticky-strike 0.54 as Derman's rule says; the §7.11 `conditional_greeks` estimator shares the helper. The **model bumps** are hybridised the same way (`hybrid_bumps`): a bumped model's path has its own history, so the plain CRN difference of a path-dependent payoff carries the history's sensitivity as noise (measured on the study cliquet's static replication `cliquet + strip − put = Σ rᵢ`, whose vega is zero: the plain-bump vega exposure grew from 0.22 to 0.80 over the year and the variance-swap quantity it drove was noise; hybridised, 0.02–0.05). Every bumped path set is kept in memory for this (`budget["pricing_paths_gb"]`: ≈ `(3 + bump sides) × 5 arrays × n_paths × n_cols × 8` bytes — 1.5 GB at 2·10⁴ paths, 260 columns, one two-sided bump). `stream_bumps=True` (`Hedger` / `ConditionalPricer`, owner decision (d) at the M8 acceptance) writes each bumped set per array as `.npy` files into a fresh `volsto-bumps-*` scratch directory (`scratch_dir`, else `$VOLSTO_SCRATCH`, else the system temp) as soon as it is concatenated and memory-maps it back per date instead of keeping it (`budget["streamed_paths_gb"]`; identical P&L to 10⁻¹², the directory removed by `close()` at the end of the run — `test_stream_bumps_matches_in_memory`). *Measured (Black–Scholes, `test_hybrid_crn_delta_holds_the_state`):* the started forward-start call's hybrid delta matches its closed form per path; at the study cliquet's first rebalance the residual delta of `cliquet + strip − put` is 0.00983 ± 0.00066 against the closed-form `Σ rᵢ` delta 0.00984 (was 0.002 ± 0.53). The fitted-gradient estimator is kept as `delta_estimator="gradient"` for diagnostics. A rebalancing date that coincides with a fixing sees the post-fixing state (rebalance after the fixing, as the spec asks; `Schedule.after_fixing` can displace it).

**Control variate on the difference (§7.11; owner decision (b) at the M8 acceptance, A3b).** The model-bump targets of the objects with a Black–Scholes proxy are controlled with the same payoff difference under a Black–Scholes **shadow** on the same spot normals (`hedging/controls.py`; `ConditionalPricer(control_variate=True)`, `Hedger(control_variate=True)`): the regression target becomes `y_i − β (c_i − E[c_i | state_i])` with `β = Cov(y, c)/Var(c)` on the alive paths of the date and `E[c_i | state_i]` the analytic Black value difference given the object's state at `t` — exact for the shadow, so the regression stays unbiased whatever `β`. The shadow is the Black–Scholes law on the pricing draws' spot Brownian `W` (`GaussianDraws` with the pricing seed, Brownian column 0, accumulated once at the record columns — one `(n_paths, n_cols)` array for every leg, vol and date instead of one path set per proxy vol), spliced at `t` like the hybrid targets: `ln S_T = ln S_t^base + ln F(T)/F(t) − ½σ²(T − t) + σ (W_T − W_t)` with the pricing model's forward curve — the numbers `BlackScholes.simulate_chunk` produces on the same seed to 6·10⁻¹⁵ (`test_shadow_brownian_matches_black_scholes_kernel`). Per leg the base proxy vol is the pricing surface's implied vol at `(K, T)` (a Black–Scholes pricing model's own `vol` — the shadow is then the model itself and the controlled vega target is analytic to round-off); the bumped vols are base + [bumped surface vol − base surface vol] at the leg's `(k, T)`, the perturbed states' surfaces travelling on the `Bump` (`up_surface` / `dn_surface`: vega / volga parallel, forward-variance buckets, the `skew_T` / `curvature_T` tents) or, for a bare Black–Scholes context, the flat shifts (`vol_shift_up` / `vol_shift_dn`); `c_i` is scaled exactly like the target (`(u − d)/2 unit`, `(u − base)/unit`, the volga second difference). Proxies in this version (state-free objects only): `EuropeanOption` (Black), `DigitalOption` (cash-or-nothing Black), `ForwardStartOption` / `ForwardStartStraddle` and `Portfolio`s whose legs all have a proxy (the forward risk reversal, the forward-start straddle, the `T1`-struck vanilla family, vanilla spreads) **before `T1` only** — the conditional value given `S_t` is the unconditional forward-start Black price on the ratio at the surface vol of `(ln m, T2)`, a proxy vol whose expectation is exact by construction; after `T1` no control (the proxy would need the history held, `S_T1`; noted). Everything else — cliquets, autocalls, barriers, variance products, accumulated-sum options — keeps its raw bump targets with one note per class. The hybrid-CRN spot bumps and the `param:<name>` bumps are untouched. Reported: `Fit.variance_reduction` (`Var(y)/Var(y − β (c − E c))` on the alive paths per bump, 1.0 without a control), `Fit.beta`, `Fit.controlled`, and `budget["cv_reduction_median"]` (the median over dates and controlled objects; NaN when nothing was controlled). Alongside, `PricingContext.bump` now halves a tent / forward-variance bump that fails the surface's arbitrage checks (M5's `perturbed_state`, `MAX_HALVINGS = 4`, the `unit` scaled so the target stays per +1 vp of 90/110, noted): before, the `skew_T:2` tent on the placeholder surface raised `ArbitrageError` (calendar condition at `T = 2`), a `ValueError` the hedger caught and silently turned into a dropped target — the FVA "+ skew" study line above may have run without its skew target (open question (e)). *Measured (local-vol pricing model on the placeholder surface, 8·10³ paths, weekly steps; `test_control_variate_vega_vanilla_lv`, `test_control_variate_forward_risk_reversal_skew_tent`):* 1y ATM call, `vega` at `t = 0.5`: variance reduction **4.24 ± 0.17 (path bootstrap)** (β = 0.86, R² of the vega regression 0.039 → 0.134), the controlled and raw fitted vegas' cross-path means differ by +0.25 ± 0.47 (the control's sample mean against its analytic expectation: consistent with 0); through the hedger (delta + vega with the spot and a 110 call, monthly) the median reduction over 12 dates × 2 objects is **5.35 (median over dates and objects; each reduction carries its bootstrap se, `Fit.variance_reduction_se`, and the budget reports the median and inter-quartile spread)**. Forward risk reversal 1y → 2y (90/110), `skew_T:2` at `t = 0.5` (tent halved to 0.5 vp by the arbitrage check): variance reduction **1.078 ± 0.013** (β = 3.1, R² 0.011) — the forward-start's tent response under local vol is a forward-smile effect that runs through the spot level over `[T1, T2]`, which a flat-vol proxy on the ratio can only partly follow; a proxy conditioning on `S_T1` (a started-leg shadow with the history held) is the next step if the skew-target noise (open question (b)) is to come from the control rather than the budget. Under a Black–Scholes pricing model the shadow is the pricing model itself: β = 1 exactly and the controlled target is the analytic per-path vega (the reported reduction — 52 for the 1y ATM call's vega at `t = 0.5`, 45 for the digital's, 4·10³ paths — is the raw target's variance over that of the true conditional vega across paths, the state dependence the regression then fits).

**Minimum-variance delta (`delta_regime="min_variance"`; owner's decision of 2026-09-16, M8b study D).** The strategy's delta target is replaced by the pricing model's minimum-variance spot-only delta, for the product (or the net object) and every priced active instrument (`hedging/hedger.py`, `spot_factor_projection` / `min_variance_delta`):

    Δ_MV = Δ_model + Σ_i (∂V/∂X_i) d⟨X_i, S⟩/d⟨S, S⟩,    d⟨X_i, S⟩/d⟨S, S⟩ = ρ_Si / (S_t σ_t)

from the model's own SDE (§3.3, §3.5): the factors are unit-vol OU processes, `d ln S = … + σ_t dW^S` with `σ_t² = L(t,S)² ξ_t^t` (what `instantaneous_variance` returns and `PathSet.variance` records), and `corr(dW^S, dW^i) = ρ_Si`. `σ_t` is the pricing model's at the world state. No extra simulation: `∂V/∂X_i` is the gradient of the **controlled** value regression `value_cv` (the §7.11 Black–Scholes shadow of the payoff centred at its Black value given `S_t`, fitted only in `min_variance` runs), because the raw value gradient is noise-dominated — measured on the study-D vanilla, the raw-gradient MV hedge was WORSE than the model delta (3.218 ± 0.029 against 3.144 ± 0.011 % of spot) and the controlled one is the best of five (1.701 ± 0.013). An object without a Black–Scholes proxy (autocall, cliquet) keeps the raw gradient, noted; at t = 0 the gradient is borrowed from the next date's fit, noted. A factor-free model (Black–Scholes, local vol) has a complete market in the spot, so `min_variance` IS the model delta (bit-identical P&L, noted); a model with factors but no readable spot-factor correlation raises.

**Delta control (`control_delta`, off by default).** The §7.11 control extended to the hybrid-CRN delta target and to a regime's delta (`ConditionalPricer.delta_control`): the legs' shadow payoffs spliced at `t` from `S_t e^{±h}`, differenced over `S_up − S_dn`, with the Black finite difference as their expectation. Under Black–Scholes the control is exact and the daily 1y ATM call's hedged std over the analytic-delta hedge falls from 1.133 ± 0.006 to 1.074 ± 0.005 (2·10⁴ paths; the diagnosis's 1.110 was read against a hedge missing the total-return factor). Under the 2F marking LSV the delta target's variance falls (per-date reduction 2.56 → 1.12 from t = 0.02 to 0.95) but the study-D hedges barely move and two rows get worse (std on/off, paired: model 1.0018 ± 0.0007, sticky_moneyness 1.0033 ± 0.0006; sticky_strike 0.9979, sticky_skew 0.9982, min_variance 0.9954), so under the owner's rule ("on only if it helps and never hurts") `DEFAULT_CONTROL_DELTA = False`. Why the target's variance falls while the hedge does not improve is open.

**§7.11 coefficient (fix, 2026-09-16).** The control is centred, `c − e` with `e = E[c | state]` varying with the state; the coefficient is now the variance-minimising one for that control, `β = Cov(y, c − e)/Var(c − e)` (`ConditionalPricer.controlled_target`, used by the surface-bump controls, the delta control and `value_cv`), so the sample variance reduction is ≥ 1 by construction. The previous `Cov(y, c)/Var(c)` held that property only empirically (a constructed case gave 0.934). Under Black–Scholes, where `y = c`, the new β is the sample estimate of a population 1 (0.99837 at 8·10³ paths) where the old one was exactly 1. The committed studies A and B are unaffected: an A row is identical to 4·10⁻¹³ and a B row's std ratio is 0.9999.

**Recalibration strip at forward moneyness (bug fix, 2026-09-16).** The rule's forward-start options pay `(S_{t+τ}/S_t − m)^±`, a strike relative to the SPOT at `t`; the strip is now struck at `m = F(t+τ)/F(t) · e^{k}` and inverted there (`Hedger._strip_objects`, `_state_surface`, which raises on a mismatch), so the reads sit at forward log-moneyness exactly `k`. Before, the strike was `e^{k}` — forward log-moneyness `k − ln(F(t+τ)/F(t))` — while the reads were labelled at `k`. Measured at t = 0 on the study-C pricing twin (8·10⁴ strip paths): the "ATMF" read was 0.2225 / 0.2364 / 0.2373 at 3M / 1Y / 3Y, which is the snapshot's vol at the SPOT strike (0.2230 / 0.2368 / 0.2380), against its ATMF 0.2200 / 0.2275 / 0.2218; after the fix it reads 0.2198 / 0.2266 / 0.2215 (z −0.35 / −1.37 / −0.39). Every study-C refit of 2026-09-15 built its break-even targets on a level up to 1.6 vp too high at 3Y. After the fix, on the worst study-C world (VKO put 12m, +3 rota, 8·10⁴ strip paths) 133 of 252 dates still read a degenerate 3M step 0 — and the pricing model's own twin, with no shock, reads 143: the short pillar's conditional ATM curvature is often genuinely negative under the 2F LSV, not only noisy.

**Curvature stencil (`RecalibrationRule.curvature_h`; the owner's value is pending).** The curvature read is a second difference whose Monte Carlo error scales as `1/(curvature_h √strip_paths)` and whose smoothing of the smile grows with `curvature_h`. `None` (default) is `h`, the three-strike strip; any other value adds `±curvature_h` (five strikes per pillar), level and skew from `±h`, curvature from `±curvature_h`. The base fit (the sticky policies' held targets, the guarded fallback's correlation) and every refit read their surface through `RecalibrationRule.marking_targets` on the same stencil (`fit_2f_marking(…, h=curvature_stencil, skew_h=h)`), so a refit never sees a stencil mismatch; without `skew_h` the M7 marking fit is unchanged. This moved the held `Corr_BE` of study C from −0.904 / −0.901 / −0.903 (the M7 read, which differed from the strip's stencil already) to −0.895 / −0.897 / −0.901. Worst world at 8·10⁴: `curvature_h = 0.05` 133 degenerate dates of 252 and 459 s of strips; `0.10` 54 and 751 s. Smoothing bias of the ±0.10 stencil on the snapshot: +0.066 / +0.016 / +0.004 at 3M / 1Y / 3Y (±0.05: +0.016 / +0.004 / +0.001). Given that the 3M curvature is often genuinely negative, a wider stencil partly smooths away a real feature, not only noise.

**Static legs are netted before the regression.** A strategy's constant static legs (a whole-life, fixed instrument with a constant quantity) are regressed together with the product as one `Portfolio` and the netted legs' Greeks zeroed in the solve: least squares is linear, so the hedged Greeks' noise becomes that of the *net* payoff (nil for an exact static replication) instead of the sum of the legs' regression noises (measured on the study cliquet under local vol: separately regressed legs left the residual delta noise at 3× the delta-only P&L; netted, the static replication beats delta only, table below). The P&L accounting stays per leg (static legs telescope exactly). Sensitivities to the factor state `dX_i` come from the gradient of the fitted value polynomial; `vanna` from the gradient of the fitted vega. The fitted polynomials are evaluated at the world paths' hedge states; a world with another factor structure gets the pricing factors imputed as their conditional mean given the spot under the pricing model, `E[X_t | ln S_t]`: each factor regressed, over the pricing paths at that date, on the pricer's own cubic spline in `ln S_t` (standardised, clipped to the pricing paths' 0.1–99.9 % range, 8 quantile knots — the value fits' spline; `ConditionalPricer.pricing_factors`, noted in the report). Held at 0 until 2026-09-23: under a fit with `ρ_SX ≈ −0.9` and `ρ12` at its admissible minimum the pricing paths' `(ln S_t, X¹_t, X²_t)` are nearly collinear at short horizons and the tensor fit evaluated at `(ln S_t, 0, 0)` is unconstrained — the M8b study-B pure-LV rows of 2026-09-22 (KO var hedged std 117 vol points). A straight line in `ln S_t` misses the conditional mean in the `ln S` tails by 0.4–1.6 conditional sd — where the KO var knocks out — and left the hedged std at 3.9 against 2.5 with the spline (same-world reference 2.8, 2·10⁴ paths); on a world whose factors are uncorrelated with the spot the spline's fit noise is 0.1 σ_X at one day where 0 was exact (bounded by the test; a ridge would damp it).

**Part 1 — `Hedger`** (`hedging/hedger.py`): `Hedger(pricing, world, schedule, costs, recalibration, sim, world_paths, world_seed)`; `schedule` daily / weekly / monthly (or explicit) plus every product fixing and instrument roll date inside `(0, T)`; `costs` half-spreads (bps of the traded spot value; vol points × Black vega on options, × `2 K_vol` × remaining window on variance swaps) with the zero-cost total always reported next to the costed one; early termination from the hedge state (autocall called, barrier / KO-var / VKO knocked): every quantity zeroed from the termination date (unwind), the product leg holds the settled value; `PricingContext` — a bare model (Black–Scholes offers vega / volga through its own vol) or a `RiskState` with the M5 builders (`LSVBuilder` through the leverage cache, `LVBuilder`, `BSBuilder`) for the surface-bump targets (vega, forward-variance buckets, `skew_T` / `curvature_T` tents, `param:<name>`, the §7.2 delta regimes); the projected wall clock (one 1000-path probe scaled by the number of bumped models and the regression count) is printed before each run. `RecalibrationRule("on_skew_move")`: at each rebalancing date `t` the world's conditional smile at the rule's tenors `τ` is read from a strip of **forward-start options** `(S_{t+τ}/S_t − e^{k})^±`, `k ∈ {−h, 0, +h}`, priced under the **world** model by the same regression engine and inverted **path by path** on the ratio (forward `F(t+τ)/F(t)`, maturity `τ`) — the ATMF vol, the central-difference skew and the second-difference curvature averaged over the paths that invert (a strike fixed at `F(t+τ)e^{k}` would sit anywhere from far below to far above a path's own forward; inverting the path-*averaged* price gives the unconditional `(t+τ)`-option, whose vol over `τ` carries the spot variance over `[0, t]` — measured: the 3M ATMF vol read 0.218 at `t = 0` and 0.394 at `t = 0.5` on a world with no shock, `sqrt((t+τ)/τ)`, and the rule refit at every date). The trigger is the world's skew **in excess of the pricing model's own prediction**: the same strip under the pricing model on the same grid, paths and seed (a CRN twin) is read the same way and the rule fires when the excess moved by more than `skew_move_threshold` (default 0.01, owner decision (c), above the 0.002–0.009 proxy noise) since the reference — the excess at `t = 0`, reset at each refit. Under world = pricing the excess is identically 0 (the twin is the same simulation: no refit, recalibration P&L exactly 0); an LSV world's *forward* skew at `t` differs from its spot skew at `0` by far more than the threshold with no shock at all (the M8 rule compared the world's skew with its own `t = 0` value and refit at every one of 11 monthly dates), which the twin removes; a *mismatched* pair (Black–Scholes world, LSV pricing set) refits at the first date because the model predicts its 3M skew to decay from the spot −0.66 to a forward −0.50 within a month while the world's stays 0 — a genuine surprise, checked with a stub refit. On a trigger the P1 set is refit under `policy`. Under `sabr_linked` every target is read on the state surface. Under `sticky_breakeven` the SpotVolCovar target and the correlation target `Corr_BE` are held at the base fit's values. The variance targets (VolVar, VoV_BE) are not held in marking mode, because the fitter derives them: step 3's VolVar target is `(SpotVolCovar_model/Corr_BE)²`, so holding `Corr_BE` is what holds it. Measured on SPX 2022-12-30 at +1 rota: the former five-array holding and the two-array one give identical parameters; each of the two held arrays moves the fit on its own; the three variance arrays alone are inert (owner's decision of 2026-09-16: no fitter support for held variance targets). `sticky_breakeven_skew` also holds the two-point skew reference. The holding is done by `held_targets`, marking targets only, shared with the shadow-rotation greek. The pricing model is rebuilt with the new parameters through the cache (the refit's leverage is a calibration unless cached), and the **recalibration P&L** `V_k(new) − V_k(old)` at the same state is isolated as its own leg.

**Strips (rebuilt 2026-09-16).** The world strip and its CRN twin run at their own path count, `RecalibrationRule.strip_paths` (default `DEFAULT_STRIP_PATHS` = 8·10⁴, independent of `world_paths`). The strip's ATM level and skew converge fast; its curvature, a second difference over h² = 0.0025, does not. On the study-C autocall state it reads −1.22 / −0.53 / −0.34 at 2·10⁴ paths against −0.56 / +0.39 / +0.56 at 1.6·10⁵, and no pillar is guarded from 8·10⁴. That is one state: on the 1y daily VKO put world (+3 rota) the 3M pillar still reads a degenerate step 0 on 136 of 252 dates at 8·10⁴ (133 after the forward-moneyness fix below, and 143 on the unshocked pricing twin — the short pillar's curvature is often genuinely negative), three of the four refit dates that used the fallback clear only at 3.2·10⁵, and one refit lands at the bound with no step-0 flag (ρ_SX1 = −0.9944 at t = 0.730, regular at 3.2·10⁵). At 8·10⁴ it is the guarded fallback, not the path count, that protects the short pillar. `Hedger.strip_surfaces` computes every date's state surface before the product's pricer is built: the world strip is built, read and released, then the twin; the loop and the refit read the same precomputed surfaces (`StripSurfaces`). The strip pricer is lean (`_StripPricer`): it simulates the base model only, chunk by chunk, keeping `ln S` and the factors at the rebalancing dates plus the payoff matrix, and builds no spot-bump sets and no hybrids. Its value regressions are bit-identical to the full pricer's at equal path counts (tested). A 1y daily strip at 8·10⁴ paths holds 1.94 GB (peak 3.18 GB over the strip phase); the full pricer at that count was not run — an estimate from its component sizes puts it near 22 GB.

**The refit's correlation target (`refit_targets`).** Step 0's radicand guard is correct for a genuinely negative ATM curvature, but it must not fire on Monte Carlo noise: once it fires, `Corr_SABR = 2 smi/(√3|smi|)` is clipped to ∓1 whatever the smile, and under `sabr_linked` `Corr_BE = −1` then makes the collapsed set `ρ_SX1 = ρ_SX2 = −1, ρ12 = +1` step 3's exact minimiser. Three things protect the refit:
- the strip path count;
- a cap `|Corr_BE| ≤ REFIT_CORRELATION_CAP` = 0.97 on every refit. At 0.975 the named state still gives ρ_SX1 = −0.990; at 0.97 it gives −0.988 / −0.939 / +0.907, at no cost to λ, k1, the achieved skew or SpotVolCovar. A converged 3M read sits at −0.955;
- as the last belt, a **guarded fallback**: on a date where the guard fired or the ρ clip applied at any fitted pillar, the base fit's `correl_target` is held for that date only — the whole vector, even when one pillar is degenerate, and the state surface's SpotVolCovar target is kept under `sabr_linked` (it is built from the guarded reduction; on the named state step 2 is unchanged by the fallback). The base fit is therefore computed under every policy, before the first rebalancing date.

The refit-only skew tolerance and the soft-skew fallback are not built: the diagnosis measured that neither removes the pinning, and the two-point QP is feasible at the pinned dates. Every refit's log row, and its row of `recalibrations`, records its step-0 flags, `fallback_applied`, `corr_capped` and `at_bound`.

**Part 2 — instruments** (`hedging/instruments.py`): `Spot`, `Vanilla`, `Digital` (cash-or-nothing or its call-spread replication with a width), `option_strip`, `ForwardStartStraddle`, `ForwardStartRiskReversal`, `ForwardStartVanilla` (a vanilla struck at the `T1` fixing and held from `T1` — the desk's near-the-money strikes once a forward start has fixed; a path-dependent strike, hence a forward-start option with its activity window starting at `T1`), `CapCallStrip` (the cliquet's per-period cap calls, `q` weights), `AccumulatedSumPut` (the global-floor leg), `VarianceSwap` / `ForwardVarianceSwap` / `VolSwap`, `ConditionalVarianceSwap` — thin wrappers around the library's products (priced by the same regression engine on the same grid and draws as the product they hedge) carrying the cost rule, an activity window and a roll rule (`"fixed"`, or `"constant_maturity"` re-struck at the forward moneyness every `roll_every` — spot-ATM rolling would need path-dependent strikes and is not offered).

**Part 3 — strategies** (`hedging/strategies.py`): Layer A `GreekTargetStrategy(targets, instruments, static, ridge, turnover, delta_regime)` solves, per date and per path, the weighted least squares of the target sensitivities (closed-form batched normal equations) with static legs held as given and the residual exposure per target reported; the solve carries a relative ridge (`ridge`, default 1e-3) on each instrument's squared sensitivity, floored at the instrument's cross-path mean at the strategy's *first* date, so an instrument whose sensitivity decays over the life (a variance swap's vega near expiry) or crosses zero on a path (its regressed vega) cannot take a noise-driven quantity (measured: the cliquet preset's var-swap quantity on the SPX marking fit at 6·10³ paths reached hundreds on single paths in the last month, P&L std 11–18 ± 5–8 % of notional; a well-conditioned solve is biased by ~0.1%); Layer B presets registered per product class (`PRESETS`, `default_strategy`), derived from `decompose()` and the product's structure as the owner listed (vanilla / digital, forward start / FVA with the forward variance swap and forward risk reversal, and the switch to the `T1`-struck vanilla family after `T1`, variance / vol swap with the log-contract strip, cliquet with the cap-call strip + accumulated-sum put + variance swap on vega, conditional variance with the complementary corridor leg, knock-out variance with the barrier call spread, VKO with the underlying put + variance swap + vanna, autocall with call spreads at the observation levels + put and digital put at the barrier, barriers with the in–out parity legs); each preset exposes its target and instrument lists (`without(...)` drops a leg); `CustomStrategy` takes a callable. Targets unavailable in the pricing context are dropped with a note, never silently.

**Part 4 — reports** (`hedging/report.py`): distribution (mean, std with its fourth-moment standard error, quantiles 1/5/50/95/99 with 200-draw bootstrap standard errors, the worst 10 paths with realised vol, spot return, end factor state and termination), regimes (realised-vol terciles, skew-proxy change — the world's `X1` move when the world has factors, else the realised-vol change between the two halves, noted —, early termination), the life-aggregated attribution with standard errors (`delta × ΔS`, `½ gamma × ΔS²`, the factor channel `∂V/∂X_i × ΔX_i`, hedge legs, recalibration, costs, residual — the surface-level moves the desk cannot observe in a model world sit in the residual, said so), the residual-exposure report per date, Excel export.

**Skew-shock world** (M8b study C, "same book as B, pricing = marking fit, world = pricing but with a skew shock: +1, +2, +3 rota applied linearly over 5 business days mid-life, then held"; `hedging/worlds.py`, `skew_shock_world`). The world is the base LSV model before `t0`; over `[t0, t_end]`, `t_end = t0 + SHOCK_DAYS/DAYS_PER_YEAR` (5/252), its leverage blends **linearly in time** to the leverage calibrated on the rotated surface (the base surface under the M7 `rotation` perturbation of `rota` rotas, base P1 set, read from the leverage cache through `LSVBuilder` — a study script may calibrate on a miss, tests never), and it holds the rotated leverage afterwards: `L_world(t_i) = (1 − w_i) L_base(t_i) + w_i L̃_rot(t_i)`, `w_i = clip((t_i − t0)/(t_end − t0), 0, 1)` per slice, with slices **added at `t0` and `t_end`** when absent (both leverages interpolated there first) so the blend is exact at the boundaries and, the slices being required grid times of the LSV model, the simulation grid contains them. **Approximation — the shock is applied through the leverage; the kernel (2F parameters and `ξ₀` of the base surface) is the base kernel throughout.** The rotation preserves the ATM vols exactly but *not* `ξ₀`: the variance-swap strip integrates the steepened put wing, so on the reference surface +1 rota raises `ξ₀` by 5.7% / 3.6% / 3.1% / 1.9% at 1M / 6M / 1Y / 5Y in variance (+3 rota: 19.7 / 11.4 / 9.6 / 5.7%; `test_shocked_surface_moves_skew_keeps_atm_and_moves_xi0`). Since `ξ₀` enters the lognormal kernel only as the deterministic multiplier `g(t) = ξ₀(t) e^{−½ω²χ(t,t)}` of `ξ_t^t`, `LSV(kernel_rot, L_rot)` is in law exactly `LSV(kernel_base, L̃_rot)` with `L̃_rot = L_rot · sqrt(ξ₀_rot/ξ₀_base)` at the slice times, and the world blends towards `L̃_rot` (`xi0_rescale=True`, the default; `False` keeps the raw leverage-only approximation for comparison): after `t_end` the world is the rotated LSV model up to the within-step treatment of the slowly varying ratio (the frozen-leverage rule holds `L̃` at the step start while the second-order SV step carries `½ ∂_t ln g`; on a synthetic pair of kernels with `ξ₀` differing by +25%/yr in slope the CRN price difference of a 1y ATM call at daily steps is −0.0015 ± 0.00001 on 7.40, 0.02%; on the reference surface at +1 rota the ratio's log-slope peaks near 0.8/yr around 1M–2M — three times the synthetic pair's — and is 0.014/yr beyond 6M, so the O(dt) effect is a few 10⁻³ of the variance per daily step at the short end and 3·10⁻⁵ beyond 6M). Between `t0` and `t_end` the world is an interpolation, not a calibrated model — only the boundaries reprice a surface. Metadata returned with the model: `rota`, `t0`, `t_end`, both cache keys, whether any leverage was calibrated, mean `|L_rot/L_base − 1|`, max `|ξ₀_rot/ξ₀_base − 1|`, the 6M 90/110 skew of both surfaces (`shocked_surface` / `skew_90_110` for reports: 9.217 → 9.777 vp at +1 rota, 10.897 at +3), slices added. `lv_shock_world` is the pure local-vol analogue (two Dupire builds through `LVBuilder`, no calibration): the local **variance** blended linearly in time on the shared Dupire grid with the boundary slices added — the variance because `LocalVolSurface` interpolates the variance linearly between slices (as `LeverageFunction` interpolates `L`), so the world's variance is exactly linear across the window whatever the slice density. Tests (`tests/test_hedging_worlds.py`, 8, no calibration): synthetic blend 1.0 → 1.2 equals the base before `t0`, the rotated after `t_end`, 1.05 / 1.10 at a quarter / half of the window, exact at both added slices; the blended LSV (reference 2F parameters, flat `ξ₀ = 0.04`) prices a 1y ATM call at 8.055 ± 0.040 between the endpoints 7.397 ± 0.036 and 8.701 ± 0.044 (2·10⁴ paths, weekly steps); the cache-backed test with the reference 2F state at 8·10⁵ particles and `allow_calibrate=False` asserts `CacheMissError` on the uncached rotated leverage and that nothing was calibrated.

**Deviations from the M8 spec, all deliberate and noted.** (1) Nested Monte Carlo pricing is not offered (regression only). (2) Spot-ATM rolling of constant-maturity instruments is not offered (forward-moneyness strikes fixed at inception); the one path-dependent strike the presets need — the vanilla family struck at the `T1` fixing after a forward start — is `ForwardStartVanilla` (measured: a family at strikes fixed at inception hedged the FVA's post-`T1` straddle from mismatched strikes and, through their gross gamma under monthly rebalancing, made the preset worse than delta only, 0.170 vs 0.104 at 2·10⁴ paths). (3) Under a world with a different factor structure the pricing factors are inferred as `E[X_t | ln S_t]` under the pricing model (a per-date spline regression on the pricing paths), not filtered from the world's path (held at 0 until 2026-09-23; see Part 1). (4) The recalibration rule refits the P1 set on a state surface built from a three-strike strip at the rule's tenors (the two-point constraint relocates to those tenors); the leverage is re-derived by parameter key from the cache (a new set is a calibration unless cached), as the spec asks. (5) Vanna is the gradient of the regressed vega, volga a second difference in the parallel vol. (6) The original cliquet / FVA study rankings are not available (the study archive is absent): the machinery's ranking on the placeholder surface is recorded by the test as the baseline. (7) Smart-gap shifted levels are not applied to the autocall call spreads (noted by the preset). (7b) The autocall preset as first committed scaled the product's *absolute* `autocall_levels` / `ki_barrier` by `spot_reference` a second time (call spreads struck at spot × level worth 0, the barrier put deep in the money): the M8 end-to-end test only asserted finiteness, so the M8 study table's autocall / Phoenix preset lines were meaningless and the first M8b runs produced quantities of 10¹³⁷ on the dead call spreads (fixed 2026-09-15; the test now bounds every leg's P&L std at 10× the product's and the hedged std at 2× the unhedged; on the placeholder the autocall preset takes the 3y note's P&L std from 0.230 to 0.090 % of notional at 4·10³ paths). The per-path solve now takes an instrument out of a path's system when its squared sensitivity falls below `DEAD_SENSITIVITY` (10⁻¹²) × its first-date level instead of scaling it up by `1/sqrt(H_jj)`. (7c) With the end-to-end test bounding the hedge, five presets do **not** improve on the unhedged product under the test's *monthly* rebalancing (Black–Scholes 20%, 8·10³ paths; hedged / unhedged P&L std): knock-out option 1.48 without costs and 2.83 with the test's half-spreads of 1 bp spot / 0.2 vp options (the barrier call spread's gamma-targeted quantity swings −16…+33 and its churn is what costs), variance swap 2.06 (the static log-contract strip's legs carry 2–7× the product's std each: the discrete delta-hedging error of a coarse strip rebalanced monthly), knock-out variance swap 1.68 without costs and 8.47 with them, conditional variance swap 1.50 (same mechanism, spot leg 8–17× the product), VKO put 2.12 (the var-swap quantity on the tiny vega exposure near the vol barrier reaches 445). Recorded, bounded at 1.5× the measured ratio by the test, not tuned. Weekly rebalancing does not change the picture (knock-out option 1.44, variance swap 2.00, knock-out variance swap 1.55, conditional variance swap 1.20, VKO put 2.71 — the VKO's var-swap quantity on the tiny vega exposure is the one genuinely noise-driven leg): the driver is not the frequency but the *scale* — under Black–Scholes pricing = world a variance product's own P&L is tiny (realised-minus-implied variance noise, std 0.0035 per unit variance notional over the year) while the regression noise of the netted book's delta (7 static strip legs of ±0.002 spot-delta each) is of the same order, so the delta leg alone is 8–17× the product; on the study surfaces with vol dynamics the products' own P&L is orders of magnitude larger and the same presets help (table B). The studies rebalance daily below 1y; preset tuning (turnover / ridge on the discontinuous payoffs, vega-only targets for barrier products) is an M8b/M9 item, and every study table should be read next to the unhedged product's std. (8) Presets whose targets need surface bumps run under a bare model with those targets dropped and noted.

**Measured (the owner's test list, `tests/test_hedging.py`, 24 tests; every P&L figure with its standard error; no wall-clock assertion; the leverage sets read from the cache).**

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Black–Scholes delta-hedged 1y ATM call, world = pricing, 2·10⁴ paths | weekly: P&L std 0.972 vs the exact-delta hedge on the same paths 0.951 (**1.02×**); daily: 0.496 vs 0.440 (**1.13×**); daily / weekly std ratio 0.51 (1/√5 = 0.45 expected). The hedged mean is the pricing error of `V₀` — +0.171 ± 0.007 weekly, +0.146 ± 0.004 daily against analytic − `V₀` = 8.349 − 8.175 / 8.196 (the regression-hedged book measures its own `V₀` error) |
| Conditional pricer, 1y call at 6M vs Black–Scholes along the paths | delta RMSE 0.008 (delta units), value RMSE 0.25 (0.25% of spot); `t = 0` value = the Monte Carlo price on the same draws to round-off |
| Hybrid-CRN delta of a started forward-start call (3M → 6M, one month in) | 0.00436 vs closed form 0.00437 per path: relative RMSE 6.5%, correlation 0.993; the plain CRN bump gives 7·10⁻¹⁹ (the payoff is homogeneous in the path) |
| Zero-cost `t = 0` valuations | equal the Monte Carlo prices on the same draws to round-off; the cached 2F cliquet `V₀` = 1.7315 ± 0.0216 % notional against the M4 headline 1.740 |
| Layer A, spanned case (2 × call(100) − put(90), same maturity; targets delta, vega, vanna; instruments the same two vanillas + spot) | residual exposure 10⁻¹⁴ of the unhedged at every date, quantities −2.000 / +1.000 on every path from the first rebalance on (at `t = 0` the fitted vega is constant, its gradient zero and the split unidentified — min-norm), hedged P&L std 0.40 vs unhedged 28.9 (gamma is not a valid third target here: under Black–Scholes gamma and vega of same-maturity options are exactly proportional and the per-path solve singular by construction) |
| Presets end to end (12 product classes, world = pricing) | finite P&L distributions with residual-exposure reports; hedge-state features as registered |
| Early termination (autocall, knock-out) | no positions after the termination date; the product leg holds the settled value; `Σ_k (V_{k+1} − V_k) = payoff − V₀` on every path to 10⁻⁹ |
| Recalibration P&L without a skew move (Black–Scholes world, cached 2F pricing set) | the world's conditional skew moves by 0.002–0.008 vp-equivalents (its own regression noise), below the rule's tolerance: no refit, recalibration P&L exactly 0 |
| Costs | costed and zero-cost totals reported side by side; costs ≥ 0, equal to the half-spread × traded notional |

**Study strategies on the placeholder surface (local-vol pricing model = world, monthly rebalancing, 6·10³ pricing and world paths — the test budget; the study archive is absent, so these rankings are the recorded baseline, P&L std in units of notional; re-measured 2026-09-15 after the LV model-delta convention, the control-variate default, the hybrid model bumps and the strategy ridge floor):**

| Product | Strategy | P&L std | robust std (99% of the paths) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cliquet 1y (12 × monthly, cap 2%, global floor 0) | delta only | 0.0139 | 0.0089 |
| | delta + static replication (cap-call strip + accumulated-sum put, `q = 1`) | 0.0595 | 0.0220 |
| | … + variance swap on vega (the net vega is zero: its quantity is noise) | 0.0376 | 0.0113 |
| | delta + half-weighted cap-call strip (`q = 0.5`) | 0.1491 | 0.0772 |
| | … + variance swap sized on the **net** vega (the study's "q-weighted cap calls + net-sized var swap") | **0.0628** | **0.0358** |
| Cliquet 1y under Black–Scholes (pricing = world) | delta only / static replication / + var swap | 0.0094 / **0.0008** / 0.0015 | — |
| FVA 1y → 2y | delta only | 0.1994 | |
| | forward-start preset (forward variance swap on `[1, 2]` + the `T1`-struck vanilla family; targets delta, `fwd_var:1-2`, vega) | **0.0475** | |
| | … + forward risk reversal on `skew_T:2` (recorded: the skew-target quantity is noise-dominated at this budget) | 0.1492 | |

**Finding (recorded by the test, asserted only under Black–Scholes).** With the LV model delta (`σ_loc` held in spot) the static replication of the cliquet does **not** beat delta only on the placeholder surface: the residual `Σ rᵢ` has the exact model-free delta `1/S_prev`, so the whole excess is the CRN tangent-process noise of the LV model delta — the pathwise difference under a spot-held local vol carries the wing's local-vol slope (several units per year on the surface's −70…−90% crash paths, which sit beyond the fitted range): total std 0.059 vs 0.014 at 6·10³ pricing paths and 0.043 vs 0.013 at 2·10⁴, robust std 0.022 vs 0.009 and 0.0092 vs 0.0087. Under the earlier `k`-held bump the replication "won" (0.008 vs 0.014) because that bump's pathwise difference is exact for returns payoffs — while measuring the wrong delta (the sticky-local-vol move, 0.69 against the model's 0.31 at `t = 0` for the 1y ATM call). Under Black–Scholes the replication is exact and the delta hedge of `Σ rᵢ` exact at every rebalance (0.0094 → 0.0008). The variance swap sized on the net vega halves the P&L std of the half-weighted strip (0.149 → 0.063), and the FVA preset cuts delta only by 4× (0.199 → 0.047) with the T1-struck family; the skew leg stays opt-in (0.149). The engine's default budget (2·10⁴ pricing paths, daily ≤ 1y / weekly beyond) is not the test budget.

**Open questions for the owner (M8b).** (a) The variance-swap sizing on a noisy net vega: the study's "net-sized var swap" is meaningful for `q < 1`; at `q = 1` (exact replication) the swap can only add noise — which `q` the study used decides the regression target. *Decision (M8 acceptance): `q` is a strategy parameter — `default_strategy(cliquet, ctx, q=…)`, the strip named `cap-call strip q=<q>` when `q ≠ 1`, the preset keyword arguments recorded in `HedgeResult.settings["preset_kwargs"]`.* (b) Skew-target hedging (the forward risk reversal, the autocall's call spreads) needs a lower-noise sensitivity estimator or a larger budget than 6·10³ paths — the tent-bump CRN target's pathwise noise is the limit, not the basis; M8b should size it (2·10⁴–10⁵ paths, or antithetic pair-averaging of the bump targets). (c) The recalibration rule's skew proxy is a three-strike regression under the world model; its noise (0.002–0.008) sets the smallest tolerance that does not trigger spurious refits. (d) The per-run memory of the kept bumped path sets (1–1.5 GB at the default budget, more for weekly 3y) — acceptable on the desk's machines or to be traded for recomputation. (e) The `skew_T:2` tent failed the placeholder surface's calendar check at +1 vp and was silently dropped as a target by the hedger before A3b (now halved and noted): the FVA "forward-start preset + skew" line in the study table should be re-run with the halved tent and the control on.


### 8.2 M8b hedging studies (2026-09-15; `volsto/studies/m8b.py`, `scripts/m8b.py`; the owner's "M8b — hedging studies (studies/m8b.py)" — library code lives under `volsto/studies/` by the repository layout, the CLI under `scripts/`, the tests in `tests/test_m8b.py`)

**Reference and pricing model.** Everything runs on the SPX 2022-12-30 snapshot marked at `ssr_target = 1`, `skew_eps = 0.10` (`configs/studies/m7_p1_marking/spx_ssr1_eps0.1.yaml`, the M7 P1 marking fit; not the placeholder SSVI); the pricing model is that fit's LSV with the leverage at `n_particles = 800 000` (the production convention, §11) unless a study says otherwise. **Book:** the M6 headline `autocall 3y` and `phoenix 3y` struck on the SPX spot, the M4 `cliquet 1y` (`AdditiveCliquet.study`), the `vko put 12m` (`VolKnockOutPut(spot, 1, 0.30, daily, notional 1/spot)`) and the `ko var 1y` (`KnockOutVarianceSwap(daily, 1.1 spot, strike 0)`, M4's convention). **Units:** autocall / Phoenix / cliquet / VKO in % of notional, the 1y vanilla of study D in % of spot, the FVA in vol points × notional, the KO var — whose P&L is in variance units — in vol points of vega notional (`variance P&L / 2 K_vol`, `K_vol` the pricing model's fair strike at `t = 0` from `fair_strike` at the study budget, recorded with its stderr in every KO-var result). **Sign:** the hedger prices the product long; every "desk" number is its negative (`to_desk_pnl`; the desk is SHORT the note, the convention of §15 Part 3), the tables say which sign they carry. Every Monte Carlo number carries its standard error; every task records its wall clock, the calibrations it performed and the cache hits.

**Tasks** (`enumerate_tasks`, one `Task` per hedger run with a stable key `<study>__<product>__<world>__<strategy>[__pricing_LV][__rota+k][__recal_<policy>][__regime_<r>]`): **A** (20) — cliquet 1y: `delta only`, `delta + cap calls q` for `q ∈ {0.5, 0.75, 1.0}` without and with the net-sized variance swap (decision (a)); FVA 1y → 2y: `delta only`, the forward-start preset, the preset `+ skew` (opt-in, decision (b)); pricing = world ∈ {`LV` (Dupire of the SPX surface), `2F` (the marking LSV)}; monthly rebalancing as the original studies. **B** (20, of which 5 gated) — pricing = the marking fit, world ∈ {(i) `same`, (ii) `historical` (`configs/studies/m8b/world_historical.yaml`, the FINAL historical fit; **only if** the discriminator's verdict reads `real` — since M10 Part 0 the run on the repaired eSSVI history, `outputs/essvi_gate/discriminator/discriminator_verdict.json`, with the pre-repair `outputs/m8b/discriminator_verdict.json` as the documented fallback (the same verdict on 2026-09-16) — today it reads `surface artefact` (§15 Part 2), so the five (ii) rows are carried as "surface artefact, skipped" with the discriminator's reason), (iii) `pure LV`, (iv) `nu x1.5` (the marking fit with ν × 1.5, its leverage through the cache)} × the five products, the per-product presets. **C** (45) — the five products × rota ∈ {+1, +2, +3} × recalibration ∈ {`none`, `on_skew_move sabr_linked`, `on_skew_move sticky_breakeven`}; world = `skew_shock_world(state, cache, rota, t0 = T/2, days = 5)` (§8.1, `ξ₀`-rescaled leverage); a refit's leverage at `refit_particles` (800 000; `RefitParticlesBuilder` rewrites only parameter-changed states when the two counts differ). **D** (8) — the 1y ATM vanilla (strike = spot) and the 3y autocall, world = pricing (2F), `delta only` under `model / sticky_strike / sticky_skew / sticky_moneyness`. **Frequency rule:** daily ≤ 1y, weekly beyond (the §8.1 default budget), study A monthly, `--frequency` overrides; the simulation step is the rebalancing step capped at weekly (`SIM_DT_MAX`), the products' own fixings refine the grid. Budget `n_paths = 20 000` pricing and world paths (`--world-paths`), seed 2024 (the world seed is the hedger's pricing seed + 1).
