**Study B numbers.** Per (world, product): the desk leakage per path (mean ± se; `leakage_desk_incl_v0` repeats it with the pricing error of the run's own `V₀` folded into the se), std (± its fourth-moment se), the 5 / 95% quantiles of the desk P&L, the regime breakdown and attribution (from `regime_table` / `attribution_table`, desk sign), the **static spread** = the marked price minus the world's price at `t = 0` (the hedger's `V₀` under the pricing model and a direct Monte Carlo price of the product under the world model on the world's own budget and seed; independent draws, stderrs in quadrature) next to the **dynamic leakage** = leakage − static spread, and `leakage_vs_same` = the leakage minus the product's leakage under world (i): measured on the plumbing smoke (LV pricing = LV world, 2·10³ paths, monthly) the world = pricing runs carry a non-zero hedged mean — the cliquet −0.30 ± 0.02 % of notional, the KO var +1.43 ± 0.10 vol points — the engine's own baseline (the pricing error of `V₀` plus the regression drift of the hedge legs: an instrument's regressed value function is not exactly a martingale under the world, only the true conditional expectation is; the product leg telescopes exactly), so the **model reserve of a world is the difference to the `same` row**, not the raw leakage. The leakage under (ii) is the reserve the SSR = 1 mark implicitly carries.

**Study C numbers.** The recalibration P&L in the desk convention, isolated per refit date and summed: `RecordingHedger` (a `Hedger` whose pricers record the product's value per date, so `Σ_refits [V_k(new) − V_k(old)]` — accumulated by the M8 loop as one per-path total — is split per refit date afterwards and checked against the hedger's total, a mismatch reported), against the static prediction `desk_pnl_shadow × rota` of `rotation_shadow_sensitivity` under the same policy (the M7 greek on its own states: 2·10⁵ particles, 2·10⁵ paths, seed 2024, cached per product and policy under `outputs/m8b/C/static_*.json`; the greek's base fit is checked equal to the marking fit); `first_order_agreement` = (ratio, `|ratio − 1| ≤ 0.30`, z) at every rota, asserted at +1 by the slow test; the nonlinearity `P&L(rota)/(rota × P&L(+1)) − 1` at +2 / +3; the `none` rows carry the total hedged desk P&L against `desk_pnl_usual × rota` (a reference, not asserted). **Trigger threshold for study C.** The footprint of a +1 rota on the world's 3M *forward* skew at mid-life is ≈ 0.007 per unit log-moneyness (measured on the 3y autocall's shock world with a stub refit: the excess over the pricing model's prediction peaks at 0.008 around `t0 = 1.5` and settles at 0.004–0.007 — the `1/√T` rotation's local-vol footprint at 1.5y is about half its implied-skew move there), below the hedger's general default `skew_move_threshold` 0.01 (owner decision (c)); study C therefore runs its rule at `STUDY_C_SKEW_MOVE_THRESHOLD` = 0.005 (documented constant): the +1 rota refit fires when the 3M strip first spans the shock window — one pillar *before* `t0`, the deterministic shock world anticipating its own shock (the 1Y pillar's anticipation stays under the threshold at 2·10⁴ paths) —, +2 / +3 rota clear both thresholds; the proxy noise is 0.001–0.003 at 2·10⁴ paths (0.002–0.005 at 4·10³). **Two findings on the M8 recalibration rule, made while plumbing study C — both ported into `hedging/hedger.py` in this commit (§8.1 Part 1: forward-start strips at forward moneyness inverted path by path, and the excess-skew trigger against a CRN twin under the pricing model); `RecordingHedger` now only records the per-date values:** (1) `Hedger._state_surface` averages the strip's conditional option prices over the paths *before* inverting them, which is the unconditional price of the `(t + τ)`-option: its "implied vol over τ" carries the spot variance over `[0, t]` and grows like `sqrt((t + τ)/τ)` (measured on the skew-shock world: the 3M ATMF vol read 0.218 at `t = 0` and 0.394 at `t = 0.5`, the 1Y one 0.221 → 0.278, at 2·10³ and 6·10³ paths alike), the skew proxy "moves" at the first date after 0 in any world, the rule refits at every date and the fitter eventually rejects the ATMF term structure (`the ATMF total variance is not increasing in T`); the M8 test could not see it (a Black–Scholes world has zero skew at every date). The correction inverts each path's conditional prices at the path's own forward and reads the smile at the strip's strikes (mid-strike vol, central-difference slope, second difference), averaged over the paths that invert. (2) Even so the rule's reference — the world's own skew at `t = 0` — refits at every one of 11 monthly dates with no shock at all: an LSV world's *forward* skew at date `t` differs from its spot skew by far more than the 0.01 threshold. Study C therefore triggers on the **excess skew**: the world's conditional skew minus the pricing model's own prediction of it at the same date, the same strip priced under the original pricing model on the same grid, paths and seed (a CRN twin: world = pricing before the shock gives exactly 0, the shock's rota is the excess after `t0`, and the reference resetting at the refit the held rotation triggers nothing further; measured on the cached 2·10⁵ shock world at +1 rota with a stub refit: excess 0.001–0.009 while no strip option reaches into the shock window, 0.018 at `t = 0.417` — the 3M option then spans `[t0, t_end]` and the world's conditional smile anticipates its deterministic shock, so the refit fires one pillar *before* `t0` — then 0.001–0.008 against the reset reference: one refit in twelve monthly dates); the refit itself still reads the world's state surface as the M8 rule does. Both belong in `hedging/hedger.py` once the owner accepts them (the M8 recalibration test would then need a skewed world).

**Budget and sharding.** `scripts/m8b.py --dry-run` prints per study the task count and per task the projected hedger wall clock (`Hedger.projected_wall_clock` summed over the tasks) and the leverage calibrations the tasks need up front (`required_states` against the cache — the parallel ±1 vp states of `vega` / `volga` / `vanna`, the autocall preset's `skew_T` tent, the regime states of D, the worlds of B, the rotated leverages of C; the refits inside C are one calibration each unless cached and cannot be counted ahead) at the manifest's median wall time per calibration (159 s at 8·10⁵). Measured 2026-09-15: A 20 tasks / 94 s projected (LV pricing needs no leverage; the 2F lines need the parallel ±1 vp states and the `skew_T:2` tent — 3 calibrations, ≈ 480 s), B 15 tasks / 891 s + the same parallel states and the `skew_T:3` tent (ν × 1.5 and the +1/+2/+3 rota leverages are already cached at 8·10⁵), C 45 tasks / 2 672 s + the same 3 states + the refits, D 8 tasks / 108 s and no calibration (the six sticky-regime states are cached at 8·10⁵); ≈ 1.4 h of hedger time plus calibrations in total. `--shard i/n` takes every n-th task of the ordered list from i (interleaved), `--resume` skips existing results, the tables are rebuilt from every result under `--out` (shards merge): `outputs/m8b/<study>/<key>.json` (`TaskResult`: the summary numbers, regimes, attribution, recalibration P&L per date, settings, budget, calibrations, wall clock) + `.pkl` (the `HedgeResult` without the world paths and the per-date Greek arrays), `outputs/m8b/m8b_table_<study>.csv` and `outputs/m8b/m8b.md` (the wall clock and whether anything was recalibrated in its header). The script calibrates and says so; the tests never do (`allow_calibrate=False`, skip on `CacheMissError`). The projected numbers are a **timing probe**, not a measurement, and the M8 probe (one 1000-path chunk of the model plus a constant per date and object) proved 3–8× optimistic against the study-B runs (autocall 44 s projected / 195 s measured, Phoenix 174 / 1570): it counted the simulation only, not the per-date payoffs, regressions and hybrid bump targets. **Rebuilt** (`Hedger.PROBE_PATHS = 2000`): the probe simulates at 2·10³ and 4·10³ paths and evaluates every object of the strategy at two consecutive mid-life dates with the strategy's bump targets, which separates the one-off cost of a pricer (payoffs on every set, the control-variate proxies and shadow draws) from the marginal cost of a date, and the per-date cost into a fixed part and a part linear in the paths; the study runner passes the bumps when every state they need is cached (a dry run never calibrates, and says so when it probes a task without them) and `required_states` looks up the **halved** tent the run will actually ask for (`MAX_HALVINGS`) instead of reporting a phantom calibration. Measured on study B at the production budget: projected 300 / 454 / 113 / 201 / 153 s against measured 195 / 323 / 92 / 175 / 110 s (autocall / Phoenix / cliquet / VKO / KO var) — 1.2–1.6× conservative, the machine being loaded during the runs. The production run's measured wall clocks per task are the ones in the study tables.

**Cache state at the build (2026-09-15).** Marking fit: base cached at 2·10⁵ and 8·10⁵; rotations +1 / −1 at 2·10⁵, +1 / +2 / +3 at 8·10⁵; ν × 1.5 at 8·10⁵; the sticky-regime states at 8·10⁵; **no parallel ±1 vp state at either count**, so every preset with a `vega` target on the LSV pricing model (all five book products, the cliquet's variance swap) needs those two calibrations at the study's particle count before it runs — the reason the B / C / D smoke tasks and the slow B / C / D tests skip today, and the slow A test's 2F variant skips at its variance-swap line (its LV variant runs and pins the order).

**Tests** (`tests/test_m8b.py`; fast ones need no cache): task counts (20 / 20 / 45 / 8) and unique keys, the frequency rule, sharding coverage (every task in exactly one shard for n ∈ {1, 2, 3, 7, 10}), the discriminator gate (a temporary `surface artefact` verdict removes world (ii) with the reason in the table; `real` keeps it; a missing file disables it), `first_order_agreement` / `nonlinearity`, the desk-sign conversion through `table_C`, the table builders on synthetic results (ranking, static / dynamic / vs-same leakage, ratio and nonlinearity, winner and closest-to-model, the markdown with the `M8B_TABLE_*` placeholders), the JSON round trip, `refit_state`, `n_pricing_models`. Slow (skip on a cache miss): study A's cliquet ranking pinned per pricing model at 6·10³ paths monthly (the recorded numbers are in the test's docstring), study B (`pure LV`, `cliquet 1y`) end to end at 3·10³ paths, study C's +1 rota agreement for the autocall under `sabr_linked` at 4·10³ paths, study D's vanilla ranking at 4·10³ paths, and the excess-skew trigger on the cached shock world with a stub refit (no refits before `t0`, the excess after it).  The defects the production runs exposed are pinned in `tests/test_hedging.py`: the Phoenix memory feature through `Autocall.coupon_amounts` and the two schedules of a 3y Phoenix with daily knock-in fixings (757 rebalancing dates with the product's fixings, 156 on the frequency grid) in the hedge-state test; `test_refit_rebuilds_every_target_column` (a stubbed trigger and a stubbed refit fire one refit mid-life and every target column — `vanna` included — survives the rebuild: it reproduces the study-C `KeyError: 'vanna'` when the call is removed); and `test_degenerate_correlations_flags_a_collapsed_refit` on the bound detector. The rebuilt refit (2026-09-16): lean-strip bit-identity, strip-count wiring and read-before-loop (`tests/test_hedging.py`); the guarded fallback on a synthetic degenerate target set; the cap; the named pinning case (autocall 3y, +1 rota, t = 0.9615) reproduced bit for bit at 2·10⁴ strip paths and cleared at 8·10⁴ (slow, cached); `refits_fallback` / `refits_capped` and their backfill; the projection counting the strips at 8·10⁴; `sticky_breakeven`'s effective held set (`tests/test_shadow_rotation.py`).

**Engine defects the production runs exposed (all fixed at the root, each with a test).** The
smoke tasks of the build ran at 2·10³ paths on one product; the production runs at 2·10⁴ over the
whole book found five:

1. **Memory — a replaced pricer stayed resident.** A `ConditionalPricer` of the autocall preset
   holds six path sets at 2·10⁴ paths (5.2 GB, `budget["pricing_paths_gb"]`); every refit built a
   new one and the old one stayed referenced by the hedger's `pricers` list (kept for the
   control-variate reductions), so study C grew by a full pricer per refit and reached 26 GB on a
   24 GB laptop. `ConditionalPricer.release()` now drops the base and bumped path sets, the
   hybrid cache and the shadow draws of the **replaced** pricer as soon as its refit P&L is
   booked, keeping what the end of a run reads (the fits, the payoffs, and the footprint it had
   through `memory_bytes` / `streamed_bytes`); the dead per-`(object, date)` `states` cache (about
   1 MB an entry, written and never read) is gone. Recalibration tasks still need 9–13 GB — the
   two forward-start strip pricers of the rule are 1404 objects — so the studies run one process
   at a time. `--stream-bumps` (the mmap'd bumped sets) is the memory answer but costs 5.7× the
   wall clock (autocall rota +1: 1120 s streamed against 195 s resident), so it stays off.
2. **The rebalancing schedule followed the product's fixings.** `Schedule` added every product
   fixing as a rebalancing date, so the Phoenix — 757 daily knock-in fixings — was hedged *daily*
   under a "weekly" schedule: 900 dates and 1570 s a run against 156 dates and 323 s once fixed.
   `Schedule(product_fixings=False)` keeps the frequency grid (the state at a date still includes
   every fixing up to it) and the studies use it (`STUDY_PRODUCT_FIXINGS`); the three study-B
   Phoenix rows were re-run on it.
3. **The Phoenix hedge state crashed** (`KeyError: 'coupons'`): `hedge_state` read the per-date
   coupon amounts from `Autocall.statistics`, which exposes `coupons_paid` (a total) and not the
   `(n_paths, N)` array the memory feature needs. New `Autocall.coupon_amounts`.
4. **A refit dropped a target column.** The loop computes the product's and the instruments'
   Greeks, adds the regression-native `vanna` column and only then runs the recalibration rule; a
   refit rebuilt the two dictionaries under the new pricer *without* it, so the six study-C
   `vko put 12m` recalibration runs (whose preset targets vanna) died with `KeyError: 'vanna'` at
   their first refit date. The rebuild now goes through the same `instrument_greeks` / `add_vanna`
   closures as any other date (`tests/test_hedging.py::test_refit_rebuilds_every_target_column`
   pins it: it reproduces the KeyError when the call is removed).
5. **Stderr twins in the tables.** Table B wrote `leakage_desk` next to `leakage_se` and no error
   at all on the desk quantiles or the dynamic leakage, table C no error on the ratio or the
   nonlinearity. Every Monte Carlo column of both now carries its own `<x>_se` (the desk
   quantiles inherit the distribution table's quantile se, the dynamic leakage adds the static
   spread's in quadrature, the ratios use the delta method of `ratio_stderr` — conservative for
   the nonlinearity, whose two runs share the world seed).

**As run (2026-09-15 / 16, this laptop; `outputs/m8b/m8b.md` carries the header of every
invocation).** Everything at 2·10⁴ pricing and world paths on the SPX 2022-12-30 marking fit at
8·10⁵ particles, the frequency rule of the module (A monthly, daily to 1y, weekly beyond), the
§7.11 control variate on, bumps resident (not streamed).

| study | tasks | hedger wall | leverage calibrations | note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 20 | 0.04 h | 0 | one invocation, 151 s; LV and 2F, the bump states cached |
| B | 15 (+5 gated) | 0.80 h | 0 | the five (ii) `historical` rows carried as "surface artefact, skipped" |
| C | 45 | 10.89 h | 88 | the refits are the cost: 88 calibrations at 8·10⁵ inside the runs |
| D | 8 | 0.06 h | 0 | the six sticky-regime states cached |

The static greeks of study C (the M7 rotation sensitivity per product and policy, 2·10⁵ paths and
particles, cached under `outputs/m8b/C/static_*.json`) cost 16.9 min for the ten (five products x two policies) and **reproduce the M7 Part 3
measurement**: the 3y autocall's `desk_pnl_shadow` is −0.0539 ± 0.0066 % of notional per rota
under `sabr_linked` and −0.0737 ± 0.0072 under `sticky_breakeven`, against the −0.054 / −0.074 of
§15 Part 3 (the same states and the same computation — the M7 CSV and the static files agree to 1e-14 — so this is a reproduction of the recorded number, not an independent measurement).

Study C is the only study whose budget is dominated by its refits. Until 2026-09-16 the trigger's
two strip pricers (about 1400 objects weekly-3y, 2268 daily-1y), priced at the world's 2·10⁴ paths,
were most of the 9 to 13 GB a recalibration run held. The rebuilt rule (§8.1) reads the strips at
`STUDY_C_STRIP_PATHS` = 8·10⁴ with lean pricers released before the hedge loop. Measured on the
worst case (vko put 12m, `sabr_linked`, +3 rota, 2·10⁴ pricing and world paths): peak RSS 4.57 GB;
wall clock 2293 s, of which 9 refit calibrations at 8·10⁵ particles took 1715 s and the two strips
288 s. The dry-run projection counts the strips at 8·10⁴ paths — 5905 s over the 30 recalibration
rows, a study-C hedger total of 4.78 h — but it excludes the refit calibrations, which dominate: the
2026-09-15 runs carried 226 refits, a rebuilt rule reads different surfaces so almost none is
cached, and at ~190 s per 8·10⁵-particle calibration they add about 12 h. A serial re-run of the
thirty rows is therefore about 15–17 h. **Measured on the re-run (2026-09-16, 12:47–19:20): 23 443 s (6.51 h) for the thirty rows**, because the rebuilt rule fires fewer refits (83 per policy against 113) and the rows that share a world reuse each other's refit leverages (the Phoenix rows those of the autocall, the VKO and KO-var rows those of the cliquet: 0 calibrations of their own). Table C carries `refits_fallback` and `refits_capped` per
row (−1 when not recorded) next to `refits_at_bound` / `contaminated`. The studies still run one
process at a time.

**Study C — the re-run under the rebuilt rule (2026-09-16).** The thirty recalibration rows were re-run under the rule of §8.1 (strips at 8·10⁴ paths struck at forward moneyness, the guarded fallback, the 0.97 correlation cap; curvature stencil = h, the owner's choice pending); the fifteen `none` rows are unchanged (identical numbers). The previous rows are kept in `outputs/m8b/C_pre_2026-09-16/`.

**Contamination is gone, and the fallback carries most refits.** No refit of either policy lands with a correlation at its bound (0 of 83 per policy, against 41 of 113 `sabr_linked` refits before). Both policies refit on the same dates (they share the trigger), 83 refits each; on **63 of the 83** the step-0 reduction of the date's state surface is degenerate and the refit holds the base fit's correlation target (`refits_fallback`), and 6 `sabr_linked` refits had their |Corr_BE| target capped at 0.97 (`refits_capped`; 0 under `sticky_breakeven`). So both columns enter the conclusion, with one reading attached: on three refits in four the `sabr_linked` policy differs from `sticky_breakeven` only through its break-even vol targets, not through its correlation. The refits now fire 2 / 5–6 / 9 times at +1 / +2 / +3 rota for every product (the 3y notes at t = 0.923 and 1.385 at +1).

**First-order agreement (ratio = simulated / static × rota, classed at 2 se against 1 ± 0.30 — above: ratio − 2 se > 1.3; below: ratio + 2 se < 0.7; within: both bounds inside [0.7, 1.3]; else undecided).**

| policy | +1 rota | +2 rota | +3 rota |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sabr_linked` | 4 above (autocall 2.44 ± 0.30, Phoenix 2.14 ± 0.40, VKO 2.46 ± 0.44, KO var 2.35 ± 0.09), 1 within (cliquet 1.267 ± 0.012) | 2 above, 1 within, 2 undecided | 1 above, 1 undecided, 3 below |
| `sticky_breakeven` | 3 above (autocall 1.82 ± 0.19, cliquet 2.296 ± 0.017, VKO 2.45 ± 0.30), 2 undecided (Phoenix 1.04 ± 0.39, KO var 1.32 ± 0.04) | 1 above, 1 within, 3 undecided | 2 within, 2 undecided, 1 below |

**What the study concludes.** At +1 rota the simulated re-marking P&L is, on every product where the comparison is decided, at least as large in magnitude as the static greek × rota, with the same sign (desk losses on the autocall, Phoenix and cliquet, gains on the KO var under both policies and on the VKO under `sticky_breakeven`; the VKO under `sabr_linked` is a small loss, −0.048 ± 0.003, like its static greek); the agreement within 30 % holds on one of the eight pairs decided at +1 (the `sabr_linked` cliquet; the `sticky_breakeven` Phoenix and KO var are undecided). The static greek is a one-step, t = 0 object and the rule re-marks twice at +1 — this is the first reason the simulated P&L is larger. The linear extrapolation `static × rota` does not describe the larger shocks: at +3 four pairs are below (the `sabr_linked` re-marking P&L of the autocall and the VKO changes sign — +0.062 ± 0.014 and +0.135 ± 0.012 against static losses — and so does the Phoenix's, not significantly, +0.033 ± 0.024; the fourth is the `sticky_breakeven` Phoenix), and the nonlinearity column (between −1.94 and +0.12) measures the firing count and the path of the refits more than any curvature of the fee.

**What it does not conclude.** (1) The policy ordering is not the static greek's where both are decided. At 2 se the static greek makes `sticky_breakeven` the larger re-marking only on the cliquet (sticky / sabr 1.282 ± 0.013) and the KO var (1.28 ± 0.07); on the autocall (1.4 ± 0.3) and the Phoenix (1.12 ± 0.22) its side is undecided (ratios of runs that share random numbers carry `m8b.ratio_stderr_bound`, valid for any correlation). Simulated, `sticky_breakeven` is larger on the cliquet at +1 and +2 (2.32, 1.77; equal at +3, 1.00) and **smaller** on the KO var at every rota (0.72, 0.59, 0.77) — the reversal — and with the VKO at +3 the two sides disagree on 4 of the 8 product-rota pairs where both are decided (S6, §10.2). On the Phoenix the simulated ratio is smaller at +1 and +2 (0.54, 0.63) and on the autocall it is undecided at +1 and +2 (1.02, 1.02) and of opposite sign at +3, but neither contradicts a decided static side. With the fallback holding the correlation on 63 of 83 refits, this ordering is between two policies that differ mostly in their vol targets; it is recorded, not explained. (2) The VKO's two policies have opposite signs, as their static greeks do (sabr −0.0195, sticky +0.0283 per rota). (3) One reading, one surface, one threshold: the ratios depend on the trigger (`STUDY_C_SKEW_MOVE_THRESHOLD`) through the firing count.

**Before the re-run** (for the record): on the 2026-09-15 rows the `sabr_linked` column was contaminated (41/113 refits at bound: at 2·10⁴ strip paths the state surface's curvature was unconverged, step 0's radicand guard clipped `Corr_SABR` to −1 and the collapsed set was step 3's exact minimiser), and the `sticky_breakeven` column read 1.19 (Phoenix), 1.20 (KO var), 2.39 (cliquet), 2.56 (autocall), 3.38 (VKO) at +1. Those rows also used the pre-fix strips struck at spot rather than forward moneyness (§8.1). The earlier wording "the M7 greek is a lower bound on the realised recalibration cost" is withdrawn: it rested on point estimates, called gains costs, and ignored +2/+3; the S6 catalogue study (§10.2) now draws this claim from the table with the 2-se classes above.

**Study D — reinstated with the minimum-variance reading (owner's decision, 2026-09-16).** Headline: **the model delta is NOT the minimum-variance spot-only hedge under its own model — incomplete market, the MV delta carries the vol-correlation term — and for these products sticky-strike and sticky-skew sit closest to it.** The withdrawal of the ranking rested on a premise the diagnosis refuted: with world = pricing the model delta is the minimum-variance delta only in a complete market. Under Black–Scholes the four regimes are bit-identical (the estimator adds exactly zero spread); under the 2F marking LSV the model / sticky-strike ratio does not shrink with pricing paths (1.61 → 1.65 from 10⁴ to 8·10⁴) and estimator noise explains 2.5% of it.

Tasks: D (10) = {1y ATM vanilla, 3y autocall} × {model, sticky_strike, sticky_skew, sticky_moneyness, `min_variance`}. Per row, `delta_diagnostics` records `mean_delta` (the relative delta averaged over dates and world paths) and the IN-SAMPLE benchmarks with path-pair bootstrap se's: `lambda_star = −Cov(product leg, hedge leg)/Var(hedge leg)`, `std_at_lambda`, `mv_delta_implied = λ* × mean_delta`. **The ranking is the distance to the COMMON minimum-variance delta** `mv_common` — the precision-weighted mean of the four regime rows' implied values (the min_variance row excluded; se the perfect-correlation bound, conservative because the rows share world paths; `mv_common_spread` reported) — `distance_to_mv` = row mean delta − `mv_common` (se in quadrature), `distance_rank` 1 = closest; `std_rank` stays visible. The min_variance row is a fifth line with `mv_valid` (its mean delta within `MV_BENCHMARK_VALIDITY_NSE` = 3 se of `mv_common`) and `mv_note` (raw gradients, no Black–Scholes proxy). It is not the reference because it is not robust: at 5·10³ pricing paths its hedged std is 2.756 ± 0.053 and 2.965 ± 0.068 on two seeds, worse than sticky-strike, and on the autocall it uses raw gradients.

Measured before the study re-run (2F LSV at 8·10⁵ particles, 2·10⁴ paths, control off, no recalibration). Vanilla, daily, % of spot: model 3.144 ± 0.011, distance +0.1131 ± 0.0012 (rank 4); sticky_strike 1.925 ± 0.008, +0.0617 (1); sticky_skew 1.972 ± 0.008, +0.0629 (2); sticky_moneyness 3.060 ± 0.010, +0.1077 (3); min_variance 1.701 ± 0.013, −0.0004 ± 0.0011 (valid). `mv_common` = 0.5362 ± 0.0009, spread 0.0035; model mean delta 0.6493. Autocall, weekly, % of notional: model 7.704 ± 0.083 (mean delta 0.243), sticky_strike 6.764 ± 0.076, sticky_skew 6.562 ± 0.073, sticky_moneyness 8.125 ± 0.087, min_variance 8.028 ± 0.079 (raw gradients, not a valid benchmark); the implied MV deltas spread 0.294–0.304 (about 3%, about four times the common se (0.0094 against 0.0022) — the autocall's benchmark is less certain than the vanilla's). **The reading is two-sided:** for the call the MV delta lies BELOW the model delta (long vega, negative spot-vol correlation), for the autocall ABOVE it (short vol); in both cases sticky-strike and sticky-skew sit closest, sticky-moneyness and the model delta furthest.

Estimator diagnostics (`scripts/m8b_delta_estimator.py` → `outputs/m8b/delta_estimator.{csv,md}`; Black–Scholes flat 20%, 1y ATM call, daily, 2·10⁴ world paths): the regimes and `min_variance` are bit-identical under Black–Scholes; the hedged std over the analytic-delta hedge is 1.133 ± 0.006 at 2·10⁴ pricing paths (1.260 ± 0.009 at 5·10³), 1.074 ± 0.005 with the delta control; pooled delta bias −0.0022, RMSE 0.0116 (0.0086 controlled), with a last-week basis floor of 0.038 that the control does not touch.

Study D — (from `outputs/m8b/m8b_table_D.csv`, re-run 2026-09-16: 10 tasks, 298 s, recalibrated: no; value ± se). Distance rank 1 = closest to the common minimum-variance delta, 0 = the min_variance benchmark row itself. autocall 3y: common MV delta 0.2993 ± 0.0022 (spread across the four regimes 0.0094); vanilla 1y atm: common MV delta 0.5362 ± 0.0009 (spread across the four regimes 0.0035). The autocall's min_variance row is not a valid benchmark (z 7.0, raw regression gradients: no Black-Scholes proxy for the controlled value target).

**Anchor switch — study A re-run (2026-09-22; `outputs/m8b/m8b_table_A.csv`, 895 s, recalibrated: yes; the plain-SSVI run kept in `outputs/m8b_pre_2026-09-22`).** On the repaired eSSVI 2022-12-30 snapshot with the 2026-09-22 market section, the 20 rows keep their ranking within each (pricing, product) block. The P&L standard deviations fall by 4 % at the median (new/old 0.959); 10 of 20 cells move by more than 2 standard errors of the difference (the two runs share no random numbers, so the errors add in quadrature), all downwards and mostly under the 2F pricing: the cliquet's delta-only hedge 2.10 → 1.84 % of notional (z −8.8), the forward-start's delta-only hedge 11.4 → 10.8 under 2F (z −5.1) and 11.1 → 9.3 under LV (z −8.5). Desk means move within noise except the forward-start rows (LV forward-start preset −1.28 → −1.17, z +3.2; preset + skew −4.35 → −4.09, z +3.0; 2F preset + skew −3.87 → −3.69, z +2.2) and the 2F cliquet with the q = 1 cap calls (0.062 → 0.073, z +3.0).

**Anchor switch — study D re-run (2026-09-22; `outputs/m8b/m8b_table_D.csv`, 1 443 s, recalibrated: yes; 2·10⁴ paths as before).** The common minimum-variance delta of the 1y ATM vanilla moves from 0.5362 ± 0.0009 to 0.5316 (z −3.6 across the two runs) and the model delta from 0.649 to 0.646; the sticky-strike and sticky-skew regimes stay the closest to it (distance 0.065 / 0.065 against 0.062 / 0.063), the ranking by distance is unchanged on the vanilla, and the autocall's sticky-strike and sticky-skew swap places (0.002 against 0.003). The P&L standard deviations of the four surface regimes move by 0 to 5 % (6 of 8 beyond 2 se, the autocall's sticky-moneyness 8.13 → 7.55 the largest). **The `min_variance` rows are another matter:** the vanilla's P&L std goes from 1.70 to 4.79 % of spot (worse than the model delta's 3.11) and the row's own consistency check now fails (`mv_valid` False, z 12.2 against −0.3 before; the autocall's row was already invalid: raw gradients, no Black–Scholes proxy). The hedger's docstring records the benchmark's fragility to the controlled regression's gradient noise (2.8–3.0 at 5·10³ paths on the old fit); on the new marking fit (ν 2.11, ρ_SX1 −0.87, ρ_SX2 −0.94) the 2·10⁴-path gradients no longer carry it. The study reports the row as invalid; the minimum-variance benchmark needs a better gradient estimator before it can be read again (open item for the owner).

**Anchor switch — study B re-run (2026-09-22; `outputs/m8b/m8b_table_B.csv`, 2 905 s, recalibrated: yes).** World (i) *same* and world (iv) *ν×1.5*: the P&L standard deviations move by −4 % at the median (new/old 0.961; the Phoenix's the most, 6.65 → 5.82 and 6.59 → 6.13, z −4.4 / −3.4), the cliquet's within noise; the desk leakages keep their signs. World (ii) *historical* stays gated (the discriminator's verdict is unchanged). **World (iii) *pure LV* exposed a defect of the hedger, now fixed (§7.11, 2026-09-23):** the conditional pricer imputed a foreign world's pricing factors as 0 and evaluated its fit off the support it was identified on; on the new marking fit (ν 2.11, ρ_SX1 −0.87, ρ_SX2 −0.94, ρ₁₂ at its admissible minimum) that put a −40 ± 0.8 % of notional error on the KO var's barrier-call-spread marks and multiplied the P&L standard deviations by three to seven (cliquet 105.9, VKO 121.6, KO var 116.8 % of notional; Phoenix 29.1). The pure-LV rows recomputed with the imputation `E[X_t | ln S_t]` on the pricer's spline (`outputs/m8b/m8b_B_purelv_2026-09-23.log`, 863 s, recalibrated: no): P&L std autocall 5.73 ± 0.06, Phoenix 21.2 ± 3.7, cliquet 0.726 ± 0.060, VKO 5.59 ± 0.22, KO var 2.46 ± 0.04 % of notional (same world: 8.44 / 5.82 / 1.11 / 4.05 / 2.85); desk leakage −0.65 ± 0.04 / −0.65 ± 0.15 / +0.27 ± 0.01 / −2.64 ± 0.04 / +1.52 ± 0.02. The Phoenix keeps a heavy tail (its standard error is 17 % of its std) and is not read further until its outlier paths are examined. **The plain-SSVI run's pure-LV column was inflated by the same defect** (the verifier measured the KO var at 60 → 5.8 % of notional at 5·10³ paths on the old fit; the run's 16.1 at 2·10⁴), so the world-(iii) figures of the study-B table above — the Phoenix's −1.513 ± 0.119 included — are withdrawn; the same-world and ν×1.5 columns were never affected (their worlds carry the pricing model's factors). The S7 diff after the fix: `outputs/studies/s7_hedging/rerun/20260923T122833Z/diff.md` (163 of 417 numbers moved against the plain-SSVI study).

**Anchor switch — study C re-run (2026-09-22/23; `outputs/m8b/m8b_table_C.csv`, 26 837 s = 7.5 h, recalibrated: yes; 45 tasks, 164 refits).** The rota is 6.47 → 7.03 vp of 6m skew per +1 (6.30 → 6.86 before) and the static predictions move little (21 of 45 cells beyond 2 se, all by less than 0.04 % of notional). The recalibration P&L moves in 23 of 30 recalibrating rows by more than 2 standard errors of the difference, with sign changes under `sabr_linked` at +2 and +3 on the autocall (−0.205 → +0.191, +0.062 → +0.305) and the Phoenix (−0.158 → +0.115, +0.033 → +0.177) and on the VKO at +3 (+0.135 → −0.053); `sticky_breakeven` moves less and keeps its signs except the Phoenix at +1. The 2-se classes against the static greek: at +1 `sabr_linked` stays above on all five products while `sticky_breakeven` is above on the cliquet and the KO var, within on the autocall and the VKO, below on the Phoenix (before: above on four, within on the Phoenix); at +2 and +3 both policies are below on the autocall and the Phoenix, above on the cliquet, and the KO var reads above / within / below across the cells (before: above on most at +2). **The comparison is now dominated by the refit rule's cap:** the break-even correlation target was capped at `REFIT_CORRELATION_CAP` = 0.97 in 144 of the 164 refits (6 of 166 on the plain-SSVI anchor), the guarded fallback fired in 108 (126 before), and 3 refits sit at a parameter bound (`contaminated`: the cliquet, VKO and KO var at +2 under `sabr_linked`; 0 before). *Mechanism (corrected 2026-09-26: the first version of this note blamed the fitted ρ_SX2).* The cap acts on the break-even correlation targets `Corr_BE` that step 0 reads off the surface, and on the new anchor the **base** marking fit's own targets are −0.833 / −0.954 / −1.000 (clipped from −1.048, step 0 degenerate) / −0.970 / −0.997 at 3m / 6m / 1y / 2y / 3y — beyond the cap at 1y, 2y and 3y before any rotation (the plain-SSVI anchor: −0.90 at every pillar). Every refit therefore caps what the base fit did not, and even a refit on an unmoved state moves the parameters: the recalibration rows carry that step on top of the policy's. The hedger now says so in the run's notes (`targets_beyond_cap`), and the marking fit notes the base fit's near-singular correlation (smallest eigenvalue 6·10⁻⁴, χ at its −0.99 bound; `correlation_min_eigenvalue`). Why step 0 reads −1 on this surface, the owner's documents on the desk's SABRW-based break-evens and the planned change of step 0's input are in §15 Part 3 (*Step 0 on (e)SSVI surfaces and the desk's SABRW*). Until that change the study-C ordering of the policies stands on the plain-SSVI run of 2026-09-16 (table above).

**S6 / S7 on the new anchor — what moved (`volsto-study rerun`, 2026-09-23).** The stored studies (commit 0394a98, plain-SSVI anchor) re-executed against the re-run artefacts: S6 `outputs/studies/s6_shadow_rotation/rerun/20260923T113624Z/diff.md` — 432 of 927 numbers moved beyond 2 stderr (the M7 greek's LV rotation −0.0917 → −0.1059, the cliquet's P1 level 1.547 → 1.708 % of notional, table C's 3 contaminated rows, the static and recalibrated shadows of every product); S7 `outputs/studies/s7_hedging/rerun/<utc>/diff.md` — 160 of 417 before the pure-LV fix (table A's standard deviations and two `rank_decided` flags, table B's same-world reserves: autocall q05 −11.47 → −10.22, Phoenix std 6.65 → 5.82), re-diffed after study B's pure-LV rows were recomputed with the corrected pricer. The rerun's rule takes the two runs as the same computation (errors not added in quadrature), which overstates the count where the anchor changed the calibration; the diff files carry every pair.

## 9. Viewers

`viewers/precompute.py` builds grids into the cache: default grid `ω ∈ {0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 2.5, 3}`, `ρ1 ∈ {−0.9, −0.7, −0.5, −0.3, 0}`, `k1 ∈ {0.5, 1.5, 4}`, 2F presets (a few `(θ, k1, k2, ρ12)` combinations including the SSR ≈ 1.2 fit), always including the 1F degenerate points so the old studies are recoverable. Precompute is a CLI with resume support. Owner additions (recorded at the M4c review, for M9): the precompute CLI takes an explicit list of grid points and a worker count, so a grid can be sharded across cores or machines and resumed — `volsto-precompute --shard i/n` runs the i-th of n interleaved shards of the point list; the cache is relocatable (relative paths only, manifest-driven), so grids can be computed on a rented multi-core VM and synced to a laptop; production entries use 8·10⁵ particles (§11).

Streamlit pages (all read-only over the cache, Plotly figures, parameter sliders snap to the grid):
1. **Surface & model** — target surface, Dupire local vol, leverage function heatmap, calibration error map.
2. **Forward smile** — forward-start smiles `T1 → T2` vs parameters; forward ATM vol vs forward variance-swap vs forward vol swap; the put-wing invariance from the study.
3. **Forward skew** — forward ATM skew and curvature term structure vs ω, ρ, θ, k's.
4. **Smile dynamics** — conditional smile after a spot move, SSR term structure, vol-of-vol term structure, `Var(V)` decomposition.
5. **Model risk** — one product, all models calibrated to the same surface, price with error bars, side by side. This is the page the studies are really about.
6. **Product grid** — price heatmaps over two chosen parameters; vega ladders; spot-shift profiles; term-sheet editor for the product.
7. **Hedging** — P&L distributions of stored hedging runs.

Excel export button on every table (openpyxl).

### 9.1 PDE cross-check for the 1F LSV (`pde/lsv1f.py`, M6 Part 4)
State `(x = ln S, X)` with `θ = 0`: `V_t = ξ_0^t exp(ω X_t − ½ ω² (1 − e^{−2k1 t})/(2k1))`, `L = L(t, x)`. Backward PDE `∂_t u + (r − q − ½ L² V) ∂_x u − k1 X ∂_X u + ½ L² V ∂_xx u + ½ ∂_XX u + ρ L sqrt(V) ∂_xX u − r u = 0`, ADI (Hundsdorfer–Verwer or Craig–Sneyd) on a non-uniform grid, `x ∈ ±4 sd`, `X ∈ ±4 sqrt(1/(2k1))`, 400 × 120 points, dt from the `StepSchedule`. Continuous barriers as Dirichlet conditions. Validation: vanilla and digital vs MC within 2 stderr; continuous KO call and European KI put vs MC (bridge form) within 2 stderr; a 1y ATM VKO is out of scope (needs a third state variable). Grid convergence reported (Richardson in dx, dX, dt). Implemented grid: 400 × 121 (odd `n_X` so `X = 0` is a node) with the x half-width ±6 ATM sd for factor models and ±4 for BS / LV (owner decision at the M6 review); measured truncation on the pure 1F set (ω = 3, κ = 1.5, ρ = −0.7, ξ₀ = 4%, central spacing fixed): 1y call 6.905522 / 6.906973 / 6.907117 / 6.907168 at ±4 / ±6 / ±8 / ±12 sd (relative 2.4e-4 → 3e-5 → 7e-6), put 5.920395 / 5.921861 / 5.922006 / 5.922053, digital 0.597621 / 0.597707 / 0.597710 / 0.597708 (1.5e-4 → 5e-6), 3m call 3.720283 / 3.720620 / 3.720655 / 3.720671.

Headline additions (M6): a 3y annual autocall (AC 100%, c = 6% p.a., European KI 60%) and a 3y Phoenix (CB 70%, memory, American KI 60% daily): price, expected life, P(KI), for ω = 0/1/2/3 in 1F and the Table 8.2 2F set, 8·10⁵ particles.

### 9.2 M9 as built — precompute and results store (2026-09-15; `volsto/viewers/grid.py`, `store.py`, `precompute.py`, `configs/grids/*.yaml`, `tests/test_precompute.py`; owner's M9 Part 1 — the owner's `viewers/...` paths are the package `volsto/viewers/`, the console script `volsto-precompute` in `pyproject.toml`)

**Standing rule as built.** `volsto-precompute` is the one place of the viewers layer that calibrates a leverage (through `LeverageCache.get_or_calibrate`; every point logs a cache hit or a miss "calibrating" and the store records `calibrated_this_run`). The store, the read API and the pages only read the results store and the cache; a missing point is reported with the `volsto-precompute` command that produces it. Every Monte Carlo number the store exposes carries a `<name>_stderr` twin (checked table by table by `store.mc_columns_without_stderr` in the toy test); closed forms in the Var(V) table are flagged `kind = closed_form` with stderr 0.

**Grid YAML** (`GridSpec`, strict loading, `configs/grids/default.yaml`): `reference_spec` (the study spec whose particle / calibration-schedule / local-vol settings every point shares — `lsv_reference_2f.yaml`), `surfaces` (each with `kind: placeholder | snapshot`, the snapshot `path` with `market` / `ssvi` sections, and explicit flags `one_factor / two_factor / marking` — no defaults), `particle {n_particles: 800000 (§11 production), horizon: 3.0}`, `one_factor {nu, rho, kappa}` axes (`ω = 2ν`), `two_factor_presets` (Table 8.2), `marking {ssr_target: [0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5], skew_eps: [0.05, 0.10, 0.20]}`, `include_degenerate` (ω ∈ {1, 2, 3}, ρ −0.7, κ 1.5 appended when the axes miss them), `products {m4, m6, conditional, cliquet_maturities}`, `pricing {n_paths: 400000, seed: 2024}` (the M4 / M6 baseline convention), `risk {tier: light, products: [autocall 3y, cliquet 1y], light_pillars: [0.25, 1, 3]}`. Default grid: 1F axes ν ∈ {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1, 1.25, 1.5} × ρ ∈ {−0.9, −0.7, −0.5, −0.3, 0} × κ ∈ {0.5, 1.5, 4} = 105 combinations **on the placeholder only** (a snapshot opts in with `one_factor: true`), Table 8.2 on the placeholder, the 4 × 3 marking presets on each of the three SPX snapshots (`hdn_2022H2_ssvi/spx_2022-12-30`, `spx_2022-09-15`, `spx_2022-12-02`).

**Points** (`enumerate_points`, deterministic: surface order, mode order `lv, one_factor, two_factor, marking`, axis values). **ν = 0 is the LV point**: ω = 0 is the local-vol limit in the model sense (a particle calibration of a zero-vol-of-vol kernel would only reproduce Dupire up to particle noise), so the 15 `(ν = 0, ρ, κ)` combinations collapse onto the surface's single pure-LV point, keyed `lv:<sha256 of market + surface + local-vol config>` and built for **every** surface (the model-risk reference and the ω = 0 headline row); `resolve_axis_point` maps a slider at ν = 0 to it. The default grid therefore holds 131 points: placeholder 1 LV + 90 one-factor + 1 two-factor, each snapshot 1 LV + 12 marking. Calibrated points are identified by the leverage-cache key of their `CalibrationSpec` (a code-tag bump or a particle-count change is a new grid). Marking points are identified by the stable label `marking:<surface>:ssr<s>:eps<e>` — the fit (≈ 2 s, `fit_2f_marking` at k2 = 0.2, ν cap 3.5, two-point skew constraint) defines the parameters, so it runs when the point is computed (`resolve_marking`), not at enumeration; the store records the fitted parameters, `status` (interior / binding / infeasible), the naked-skew gaps, the mean skew gap, the messages and the cache key; an infeasible fit is a stored row with status `infeasible` and no calibration. `--shard i/n` is the interleaved shard `points[i-1::n]` (1-based).

**Results store** (`ResultsStore(root)`, everything relative to `root`, no absolute path stored — relocatable together with its cache): per point one directory `results/points/<id>/` holding one parquet per table (written to a temporary name and renamed) and `manifest.json`; per CLI run `results/runs/<utc>_<shard>.json` (argv, grid mapping, code tag, git commit, particle count, pricing config, cost model, per-point wall clocks); `results/manifest.json` the merged view. **Per-point files + merge on read** was chosen because concurrent shards then never touch the same file; `compact()` optionally writes `results/<table>.parquet` (the reader takes per-point files first, compacted rows for removed point directories). Tables: `points` (id, label, surface, mode, status, the seven model parameters, axis values, `cache_key`, `n_particles`, `horizon`, `calibrated_this_run`, `calibration_seconds` — the leverage's measured wall time, from the cache on a hit —, `mean_abs_L_minus_1` in the M7 convention (`|k| ≤ 2` ATM sd per slice), `max_abs_error_vp` / `max_z` from a stored `CalibrationReport` when present, `wall_seconds` split in calibration / pricing / analytics / risk, `risk_tier`, pricing paths and seed, `git_commit`, `code_tag`, `created_utc`, the fit columns of marking points); `products` (product, quantity, `key` = the `run_headline` column or `<product>:<column>` of the M6 table so the model-risk page matches the baselines on it, value, value_stderr, unit); `forward_smile` (1y → 2y and 2y → 3y on the headline strikes + ATMF: iv, price, each with stderr); `forward_vols` (fwd ATM vol / fwd VS / fwd vol swap per window); `ssr` (T, `ssr_lsv`, `skew_lsv`, `slope`, `atmf_vol` with stderr, `eps` = 0.05 the M7 stage-3 convention, `ssr_naked_first_order` = the fit's first-order P1 SSR at the pillar for marking points, `ssr_target`); `varv` (T, term, value, stderr, kind); `risk` (product, tier, group, name, value, stderr, unit, size, scheme, states, T, bucket, regime, variant). The M4 columns without a standard error (`p_itm`, `p_ko_itm_*`, the ITM-conditional realised-vol percentiles) are **not** stored (no MC number without its stderr) — an open item for the M4 runner.

**CLI** `volsto-precompute --grid PATH --store ROOT --cache ROOT --shard i/n --workers k --resume --risk none|light|full --n-particles N --dry-run --probe --limit N`. Per point: (1) the leverage through the cache, (2) `run_headline` (+ `run_m6_headline`) on this point's model with the pricing `SimConfig` (schedule and scheme of the spec, 400k paths, seed 2024), (3) the 1y → 2y smile from step 2 and the 2y → 3y smile + `forward_vol_comparison`, (4) `ssr_numerical_many` at {0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 3}y within the horizon, (5) `var_decomposition` at 1y and 3y within the horizon (LSV points), (6) the `RiskReport` tier for `autocall 3y` and `cliquet 1y` — light = `delta` (five regimes) + `fwd_var` (default 20 buckets) + `skew` at the three light pillars; full = every section at the default pillars — (7) the store write. `--workers k` runs the shard in a spawn pool with `NUMBA_NUM_THREADS = max(1, cpu_count // k)` per process (set in the environment before the pool starts). `--resume` skips a point whose store row exists and whose leverage sits in the cache (LV / infeasible rows need none); nothing is recomputed, so the per-point manifest entries are unchanged (asserted).

**Cost model and projection** (`--dry-run` / before every run, a running ETA after each point): calibration = the median measured `wall_time` of the cache manifest at the grid's particle count and horizon (current code tag; linearly rescaled from another count when absent; else the documented fallback 140 s), charged to cache misses; pricing and analytics = the `--probe` measurement or the measured fallbacks; risk = calibrations per tier × calibration cost (LSV points) + pricings per tier × the single-pricing cost. **Measured on this machine (12 cores)**: risk-report cost per point (both products, states shared; counted with the LV builder on the placeholder surface, `RiskEngine.n_calibrations` / `n_pricings`; the Black–Scholes test bed gives the same light count): light **34 calibrations, 72 pricings**; full **90 calibrations, 356 pricings**. Pricing at 400k paths on the cached placeholder 1F ω = 2 point (8·10⁵ particles): M4 headline 33.8 s, 2y → 3y smile + triple 47.4 s, M6 autocall / Phoenix 95.2 s (pricing 176 s); SSR at five pillars 64.3 s, Var(V) at 1y and 3y 60.3 s (analytics 125 s); one risk pricing 21.7 s (autocall 3y) / 10.5 s (cliquet 1y). Calibration at 8·10⁵, 3y: median 138.0 s over the 32 code-tag-m6 entries of the repository cache at the time of the dry run. Toy grid (`configs/grids/toy.yaml`, 2·10⁴ particles, 1y horizon, 4000 paths): 3 calibrations of 2.2–2.5 s, per point ≈ 9.7 s total (calibration step 8.4 s including the market build, pricing 0.7 s, analytics 0.6 s), the LV point 4.0 s; the whole test file 35 s.

**Projected wall clock of the default grid at 8·10⁵ particles** (`volsto-precompute --grid configs/grids/default.yaml --dry-run`, verbatim; 123 of the 127 calibrating points are cache misses today — the three placeholder degenerate points and Table 8.2 are cached at 8·10⁵; the 36 marking points are re-fitted at compute time and count as misses until resolved, and the SPX 2022-12-30 (1.0, 0.10) fit's 8·10⁵ leverage of M7 is a hit when the fit reproduces its recorded parameters exactly; the calibration cost comes from the cache manifest's measured wall times):

```
grid 'default' (configs/grids/default.yaml): 131 points, 800000 particles, horizon 3y, pricing 400000 paths seed 2024, risk tier light (20-bucket fwd-var ladder, skew pillars [0.25, 1.0, 3.0]); shard 1/1: 131 points
  store <a scratch store>
  cache /Users/idrissdadoun/Code/volsto/cache
  placeholder          lv              1
  placeholder          one_factor     90
  placeholder          two_factor      1
  spx_2022-12-30       lv              1
  spx_2022-12-30       marking        12
  spx_2022-09-15       lv              1
  spx_2022-09-15       marking        12
  spx_2022-12-02       lv              1
  spx_2022-12-02       marking        12
  one_factor axes: 7 nu x 5 rho x 3 kappa = 105 combinations per surface on ['placeholder']: 90 LSV calibrations + 15 ω=0 combinations collapsing onto the surface's single LV point (pure Dupire, no calibration); the degenerate ω=1,2,3 points are included
projected wall clock (per tier; the requested tier is marked *; the light tier at the configured fwd-var bucket count and at the alternative one):
  overhead: 3.8 s per point (market build, leverage load / Dupire)
  calibration cost: 137.9 s per cache miss — cache manifest median of 120 entries at 800000 particles, horizon 3y (code tag m6)
  diagnostics cost: 26.0 s per calibration (pillar repricing; also per hit without a report under --diagnostics)
  pricing / analytics cost: 176.4 / 124.6 s per point, risk pricing 16.1 s each — measured fallbacks at 400000 paths rescaled to 400000
  none   131 points (123 misses) x (3.8 overhead + 137.9 cal + 26.0 diag + 176.4 pricing + 124.6 analytics + 0.0 risk [0 cal, 0 pricings] s; mean 458.7 s/point) = 16.69 h
 *light  131 points (123 misses) x (3.8 overhead + 137.9 cal + 26.0 diag + 176.4 pricing + 124.6 analytics + 5848.3 risk [34 cal, 72 pricings, 20-bucket fwd-var ladder] s; mean 6163.8 s/point) = 224.29 h
  light  131 points (123 misses) x (3.8 overhead + 137.9 cal + 26.0 diag + 176.4 pricing + 124.6 analytics + 2956.3 risk [17 cal, 38 pricings, 3-bucket fwd-var ladder] s; mean 3343.5 s/point) = 121.66 h
  full   131 points (123 misses) x (3.8 overhead + 137.9 cal + 26.0 diag + 176.4 pricing + 124.6 analytics + 18143.9 risk [90 cal, 356 pricings] s; mean 18223.6 s/point) = 663.14 h
dry run: nothing computed
```

Reading: the tier is the whole decision. Without risk the default grid is 16.7 h of single-process laptop time (123 calibrations 4.7 h, their diagnostics 0.9 h, pricing and analytics 11 h). The light tier's cost is its forward-variance ladder: at the M5 20-bucket ladder it is 34 leverage recalibrations and 72 pricings per LSV point — 1.6 h a point, 224 h in all — and at the 3-bucket ladder built from the light pillars 17 and 38, i.e. 122 h. The full tier is 90 recalibrations and 356 pricings a point, 663 h. `--shard i/n` divides any of these; the VM decision is the tier and the shard count, not the calibrations, which are 5.6 h of the total whatever the tier.

**Tests** (`tests/test_precompute.py`, 5 tests, fast): the default grid counts and shard partition (no computation), `--dry-run` on the default grid against the repository cache (prints the projection, computes and stores nothing, uses the manifest median), the toy end-to-end test — it **consumes the one sanctioned toy calibration of the session fixture `tests/conftest.py::toy_build`** (shards 1/2 and 2/2 into a temporary cache; see "Tests and measured" below) and performs live only the non-calibrating steps (resume on both shards with hits only and identical manifest entries, store + cache moved together and read unchanged on a private copy, stderr on every MC column, the LV point present), an empty-store reader, and the compaction round trip.

**Tests and measured** (S4 of M9, 2026-09-15; `tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_viewers_app.py`, `tests/test_precompute.py`; the S4 adversarial review applied). **No viewer, no API call and no test recalibrates except the conftest toy fixture**: the toy calibration runs exactly once per pytest run, in the session fixture `tests/conftest.py::toy_build` — `volsto-precompute` on `configs/grids/toy.yaml` as shards 1/2 then 2/2 into a temporary directory (never the repository cache), the synthetic M7 / M8b outputs written beside it; under `pytest -n auto` the workers share that one build (controller base temp + `os.mkdir` lock + `done.json`, stdlib only; verified after each run: one `done.json`, three precompute "calibrating" records — one in shard 1/2, two in 2/2). The fixture records per shard the wall clock, the captured stdout, the `volsto` log records and the cache manifest; `tests/test_precompute.py::test_toy_precompute_end_to_end` consumes them (4 store rows, 3 calibrated + the LV point, the manifests, the calibrating records, stderr on every MC column) and performs live only the non-calibrating steps on a private copy — `--resume` on both shards with hits only and identical per-point manifest entries, store + cache moved together and read unchanged, resume against the moved pair — the fixture directory asserted unchanged in size and mtime afterwards. Measured on this laptop: toy build 12.9 s (shard 1/2: the LV point + one calibration) + 18.2 s (shard 2/2: two calibrations) = 31.2 s inside the fixture (3 calibrations at 2·10⁴ particles of ≈ 2.2 s each; the rest is the market build, pricing at 4000 paths and the analytics). `tests/test_viewers_app.py` (21 tests): `test_every_page_renders` (8 pages × {synthetic, toy}: title, ≥ 1 dataframe, ≥ 1 figure or — page 6 on the toy store — the two-grid-axes notice and the `--risk` command, the Excel export; on the toy store the cache files unchanged by size and mtime after the render and every LSV point's leverage read through `api.get_leverage`), `test_toy_api_stderr_schema` (every API frame on the toy store passes `columns_without_stderr`, the npz metadata carries `n_particles` 20 000 and the 1y horizon, a risk request raises `MissingPoint` naming `--risk light`), `test_relocatable_end_to_end` (store + cache + outputs copied A → B; nothing under B names A — every store JSON with the provenance `argv` stripped, every string cell of every store table, the cache `spec.json`s and the npz metadata; outside xdist A is renamed away while the B half runs and restored; API frames, leverage values and every page's dataframes and figure specifications identical: 30 dataframes and 27 figures over the 8 pages; 45.2 s serial including the toy build, the rename-aside branch taken), `test_check_pages_in_process`, `test_viewer_check_cli` (`--check` on the toy triple: 8 pages ok; on an empty triple: exit 0, store reported EMPTY, and **nothing created** — `ResultsStore` no longer makes directories on construction, the writers create them), `test_precompute_dry_run_cli` (toy grid: `4 points (0 misses)`, nothing written; default grid against the repository cache and a temporary store: the 131-point projection, the store root not created). `tests/test_precompute.py` (5 tests): the grid counts and shards, the default-grid dry run (writes nothing — not even the store root), the toy end-to-end above, the empty-store reader, the compaction round trip. Headless page renders (`volsto-viewer --check`, wall per page, cold process): page 1 (SSVI + Dupire surface build) 5.7 s synthetic / 6.8 s toy / 6.2 s on the repository store; pages 2–8 0.08–0.24 s each; whole check 6.6 s synthetic, 7.7 s toy, 7.2 s on the repository roots (`outputs/store`, 5 points at 8·10⁵ particles, `cache`, `outputs`), 6.3 s on an empty triple (all 8 pages ok, notices only). Runs (`caffeinate -i`, `-n auto`, 12 cores): `tests/test_precompute.py` + `tests/test_viewers_app.py` 26 passed in 54.5 s (one toy build); the whole viewers set `test_viewers_app.py`, `test_viewers_api.py` (23 tests), `test_viewers_pages_a.py`, `_b.py`, `_c.py`, `test_precompute.py` with `-m "not slow"`: 83 passed in 72.1 s. The default-grid cost is the projection block quoted above in this section (131 points; none 15.67 h / light 223.42 h / full 662.49 h laptop-hours) — the tests only re-read it from the dry run. Open item of this pass: `--resume` is per point (a stored point with a missing risk table is skipped; the per-point flag is an S1 item). (2026-09-17: since M10 the suite has two more sanctioned calibrating fixtures, `toy_marking_build` in `tests/conftest.py` and `toy_backtest_build` in `tests/_backtest_build.py`; §10.2–10.3.)


**Read API and app** (S2 of M9, 2026-09-15; `volsto/viewers/config.py`, `api.py`, `components.py`, `app.py`, `pages/`, `tests/test_viewers_api.py`, `tests/_synthetic_store.py`; console script `volsto-viewer = volsto.viewers.app:main`). `ViewerConfig(cache_root, store_root, outputs_root, snapshots_root, grid_path)` is resolved defaults ← `configs/viewer.yaml` (optional, or `VOLSTO_VIEWER_CONFIG`) ← `VOLSTO_CACHE / VOLSTO_STORE / VOLSTO_OUTPUTS / VOLSTO_SNAPSHOTS / VOLSTO_GRID` ← the `volsto-viewer --cache/--store/--outputs/--snapshots/--grid` flags; defaults `cache/`, `outputs/store`, `outputs/`, `configs/surfaces/snapshots`, `configs/grids/default.yaml` (repository-root-relative). The read API (`volsto/viewers/api.py`, the surface an MCP server will sit on in M11) is small and typed and returns compact frames / records: `list_grid` (one row per stored point: id, label, surface, mode, status, the seven parameters, axes, particle count, `has_<table>` flags and `has_leverage` — a file-existence check), `get_point → PointRecord` (params, diagnostics, fit, provenance, the `products / forward_smile / forward_vols / ssr / varv` frames), `list_surfaces` / `get_surface → SurfaceRecord` (the `ImpliedSurface` and the Dupire `LocalVolSurface` built from the placeholder / snapshot config — pure construction, measured 3.5 s / 2.0 s here — with `implied_vol_table`, `local_vol_table`, `atm_table`), `get_leverage → LeverageRecord` (the cached `LeverageFunction` read **by file**, `<cache>/<key>/leverage.npz` + `diagnostics.json`, with the error map `T, k, error_vp, error_vp_stderr`), `get_products` (the term-sheet catalogue: name, class, unit, fields, flagged with what the store holds), `get_risk` (section, name, T / bucket, regime, value, value_stderr, unit, tier), `list_hedging_runs` / `get_hedging_run → HedgingRunRecord` / `get_hedging_pnl` / `get_hedging_table` (the stored M8b `TaskResult` JSONs, the per-path P&L samples of the `.pkl`, the A–D tables) and `get_marking → MarkingRecord` (the M7 Part 3 tables with `*_se` renamed `*_stderr`, the `per_vp_90_110_*` columns given `per_rota`'s scaled stderr, and the rotation convention). **Nothing in the API calibrates or simulates** — `LeverageCache` and `get_or_calibrate` are not even imported (asserted by AST in the test); a missing point raises `MissingPoint(key, command)` whose `command` is `volsto-precompute --grid <grid> --store <store> --cache <cache> --resume` (the precompute CLI has no `--only KEY`; `--resume` is **per point** — it skips a point whose store row exists and whose leverage sits in the cache, so a stored point with a missing risk table is skipped until a per-point flag lands; that flag is an open S1 item), a missing risk table adds `--risk light`, a missing M7 / M8b file raises `MissingArtefact` naming `scripts/m7_p1_marking.py` / `scripts/m8b.py --study X`. Standard errors: `columns_without_stderr(df)` walks a frame against the declared exact set (parameters, axes, maturities, counts, wall clocks, closed-form surface values, first-order SSRs); the upstream columns M7 / M8b wrote without a stderr are listed in `UNPAIRED_UPSTREAM` / `MARKING_UNPAIRED_PREFIXES` (the stage-3 bias maxima of `p1_marking_fits.csv`, the M8b per-regime `std` / mean realised vol, the stage-3 SSR means of the binding maps) rather than padded — open items for those runners. Components (`components.py`): `snap_slider` (a `select_slider` over the grid's values only), `stderr_bar` / `error_trace` (plotly `error_y` + `± stderr` hover), `table_with_export` (`st.dataframe` + an openpyxl Excel `download_button`), `figure_with_download` (PNG / SVG through kaleido, deferred to the click, HTML fallback when the engine fails), `missing_point_notice`, `page_header` (roots, code tag, git commit, grid, particle count, points and runs). App: `volsto-viewer` resolves the config, exports it to the environment and runs `streamlit run volsto/viewers/app.py -- <flags>`; the script registers the eight pages with `st.navigation` — as rendered by the page streams: `1_surface_model.py` Surface & model, `2_forward_smile.py` Forward smile, `3_forward_skew.py` Forward skew, `4_smile_dynamics.py` Smile dynamics, `5_model_risk.py` Model risk, `6_product_grid.py` Product grid, `7_marking.py` Marking / calibration, `8_hedging.py` Hedging (`volsto.viewers.pages.PAGES`); every page exposes `render(cfg)` and the guard `run_if_streamlit(render)` (renders with `ViewerConfig.from_env()` only under a Streamlit script-run context, so `AppTest.from_file` drives it and importing renders nothing). Tests (`tests/test_viewers_api.py`, 23 tests, ~18 s, no calibration, no MC): config precedence; the synthetic store (`tests/_synthetic_store.py`: 3 points on 2 surfaces incl. a marking point, every table, a synthetic M8b run and the M7 tables); the stderr walk over every API return; `MissingPoint` / `MissingArtefact` commands; the API never creates the (empty) cache root; relocatability (store copied, frames identical); `get_surface` for the placeholder, the grid snapshot and a snapshot file off the grid; the real `outputs/m7` and `outputs/m8b` schemas when present; the export encoders (xlsx round trip, kaleido SVG / PNG); the page registry; `volsto-viewer --help`; the eight pages (as listed above, rendered by the page streams) rendered headless with `AppTest` against the synthetic store, and the empty-store header printing the precompute command.

**Adversarial review and the fix pass (2026-09-15).** Every stream was reviewed by an independent
agent that reproduced each claim before filing it (headless `AppTest` renders, greps, scripts
against the repository's own cache and outputs) and then re-verified after the fixes; the review
verdicts were `accept_with_fixes` throughout, and 57 findings were applied at the root,
each with the test the finding asked for. What the reviews changed, beyond the cosmetic:

- **A refit dropped a target column** — the same defect class as the hedger's (§8.2): nothing in
  the viewer layer, but found the same day.
- **Page 6 never showed a gamma regime from a real store**: the page looked for gamma rows inside
  the `delta` section while `RiskReport` groups them under `gamma` (the synthetic fixture happened
  to write the page's own layout, so the test agreed with the bug). The page is now keyed on the
  store's `section` column and renders both namings, and the fixture writes what the precompute
  writes.
- **Page 7 showed nothing by default on the real fits**: the two snap sliders were independent and
  their default cross product (`ssr_target` 1, `skew_eps` 0.05) is a pair no run ever fitted. The
  second slider now offers only the values fitted for the chosen first one, and the surface
  defaults to the SPX snapshot.
- **Monte Carlo numbers with a padded error bar**: page 3's forward/spot ratio zero-filled a
  missing stderr twin, page 7's pillar reader padded a missing `@T_stderr` with NaN, page 8 drew a
  study-B bar whose stderr was NaN. A column without its twin is now not plotted at all — the page
  names the column and the script that produces it — and rows with a NaN value or stderr are
  counted in a caption instead of being drawn.
- **A guessed stderr twin broke on a new column** (2026-09-16): the M8b tables wrote the desk mean's stderr under a stem (`desk_mean` beside `mean_se`, `recal_pnl_desk` beside `recal_se`) and the read API re-attached it by prefix. When study D gained `mean_delta`, the prefix matched two columns and the walker test failed on the real table. The writers now emit exact twins (`<value>_se`, pinned by a walk over every table the builders emit), and the API's re-attachment of older tables skips any candidate that already has its own twin.
- **A read path that wrote**: `ResultsStore` created `results/points` and `results/runs` in its
  constructor, so `volsto-viewer --check` on an empty store left directories behind. The
  directories are created by the writers now, and the test pins that a check creates nothing.
- **The cache stamp missed a running shard**: the pages keyed their caches on the merged
  manifest's mtime, which the precompute writes once at the end of a run. The stamp is now the
  newest of the merged manifest, every per-point manifest and the cache root.
- **Claims stronger than their assertions**: several tests asserted `>=` where the property is an
  equality (one Excel button per table, two image buttons per figure), listed four forbidden
  symbols where the rule names eight, or printed a docstring the body did not check. Those are
  equalities now, and the module docstrings say what is actually asserted.
- **One owner rule was contradicted by the build**: the toy calibration ran in two test sites. It
  now runs exactly once per pytest run, in the session fixture `tests/conftest.py::toy_build`,
  which both `tests/test_precompute.py` and `tests/test_viewers_app.py` consume; every other test
  reads the cache and never calibrates.

The fix passes were run as four workflows of implementer plus independent verifier, each verifier
reproducing every claim before and after: the store and API streams (30 findings), the three page
streams (27), then two sweeps for one class of defect the first verifier found beyond its brief —
a Monte Carlo number plotted without its standard error. That class is now closed by construction:
`mc_rows` / `missing_mc_notice` / `nothing_plottable` in `pages/_common.py` are the single
implementation, the four derived helpers (`fit_smiles`, `wing_spread`, `forward_spot_skew_ratio`,
`model_risk_table`) each return `(frame, dropped, absent)` so a caller can never plot what it
cannot annotate, and every page declares its `(value, stderr)` pairs in `MC_PAIRS`, walked against
the read API's real output by a test. The final adversarial sweep ran 248 headless renders over
pathological copies of the store, cache and outputs — the stderr column dropped, NaN on one row,
NaN on every row, the value NaN, the value column dropped, the table empty — decoding every
plotly specification: zero NaN error bars, zero "± nan" hovers, zero empty figures, zero
disagreements between a value heatmap and its stderr heatmap.

**Measured at the close (2026-09-16).** The viewers set is 131 tests, 85 s under `-n auto`
(`tests/test_viewers_pages_a|b|c.py`, `test_viewers_api.py`, `test_viewers_app.py`,
`test_precompute.py`), with exactly one calibrating site in the whole suite: the toy-grid session
fixture in `tests/conftest.py` (3 calibrations at 2·10⁴ particles, ~31 s, shared across xdist
workers). `volsto-viewer --check` on the repository roots renders all eight pages in 6.9 s
(page 1 5.8 s for the surface build, the rest 0.10-0.25 s) against the 5-point placeholder store,
and on an empty store-cache-outputs triple it renders all eight, exits 0 and creates nothing.

**VM runbook and measured-cost sizing** (2026-09-16; `docs/vm_grid_run.md`, `volsto-precompute report`, `--cost-from`). The owner provisions the VM; the runbook gives the command lines, the expected per-shard wall clock, the rsync back of the store AND the cache, and the arrival check. `volsto-precompute report --store ROOT` reads the run records (wall clock, host, workers, threads, calibrated vs cache hits, per-point cost by mode and step, core-seconds); `--dry-run --cost-from ROOT` sizes a grid from a previous store's measured costs. **The owner's ~3 h target is not reachable on one 48-vCPU instance:** a grid point's work is mostly serial (12 threads buy 1.70× over one; the particle pass 1.41×), so the 126.5-hour projection is twelve-thread laptop time and the grid needs many single-thread workers, capped by physical cores and memory.

**VM sizing, corrected** (2026-09-16; `volsto/viewers/precompute.py`, `volsto/calibration/cache.py`, `docs/vm_grid_run.md`).

*Risk-step cost.* Measured on the laptop at 1 thread on one placeholder 1F point and the LV point, light tier on the 3-bucket ladder (17 bumped states, 38 pricings), at two budgets: probe C (10⁵ particles / 5·10⁴ paths) and production (8·10⁵ / 4·10⁵; 1F risk step 4283.0 s of 4984.8 s, LV 943.5 s of 1223.9 s, peak RSS 2.36 GiB). The step decomposes as

`states × (RISK_STATE_OVERHEAD_S + RISK_STATE_BUILD_S[mode]) + (states − 1) × particle pass (LSV) + pricings × RISK_PRICING_RATIO[mode] × pricing step`

with:
- RISK_STATE_OVERHEAD_S = 4.86 s: work outside the engine (arbitrage checks, bucket ε sizing, base build); budget- and mode-independent to 2%;
- RISK_STATE_BUILD_S = 6.05 s (LSV) / 1.04 s (LV, the Dupire rebuild);
- RISK_PRICING_RATIO = 0.1044 (LSV) / 0.1183 (LV); budget-independent to 3.5%.

The 17th state is the point's own leverage, so an LSV point pays 16 particle passes.

`--cost-from` derives the same quantities from a store's risk budgets by mode (`risk_budget_costs`):
- per-state outside work = (step wall − engine wall) / states;
- LSV pricing = (engine − misses × particle pass − states × build) / pricings;
- LV pricing = (engine − states × build) / pricings, since an LV point's "misses" are Dupire rebuilds.
Pricings scale with paths and threads; per-state costs are serial and are never rescaled.

*Threads.* `thread_factor` reads MEASURED_THREAD_RATIO: t(n)/t(1) at 1/2/4/8/12 threads on the probe, interpolated in 1/n.
- Particle pass: 0.918 / 0.801 / 0.717 / 0.644.
- Pricing: 0.752 / 0.616 / 0.554 / 0.545.
- Diagnostics: 0.733 / 0.586 / 0.519 / 0.503.
- Analytics: 0.754 / 0.621 / 0.557 / 0.552.

Least-squares Amdahl fractions over all counts (0.316 / 0.549 / 0.504 / 0.498) are used only beyond 12 threads. They fit diagnostics, pricing and analytics to within 0.008, but misfit the particle pass by +0.076 at 2 threads and −0.066 at 12. The 2-thread figures are for real cores; an SMT sibling gains less.

*Grid cost.* The default grid (light tier, 3 buckets) costs 177.0 process-hours at 1 thread at laptop per-core speed (cross-checks: 180.6 and 185.7). The wall-clock floor at laptop speed (a lower bound) is 7.37 h on 24 × 1, 3.69 h on 48 × 1, and 3.67 / 3.70 h for `--shard 1/2`, `2/2` at 24 × 1. With a VM per-core factor f (1.0–1.5) and a concurrency efficiency e (0.75–1.0), the expected wall clock is:

| layout | expected | range |
|---|---|---|
| one 48-vCPU instance | about 10 h | 7–16 h |
| one 96-vCPU instance | about 5 h | 3.5–8 h |
| two 48-vCPU instances | about 5 h | 3.5–8 h |

Memory is 2.36 GiB per process with the risk step on, so a 48-vCPU instance needs 57 GiB and a 96-vCPU instance 113 GiB. The runbook's probes A–D measure f, e(K), the risk costs and the production RSS on the VM.

*Dry run.* The `parallel (shard i/n, M points to compute)` line is the wall clock of the points that invocation computes, floored at its longest point.

*Report.* A wall a record does not carry is NaN and shown as absent, never 0. Peak RSS is reported in GiB.

*Cache manifest.* `LeverageCache._append_manifest` holds an exclusive `flock` on `manifest.parquet.lock` and publishes by temp file + `os.replace`; the format is unchanged. `tests/test_cache_concurrency.py` runs 12 writer processes × 30 rows with 3 readers: 0 failures, no rows lost. The pre-fix code on the same test failed 571–1188 operations and lost 306 of 360 rows.

**Atomic leverage cache** (2026-09-16; `volsto/calibration/cache.py`).

Every file the cache writes goes through `atomic_write(dest, write)`. This covers `leverage.npz`, `spec.json`, `diagnostics.json` and `manifest.parquet`.
- **How a write works.** The writer fills a temporary file beside the destination. The file is named `.<name>.<16 hex>.tmp<suffix>` (`TEMP_GLOB = '.*.tmp*'`) and is created with `O_EXCL` and mode `0666 & ~umask`. The file is fsync'd, then `os.replace`d over the destination, and the directory is fsync'd. On an exception the temporary is removed.
- **What a kill leaves.** The previous file or the new one, never a torn file. At most one leftover temporary remains, and no reader looks at it.

Order within an entry:
1. `spec.json`;
2. removal of a stale `diagnostics.json`;
3. `leverage.npz`, the commit point;
4. `diagnostics.json`;
5. the manifest row, under the `flock`.

Reading an entry:
- **`has()` / `has_key()`:** a hit is a `leverage.npz` that is a complete zip archive. A torn file from before this change is a logged miss, so `--resume` recalibrates it.
- **`unlisted_keys()`:** lists committed entries that have no manifest row (a kill between steps 3 and 5). Such an entry is still a valid hit.
- **`temporaries()`:** lists leftovers. Delete them when no writer is running.
- **`merge_manifest(rows)`:** adds another copy's rows for keys this cache lacks, under the lock and atomically. It is the runbook's merge of a VM's manifest.

The precompute's hit test, its `--resume` test and its diagnostics refresh go through `has_key` and `write_report`.

Tests (`tests/test_cache_concurrency.py`):
- a walking test that every file is published by `atomic_write` with the umask mode;
- a SIGKILL halfway through a leverage save, for a new key and an existing key;
- a save that raises half-way;
- a torn legacy file;
- an unlisted entry.

On the old code, the kill scenario gives has() = True followed by `BadZipFile` on load.

**LV risk-pricing fallback** (`volsto/viewers/precompute.py`). `RISK_PRICING_RATIO["lv"]` is measured against the LV pricing step. When no LV pricing is measured (the default model, or a `--cost-from` store without an LV point), that step is taken as `LV_TO_LSV_PRICING_RATIO` (187.5 / 265.4 = 0.7065, production, 1 thread) × the LSV pricing. The default dry run of `configs/grids/default.yaml` gives 126.52 h for light on the 3-bucket ladder and 238.54 h on the 20-bucket ladder. It is a single-process projection from the cache manifest and the 12-thread fallbacks. The measured 1-thread light-tier cost is 177 process-hours (`--cost-from`).

---

## 10. Tests and studies

Unit and property tests (pytest, fast, `n_paths` small with loose tolerances; a `slow` marker for the full-size ones):
- BS: put-call parity, implied-vol round trip, Greeks vs finite differences.
- SSVI: no-arbitrage checks; Dupire local vol positive; local-vol MC reprices SSVI at pillars to within tolerance.
- Variance swap: replication strike equals `E[realised variance]` under local vol MC; `ξ_0` integrates back to the strip.
- Bergomi: `E[ξ_t^T] = ξ_0^T` (martingale) for 1F and 2F; exact step (eqs. 7.15–7.18) vs fine Euler; `χ(t,T)` (eq. 7.35) vs sample variance of `x_t^T`; `Cov(ξ_u^u, ξ_v^v)` closed form vs MC; `Var(∫ξ)` closed form vs MC; θ = 0 reproduces the 1F model path-by-path with the same seed; vol of VS vol from simulation vs eq. 7.39; with Table 8.2 parameters and a flat 20% VS term structure, the mixing-solution ATMF skew vs eq. 8.55 and the numerical SSR (§4.4) vs eq. 9.21 (book Figure 9.1 dots vs line — tolerance consistent with 'order one in ν' accuracy, i.e. agreement within a few percent relative at 1y, not exact); mixing-solution smile vs spot-simulation MC smile within 2 stderr.
- LSV: calibration acceptance criterion of §4.2 on the reference surface; variance swaps unchanged across ω (surface-calibrated invariance); LSV SSR numerical estimator vs the analytic decomposition eq. 12.52 (order-one accuracy).
- Identity tests from the study: ATM implied variance equals the dollar-gamma-weighted average of realised variance along the path (verify to ≤ 0.1 vol point); forward-start ATM vol below forward vol swap when ρ < 0.
- PDE cross-check: 1F LSV, 2D finite differences (ADI) on `(ln S, X1)`, vanilla and forward-start prices vs MC within 2 stderr.
- Products: cliquet decomposition (strip of capped calls + global-floor put) reprices the product; KI put decomposition reprices the autocall's put leg; discrete barrier converges to continuous with the bridge correction.
- Risk: CRN bump Greeks vs analytic BS Greeks in the BS model; vega ladder sums to the parallel vega.
- Scheme tests: pure LV vs SSVI at 1m/3m/6m ATM within 0.1 vp on the default schedule; bias halves when dt halves; Richardson diagnostic 2P(dt/2) − P(dt) under CRN; the calibrated LSV simulated with the calibration seed reproduces the particle cloud at the horizon to 1e-12; acceptance tests price on fresh seeds.
- M4 regression baseline (placeholder surface, 400k paths, seed 2024; `tests/test_m4_regression.py`), re-recorded at M4b on the second-order step and the 1/1460–1/365–1/250 schedule with 2·10⁵ particles: 1y→2y ATMF forward vol 21.44/20.76/19.69/18.45 (ω = 0/1/2/3, 1F ρ = −0.7, κ = 1.5) and 19.17 (2F Table 8.2); forward VS 25.1–25.3; capped cliquet 1y 1.193/1.314/1.624/1.968 % and 2y 0.754/0.951/1.459/2.024 %; tolerance max(2 stderr, 0.02% of notional / 0.02 vol points). Against the M4 record every entry moved by less than 2 stderr except the 2y cliquet at ω ≥ 2 and the 2F set (+0.017–0.025% of notional, 2.1–2.9σ) and the ω = 3 forward vol swap (−0.05 vp, 2.3σ). Re-recorded at 8·10⁵ particles in M4c (`HEADLINE_N_PARTICLES` in the test file): 21.46/20.82/19.70/18.45 and 19.22 forward vol, forward VS 25.1–25.3, cliquet 1y 1.199/1.321/1.629/1.971 % and 2y 0.749/0.954/1.464/2.033 % — at most 1.2σ from the 2·10⁵ record — plus the §6.4 conditional-variance and VKO columns. The study-archive tests are skipped until `study_archive/` is present.
- Regression tests (`slow`, recalled from the original study — must be re-verified against the study archive; the SSVI parameters and seeds must be taken from that archive, not guessed): with the study surface, 1F, `ρ = −0.7`, `κ = 1.5`: 1y-into-1y ATM forward vol 21.5% (LV) → 18.6% (ω = 3), forward variance-swap level 25.2–25.5% across ω; 1y capped cliquet (2% cap, global floor 0) 1.082% / 1.194% / 1.472% / 1.763% of notional for ω = 0/1/2/3 with stderr 0.004–0.009%; 2y version 0.639% → 1.718%. Tolerance: 2 stderr, or 0.02% of notional if the seeds cannot be matched.

### 10.1 M10 Part 1 — study runner (as built, 2026-09-16)

`volsto/studies/runner.py`, `results.py`, `latex.py`, `style.py`, `volsto/studies/catalogue/`, `configs/studies/catalogue/README.md`, `tests/test_study_runner.py`; console script `volsto-study`, optional extra `studies = ["matplotlib>=3.8"]`; LaTeX engine Tectonic.

**A study is a strict YAML config plus a runner module.** Config keys: `name`, `runner`, `question` (optional one line; defaults to the module's `QUESTION`), `grid` (may be null), `store`, `cache`, `outputs`, `mode` (full | fast), `seeds`, `params`; an unknown or missing key is an error. YAML paths resolve against the repository root; the CLI overrides (`--grid --store --cache --outputs --out`) resolve against the cwd; `--fast` sets the mode; `--set a.b=v` sets a YAML-parsed params entry. The module declares `TITLE`, `QUESTION`, `REQUIRED_PARAMS` (optionally `OPTIONAL_PARAMS`, `validate_params`) and implements `requirements(ctx)`, `compute(ctx) -> Results` and `tables / figures / narrative(results)`; the last three see the stored results only, and an undeclared params key is an error.
