**Output directory** (`volsto-study run`, default `outputs/studies/<name>`): `results.parquet`; `tables/<name>.tex` (booktabs); `figures/<name>.pdf` and `.png` (house style); `study.md` (title, the question first, the narrative with tables inlined — a line `{{table:x}}` / `{{figure:x}}` places one, the rest follow — then the provenance block); `study.tex` (master document); `study.pdf` (Tectonic; `--no-latex-check` skips it); `manifest.json` — study, runner and its sha256, the resolved config and its sha256 (and the YAML file's), git commit and `git_dirty`, `code_version`, `calibration_code_tag`, grid {path, sha256, name}, `cache_keys` [{key, what, n_particles}], `store_points`, `artefacts` [{path, sha256}], particle counts, seeds, `n_paths` and other recorded entries, mode, table and figure names, `wall_clock` {requirements, compute, render, total}, `recalibrated: false` (asserted), host and library versions, `created_utc`, masked `argv`, `results_sha256`, the LaTeX check result.

**Fast mode is said once, by the runner:** a study whose manifest mode is `fast` carries the banner "FAST MODE — reduced paths and toy inputs: plumbing check, not results." under its question in `study.md` and `study.tex` (read from the stored manifest, so render stays byte-identical), a `mode` row in the provenance block, and `volsto-study list` prints each config's mode.

**Results — the one place that enforces "no Monte Carlo number without its stderr".** Long format `table, row, column, value, stderr, exact, unit, source, note, axes`: a non-exact finite value needs a finite non-negative stderr; an exact value stores stderr NaN; a non-exact NaN value (a missing number, printed `--`) has a NaN stderr; a non-exact value needs a unit (`"1"` for a ratio); `source` is `store:`, `cache:`, `artefact:` or `computed`; duplicates raise; every violation is listed at build time and again on read.

**Formatting.** The stderr is rounded to 2 significant digits when its leading digit is 1 or 2, else 1 (half-up; a carry to the next power of ten gives 2 digits, 0.096 → 0.10); the value to the same decimal place; `$v \pm s$`; units in the header; NaN → `--`; exact values to the column's significant digits. Figures: matplotlib (Agg, imported lazily), eight categorical hues in fixed order with a marker per series; `mc_errorbar` refuses a Monte Carlo point without a finite stderr; PDF/PNG written without a creation date so a re-render reproduces them.

**No calibration — enforced in one place.** `volsto.calibration.particle.calibrate_leverage`, the only function that runs the particle calibration, calls `volsto.calibration.guard.check_calibration_allowed` as its first statement (an AST test over `volsto/` pins that the particle step is used only there and by `models/lsv.py`). `volsto-study run` and `render` hold `calibration_forbidden` from the requirements check to the end of the LaTeX check: a process flag (a depth counter under a lock, covering every thread) plus `VOLSTO_FORBID_CALIBRATION`, inherited by every child process started meanwhile. However the function is reached — an alias bound at import, a dict or `functools.partial`, a thread pool, a spawned process pool, a thread still running during render — it raises `CalibrationForbiddenError` and the run exits 1. `ctx.cache` is also read-only (`allow_calibrate` forced false, writers raise, hits recorded in `cache_keys`, a miss reported as a missing requirement). An earlier version rebound the function by name; the verifier found four routes around it (and it had stopped refusing from the second run in a process — one test run calibrated a 5000-particle, 0.1y leverage into a pytest temporary directory before that was caught).

*Did this study calibrate.* Each run passes a temporary marker directory to the guard (`VOLSTO_CALIBRATION_MARKERS`, inherited); every call in the process or a child leaves a `refused` or `started` marker (pid, host, time). `recalibrated` is true only when a calibration started — possible only in a child whose environment dropped the prohibition — and the run then fails, listing `calibrations_started`; a refusal, even one the study caught, also fails it. *Did the cache change.* The configured cache root is snapshotted before the requirements, after compute, after render and after the LaTeX check; new or rewritten entries and added manifest rows are recorded as `cache_changed_during_run`, warned about and marked not attributed (the cache records no writer identity, and a concurrent precompute or study-C refit may legitimately be writing) — this does not fail the run. *Residual:* a thread or process that calibrates or writes after the run has ended, or into another cache root, is neither refused nor observed.

**Requirements.** `point` requirements need a configured grid (`grid: null` with one is a config error); `leverage` and `artefact` requirements do not. `run` checks them all before computing and on any miss prints the commands and exits 2 without creating the output directory. The point and leverage ids of the configured grid become one runnable line, `volsto-precompute --grid G --store S --cache C --only <ids> --resume`, every path relative to the current directory (absolute outside it) — a test executes it with `--dry-run`. `#` note lines cover an id outside the grid (add it to the grid first), a store whose run records name another grid (the configured grid is kept), and a missing leverage with no grid. Artefacts print their module's command. A miss met inside compute exits 2 the same way. Runs are written into a staging directory next to the target and moved into place, so a failed run leaves nothing behind.

**LaTeX check.** Tectonic compiles `study.tex` with its logs kept (on by default for `run` and `render`); the check fails (exit 1, reason in the manifest) when the engine fails, the PDF is missing, or the log shows a TeX error, a missing character, a float too large for the page, an undefined reference or an overfull box above 3 pt. Every table goes through `latex.split_table` (row groups, then parts of at most 25 rows) and is scaled to the page width; common symbols are mapped; narrative math is written `\(...\)` and `$` is literal; a code block containing `\end{verbatim}` is refused; numbers beyond exponent ±6 print as `(v ± s)×10^n` and no finite value makes the formatter raise. `--no-latex-check` removes a stale `study.pdf` and says so; a missing engine skips the check with the install line (`brew install tectonic`; the first compile on a machine downloads the TeX bundle).

**Strict inputs.** Duplicate YAML keys are errors; `1e-3` and `2e4` are floats; only `true`/`false` are booleans; an empty `--set` value and `nan`/`inf` are rejected. `diff_results` also reports a changed unit or an exact↔Monte Carlo switch (`changed`, with the reason), uses the 1e-12 relative tolerance for a row with zero stderr on both sides, and requires a finite positive `nse`; usage errors exit 1, so 2 means only "missing requirements".

**Rerun and render.** `rerun <dir>` executes `manifest["config"]` (never the YAML on disk) into `<dir>/rerun/<utc>` (path flags may relocate the roots) and writes `diff.csv`/`diff.md`: a Monte Carlo number moved when `|Δ| > 2·max(se_old, se_new)` (the same seeds make the two estimates correlated, so the errors are not combined in quadrature); an exact number when it changed by more than 1e-12 relative; exit 0 when nothing changed, 1 when a number moved, was added or was removed, 2 on a missing requirement. `render <dir>` rebuilds the tables, figures, `study.md` and `study.tex` from `results.parquet` and the stored manifest, byte-identically (tested).

**Tests** (`tests/test_study_runner.py`, 57 after the verification rounds): run → files and manifest; rerun with the YAML changed on disk → nothing moved (Δ = 0); a stored value shifted by 3 stderr → moved, exit 1; a missing point / leverage / artefact → exact commands, exit 2, compute never called; the read-only cache and the process-wide guard (entered repeatedly, nested); render byte-identical; formatting cases; Results invariants; diff statuses; Tectonic compile; import without matplotlib; the console script; the toy store's points and leverages found present and made-up ids missing. The example study (Black–Scholes calls, 2·10⁴ paths) gives 21.65 ± 0.05 / 8.36 ± 0.07 / 2.32 ± 0.05 against the closed form 21.6458 / 8.34941 / 2.31736.

### 10.2 M10 Part 2 — study catalogue (as built, 2026-09-16)

Seven catalogue studies, each a runner module `volsto/studies/catalogue/sN_*.py` plus YAML configs `configs/studies/catalogue/sN.yaml` and `sN_fast.yaml` (shared helpers in `_common.py`). Each `study.md` opens with its question in one sentence. Every Monte Carlo number carries its stderr; a value recorded upstream without one is shown as missing with a note, and counts, flags, inputs, fitted parameters, closed forms, and ranks that carry a decided-at-2-se flag are exact (`EXACT_KINDS`); z-scores carry the nominal stderr 1. Tables and figures are functions of `results.parquet` only (render byte-identical, rerun moves nothing — tested). The model helpers select models from a grid (`models: {surface, lv, one_factor: {nu, rho, kappa}, two_factor}`, every key required), label rows `LV (ω=0)` / `1F ω=… ρ=… κ=…` / `2F <preset>`, and load models without calibrating (the LV is a Dupire build, `source = computed`; an LSV is read through `ctx.leverage`, `cache:<key>`; store reads carry `store:<id>`). A walking test runs every `*_fast.yaml` with the LaTeX check and asserts `recalibrated: false` and an unchanged toy cache (manifest rows, file sizes and mtimes). The fast configs run on `toy_build` and `toy_marking_build` (`configs/grids/toy_marking.yaml`: 4 marking fits on the placeholder surface, 2·10⁴ particles, 1y — every one binding at the ν cap 3.5 with ρ_SX1 ≈ −1 and ρ12 ≈ 0.97–1.0, as the placeholder is known to do), both built in `tests/conftest.py`; the sanctioned calibrating sites of the test suite are `toy_build` and `toy_marking_build` (`tests/conftest.py`) and `toy_backtest_build` (`tests/_backtest_build.py`, M10 Part 3). No number derived from Monte Carlo values is exact. Each catalogue study declares every exact row (`EXACT_KINDS` in its module) as a count, a flag, a fitted parameter, a closed form, an input, or a rank; a rank of Monte Carlo point estimates may be exact only if its declaration names a flag column in the same table (itself declared a flag, values 0/1) saying whether its ordering against the next-ranked row is decided at 2 stderr. The fast-config walking test enforces the declarations (`_common.py`), and fails a study without a declaration on any exact row named like a z-score, an error, a rank or a median. z-scores carry the nominal stderr 1.

**Paired errors.** Every cross-model difference a study prices itself (S2, S3, and S4 when both models are priced there) is paired: both models use the same seed and draws addressed by (path, step, Brownian), and the stderr is that of the per-pair difference of the two estimates' influence functions (`_common.py`: `Estimate`, `paired_difference`, `price_paired`; delta method for ratios). The local vol is stepped on the reference LSV's grid, its leverage-slice times added (886 steps to 1y against the 756 it would use alone — which shifts the LV prices by ≈ 2σ over 400 seeds and is why the LV store-check rows are not exactly zero; studies say so); grids that still differ stay paired by path, since the draws are independent across the path index. Checked against a 400-seed brute force (5000 paths each, LV against the cached 1F ω = 2 LSV): reported se / empirical sd 0.98–1.05 for the variance, conditional, KO and VKO statistics and the conditional probability (±3.5 % precision), also across different grids. The quadrature error and the per-pair correlation are recorded beside each paired stderr and the narrative compares the two from the run's own numbers (S3's 4·10⁵ headline run: quadrature 0.97–4.06 times the paired stderr, median 1.40, larger in 35 of 36 cells, smaller in one where the estimates are negatively correlated, −0.11). A difference of two *stored* estimates (S1, S4 when read from the store) carries the quadrature error, labelled "not the exact error": the estimates share random numbers, the store keeps no per-path samples, and quadrature bounds the exact error only when their correlation is non-negative, which no study checks at run time. The forward 90/110 skew of stored smiles (S4, S5) carries the sum of the two strikes' errors instead — a bound whatever the sign of the correlation (the two strikes' estimates correlate at −0.05 to −0.13 under the LSVs). A store cross-check that finds bitwise-equal numbers reports an exact zero, "identical (same computation)". The first version called these path sets independent and combined errors in quadrature everywhere; the catalogue verifier refuted that.

**S1 — forward vol and cliquets vs vol-of-vol.** Reads the store's M4 cells, forward smiles and forward vols; a point missing from the store but with a cached leverage is priced with the precompute's own functions (`price_missing`; `--set price_missing=false` gives a store-only run). Reports the headline, forward vols per window, the per-strike LSV − LV forward smile with the quadrature error (not the exact error) and the corresponding z, the cliquet ladder — read as the study cliquet at its fixed 2 % cap across the models (the owner's "capped-cliquet ladder" is not defined further; interpretation stated in study.md, pending the owner) — and the regeneration of `tests/test_m4_regression.py` read as data. **Regeneration on `outputs/store` (`configs/grids/placeholder_cached.yaml`, 2026-09-16): 80/80 keys within the regression tolerance, max |d|/se 0.0075 — the baseline file's six-digit rounding — with particles (8·10⁵), paths (4·10⁵) and seed (2024) identical on all five models: the store and the baseline are the same computation**; 2.5 s, nothing priced. The put-wing verdict is stated per window and put strike from the wing rows: which headline models lie within 2 quadrature errors of the local vol. On the placeholder store, k = 0.8 holds in 1y→2y except 2F (+0.22 ± 0.08 vp, z +2.9), and in 2y→3y except ω=3 (−0.38 ± 0.08, z −4.8) and ω=1 (+0.16 ± 0.07, z +2.2); k = 0.9 and 0.95 are not invariant for any model (up to 1.28 / 2.05 vp in 1y→2y, 2.11 / 3.12 vp in 2y→3y). Each z is recorded with the nominal stderr 1. The largest wing |LSV − LV| is 0.22 ± 0.08 vp (1y→2y, 2F, k = 0.8) and 0.38 ± 0.08 vp (2y→3y, ω=3), against the smallest at-the-money difference 0.67 ± 0.07 and 0.92 ± 0.07 vp. Every number whose product ends beyond the calibration horizon says so in its note, in every table (forward windows by the store's flag; the 1y→2y headline and regression cells by that window's flag; cliquets by maturity against the point's horizon), and study.md counts the flagged rows. The study archive is absent: the placeholder baseline stands (study.md says so). `s1_grid.yaml` covers the default grid's 1F sweep and exits 2 with the precompute line until the VM run lands.

**S2 — the VKO put.** Prices the 12m VKO for every model from its cached leverage (the store holds neither the ITM distribution nor its errors): the ratio sweep over vol_ko ∈ {20, 25, 30, 35, 40} %, prices, P(KO), the realised-vol percentiles conditional on S_T < K with a paired pair bootstrap (the same resampling for every model), P(σ_real > h | ITM). Two readings of the sign of the ratio difference are scored per cell where resolved: ΔP(σ_real > h | ITM) (§6.2's reading since 2026-09-17, right in 13 of 14) and the superseded width and median position (6 of 11). The store cross-check reports identical numbers as exact zeros. The 2022 H2 case (repaired surface history) reports the realised vol to date, the budget used, the knock-out state, the largest vol that keeps the put alive and the start-date marks; the payoff is not observable in the window. Full run ≈ 113 s. Every paired difference's stderr, quadrature error and per-pair correlation is recorded with its own delta-method stderr (0.97–0.99 of the replicate spread over 200 replicates); store-check |d|/se values are z-scores with the nominal stderr 1.

**S3 — conditional and knock-out variance.** On one path set per model: the variance swap, up/down/corridor variance at B ∈ {90, 100, 110} %, the convexity spread (joint delta method), the KO swap at {105, 110, 120} % (strike, P(KO), E[τ/N], K_KO − K_var paired). **Gyöngy, stated precisely:** with continuous monitoring every leg weighted by a function of (t, S_t) — up, down, corridor, the variance swap — is pinned exactly to the local-vol value; with daily fixings the previous-close legs are pinned only up to O(Δt) plus the leverage calibration residual, and the current-close legs add a model-dependent O(Δt) term (the one-step skewness is O(√Δt), so the boundary-layer term is O(Δt), not O(√Δt); its size depends on the one-step kurtosis at the barrier, which the marginals do not fix — measured: halving Δt multiplies it by 0.49–0.52, all six ratios excluding 0.71; LSV − LV +2.62 ± 0.10 vp² at 252 fixings a year); the KO swap is never pinned. The verdict is computed per cell (model × leg × barrier): a paired z and a tolerance equal to the model's own variance-swap residual floor (|δK_var²| + 2 se, converted to the leg's strike — a heuristic, not a bound). On the five headline models (`s3_headline.yaml`, 4·10⁵ paths, 162 s): floors from the variance-swap residuals +0.026 ± 0.009 / −0.032 ± 0.016 / −0.100 ± 0.021 / −0.019 ± 0.014 vp (ω = 1 / 2 / 3 / 2F); 0 of 36 cells beyond the floor, 25 significant but within it (18 of the 24 previous-close cells, all positive, largest 0.065 vp): at daily fixings and this calibration the LSV does not reproduce the LV's conditional strikes exactly, every difference sits within its model's floor, and no invariance is claimed below it (the first version declared the zero line in fixed text while its own run had 17/24 cells beyond 2 se). The KO LSV − LV strike is swept against ν, ρ and θ on `configs/grids/s3_ko_var.yaml` (the default grid's 1F specs at κ 1.5 plus Table 8.2 at θ ∈ {0.1, 0.245, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8}); the full config exits 2 until that grid is precomputed (`volsto-precompute --grid configs/grids/s3_ko_var.yaml --dry-run`: 4.46 h at 2 threads, 4.04 h at 12, for the 31 missing leverages). study.md records: "K_KO > K_var holds under negative skew and a non-inverted term structure but is not a theorem." z carries the nominal stderr 1; the floor |δK_var²| + 2 se and the leg tolerance are Monte Carlo numbers with delta-method stderrs; the verdict is an exact flag at the stated thresholds; quadrature errors and correlations carry their own stderrs.

**S4 — autocall and Phoenix model risk.** Reads the store's M6 cells (stored as fractions under a "% notional" label — converted and noted) or prices them on the shared schedule (paired LSV − LV by leg): price, expected life, P(KI), P(breach), the autocall probabilities, LSV − LV by leg, the forward 90/110 skew per window over the spot 1y skew. Notes and forward windows beyond a leverage's calibration horizon are disclosed per number, in a `horizon` table and at the top of study.md (the store's `beyond_horizon` flag is honoured; the LV's Dupire grid runs to 3y and is never flagged). The forward-skew exposure per leg is computed **under local vol** (the bumped surface rebuilt by Dupire, CRN; exposures that are zero on every path are exact): for an LSV it needs a leverage recalibrated on every bumped surface, which the cache does not hold — holding the cached leverage only moves ξ₀ — so the LSV `skew_T` is read from the store's risk tier and is missing until the store is refreshed with `--risk light` (≈ 0.8–1.6 h per LSV point). The store's `skew_T` tents sit on the grid's risk pillars (0.25, 1, 3y) and the LV exposure's on the observation dates (1, 2, 3y); a tent spans the interval between its neighbouring pillars, so the two are not like-for-like, and study.md says so with the pillars read from the results. Every number of a model whose notes run past its leverage's horizon, and of a forward window ending past it, says so in its note. Full run ≈ 4 min. Every paired difference's stderr, quadrature error and per-pair correlation is recorded with its own delta-method stderr (0.97–0.99 of the replicate spread over 200 replicates); store-check |d|/se values are z-scores with the nominal stderr 1.

**S5 — the marking dials.** The binding map is fitted inline (through `volsto.viewers.grid.marking_fit`, the helper `resolve_marking` uses: `fit_2f_marking` without stage 3 — a parameter fit of seconds, not a leverage calibration; stated in study.md): status, parameters, the gap and binding edge at T_s / T_l, the ν-box, ν-cap and ρ12 flags, the largest first-order SpotVolCovar miss and, at grid points, the difference to the stored fit (all exact). The store's marking points `marking:<surface>:ssr<s>:eps<e>` supply mean |L − 1| (an exact functional of the cached leverage — its particle-seed noise is not estimated upstream — drawn without error bars), the realised LSV SSR ± se beside the first-order SSR, the forward 90/110 skew of the stored forward smiles, σ(0.9) − σ(1.1), with stderr the sum of the two strikes' errors (a bound valid for any correlation between them, the S4 convention; the store keeps no per-path samples), against the snapshot's exact spot skew, and their ratio (se `fs_se/|spot|`), product prices (M6 fractions ×100), and the light-tier risk of the two risk products when present (fractions of notional ×100, unit `% notional <store unit>`; an unknown Greek name is a config error; a missing risk table prints the `--risk light` line). The cost of a mark is its price minus the price at the reference mark (1.0, 0.10), stderrs in quadrature labelled `QUADRATURE_NOTE` (not the exact error; a bound only if the unmeasured same-seed correlation is non-negative); its z-score carries stderr 1. The forward 90/110 skew of each window is shown beside the snapshot's spot skew at the window's length, both keyed by the window (`spot_skew@<t1>y-into-<len>y`), so windows of equal length never collide (the shipped windows 1y→2y and 2y→3y crashed the first version with a duplicate row). The recorded M7 fits are shown as recorded. Today the store holds none of the default grid's 36 marks, so the full run exits 2 with the `volsto-precompute --grid configs/grids/default.yaml … --only <36 ids> --resume` line (accepted by a dry run). Fast mode: the two inline fits reproduce the stored fits exactly; ≈ 17 s. The SPX 2022-12-30 surface used by the default grid, `studies/m8b.SPX_SNAPSHOT` and S5–S7 is the committed plain-SSVI snapshot `hdn_2022H2_ssvi/spx_2022-12-30.yaml`, not the repaired eSSVI one (that day needed the calendar repair; its 2y / 3y skew is −0.2181 / −0.1910 under SSVI against −0.1940 / −0.1660 repaired, §13.1) — switching is the owner's decision.

**S6 — shadow rotation.** Artefacts only: the M7 greek (`m7/p1_marking_shadow_rotation.csv`), the static greek files (`m8b/C/static_*.json` — the same computation as the M7 CSV, identical to 1e-14, not an independent run) and table C (read through the API, so new twins and old stems give the same numbers). Ratio ± delta-method se, z and nonlinearity ± se are recomputed with the M8b functions; the refit flags are carried; the table's own state (modification time, missing rows, rows not ok, task results newer than the table or absent from it) is part of the results. Every ratio is classed at 2 se against 1 ± 0.30, separately at each rota (above: ratio − 2 se > 1.3; below: ratio + 2 se < 0.7 — a smaller magnitude or the opposite sign; within: both bounds inside [0.7, 1.3]; else undecided). A policy column enters the claim only when clean (every row ok, no refit at a correlation bound). The claim that the static greek does not overstate the simulated re-marking P&L is made only when every decided product's ratio minus 2 se is at least 1; otherwise it is stated class by class. A simulated P&L is called a desk gain or loss only when |z| > 2; the others are listed as sign-undecided. The claim says when the table predates its task results. For each policy column S6 also states the refits and how many took the guarded fallback or had their correlation target capped: on a fallback refit `sabr_linked` holds the base fit's correlation target, which `sticky_breakeven` always holds, so on that share of refits the two policies differ only through their break-even vol targets. Beside the static greek's policy ordering (sticky / sabr of the static greek) S6 shows the simulated ordering (sticky / sabr of the re-marking P&L) at each rota, each side decided at 2 se, and lists where the two disagree. **On the re-run's table (2026-09-16, 23:20 UTC; every row has its task result):** both columns clean (0 refits at bound); 83 refits per column, 63 fallbacks in each (76 %), 6 capped under `sabr_linked` and 0 under `sticky_breakeven`; at +1 `sabr_linked` 4 above, 1 within and `sticky_breakeven` 3 above, 2 undecided (Phoenix, KO var), so on every decided product |simulated| ≥ |static| at 2 se under both policies; the ordering sides are decided on 8 of 15 product-rota pairs and disagree on 4 — the KO var at every rota (simulated 0.72 ± 0.03, 0.59 ± 0.03, 0.77 ± 0.06 against static 1.28 ± 0.07) and the VKO put at +3 (simulated 0.69 ± 0.10, same sign, against static −1.45 ± 0.40, opposite signs). Before the re-run (the 16:02 UTC table) `sabr_linked` was excluded (15/15 rows contaminated) and `sticky_breakeven` read 3 above, 1 within, 1 undecided at +1. The claim is redrawn automatically from the re-run's table. The simulated/static ratio carries M8b's independent-errors delta-method stderr, labelled not a bound; ratios of runs that share random numbers — the nonlinearity and both policy orderings — carry `m8b.ratio_stderr_bound`, a first-order bound valid for any error correlation (at most √2 times the independent figure). z-scores carry stderr 1; class codes, counts and flags are exact given the table's values. The M7 CSV and study C's static greeks are the same computation (identical numbers).

**S7 — hedging.** Artefacts only: tables A, B and D and the discriminator verdict. The model-mismatch reserve is the leakage minus the same-world leakage — pure LV: −1.99 ± 0.17, −1.87 ± 0.13, +2.05 ± 0.28, −21.1 ± 0.4 % of notional (autocall, Phoenix, cliquet, VKO) and −0.31 ± 0.12 vol points (KO var); ν × 1.5: within 2.3 se of zero except the cliquet, +0.069 ± 0.013. The historical world is carried as skipped with the verdict's reason. The study-D headline is quoted verbatim and checked against the table: model minus the common minimum-variance delta −0.0563 ± 0.0027 on the autocall and +0.1131 ± 0.0012 on the vanilla; the spread of the regimes' implied MV deltas 0.009 ± 0.004 and 0.0035 ± 0.0018. The world-(ii) gate and its SSR table come from the repaired eSSVI history's discriminator run (`outputs/essvi_gate/discriminator/`); study.md names that history; the M8b gate's pre-repair plain-SSVI verdict is only a documented fallback (the same verdict: "surface artefact", raw 1m 0.942 + 2 × 0.153). The spread of the implied minimum-variance deltas carries the sum of the two extreme rows' stderrs (a bound for any correlation) and is shown over its noise floor d_k × the mean row stderr as a Monte Carlo ratio (autocall 2.0 ± 1.0, vanilla 1.9 ± 1.0: both within about one stderr of pure noise). S7's ranks — the strategy std rank of table A, the std and distance-to-common-MV ranks of table D and the model's distance rank — are exact given the tables' values, and each carries a companion flag (`<rank>_decided`: 1/0, NaN for the last or an unranked row) saying whether its ordering against the next-ranked row is decided at 2 stderr (the two value ± 2 se intervals do not overlap, valid whatever the correlation); `headline_decided` is 1 when every one of the headline-sized closest regimes is ahead of every other ranked regime at 2 stderr (the largest |distance| + 2 se inside the set below the smallest |distance| − 2 se outside it), so the closest set is decided whatever the order within it. On the 2026-09-16 tables every table-A rank is decided; for the autocall the closest set {sticky_skew, sticky_strike} is decided but not its internal order, and the model's rank 3 of 4 is not decided; for the vanilla the closest set is decided and the model is the farthest. z-scores carry stderr 1.

### 10.3 M10 Part 3 — rolling backtest (as built, 2026-09-16)

`volsto/studies/backtest.py`, console script `volsto-backtest`, `configs/backtest/hdn_2022h2*.yaml`, `configs/studies/catalogue/backtest_2022h2*.yaml`, `tests/test_backtest.py` (fixture `toy_backtest_build`).

**Two stages.** `volsto-backtest run <config> [--shard i/n] [--resume] [--no-calibrate] [--limit N] [--only-dates …] [--force] [--out/--cache/--snapshots]` is the only M10 path that calibrates. Per trading date it (1) imports the surface (eSSVI with the calendar repair) and fits the P1 marking model at (ssr_target, skew_eps) with stage 3 off; (2) gets the date's leverage through `LeverageCache.get_or_calibrate`; (3) prices the book on one engine based on the previous date's state; (4) attributes the P&L of (d−1, d]; (5) publishes the date's outcome (`rows.parquet`, `fit.json`, `done.json`) as an immutable attempt `dates/<d>/attempts/<id>/` and points `dates/<d>/CURRENT` at it (Integrity, below). With `VOLSTO_BACKTEST_REQUIRE_PATHS=1` in the environment every `volsto-backtest` command that lacks `--out`, `--cache` or `--snapshots` (or gives a blank one) is refused (exit 2) before anything is read or written — set it for agents and scripted runs, so the config's `paths.*` are never used by accident. `dry-run` prints the projection and writes nothing but snapshots and `probe.json` (the latter only into a store of this config); `status` lists the dates. The study `configs/studies/catalogue/backtest_2022h2.yaml` (runner `volsto.studies.backtest`) renders from that store and never calibrates; a missing date is an artefact requirement whose command is the `volsto-backtest run … --only-dates … --resume` line. Under `--no-calibrate` the job runs inside `calibration_forbidden()` and a missing leverage exits 2 with that line.

**Config** (strict YAML: every key required, unknown or duplicate keys are errors; schema in the module docstring): `name`, `data {vendor, root, underlying, missing_close}`, `dates {start, end}`, `surface {essvi, calendar_repair}`, `marking {ssr_target, skew_eps, stage3}` (stage 3 on is refused), `calibration {base_spec, n_particles, horizon}`, `pricing {n_paths, chunk_size, seed}` (one seed on every date), `book {fixed, rolling {every, mark, products}}`, `attribution {mode, detail, trades, pillars, bump, max_halvings}` (mode `sticky_leverage` only), `ssr {window, pillars}`, `stability {window_vol, window_ssr, share, band, fit}` (`fit` — the historical-mode fit configuration: pillars, mat_min, k2, skew_mode, skew_eps, nu_cap — required), `paths {out, cache, snapshots}`. Its hash covers the base spec's *content* and the resolved marking and stability fit configurations, and leaves out the paths, the data root and the end date, so a moved store or an extended range keeps the dates already computed; `run` refuses a store built under another hash unless `--force`. The multi-year run changes `dates.start`, `dates.end` and `data.root`, and needs a new store (`paths.out` / `paths.snapshots`, or `--out` / `--snapshots`): `dates.start` is hashed, so `run` refuses the old store.

**Integrity — immutable attempts, one pointer, one reader.** Each date's outcomes are immutable, content-addressed attempts (`dates/<d>/attempts/<status>-<sha256 of the listing>/`: `done.json`, and for results and skips `rows.parquet` and `fit.json`), staged and published by one rename and never modified; `run` never deletes one. One pointer, `dates/<d>/CURRENT` (version 1, attempt id, status, every file's SHA-256), is replaced by one `os.replace` of a synced temporary. Every mutation goes through the `flock`ed descriptor of the date directory itself (`dir_fd` renames, replaces and removals; there is no lock file: a process that swaps the path locks another inode, so two holders never mutate one directory), and migrations run under a `flock` on the store root. A `CURRENT` of another pointer version is a refusal for every mutating command, checked before anything is published; a commit fails if the date directory was moved while it was locked (`DateLock.still_at` — this narrows the window, it does not close it: moving a live store is unsupported). Attempt checks ignore exactly the regular files `.DS_Store` and `._<held name>`. A date is *done* iff one read of `CURRENT` and of every file it names verifies, and `dependency_record`, computed from those same bytes, reproduces the stored record: the config hash (the base spec's content and the resolved fit configurations); the inputs digest (the chain over every calendar date's day file and vendor-manifest entry up to the date); the calibration code tag; the digest of the snapshot bytes the run parsed; the rows and fit checksums with the row count; the leverage key, its completeness and (record version 2) the digest of its numeric arrays — the bytes pricing loaded; the previous date and whether its leverage was used; the state links (snapshot, parameters, key, leverage content) of every date whose state the rows use; the digests of the closes they use; for a skip, its re-derived cause. Version-1 records (stores written before 2026-09-17) are judged at their version: a date whose results carry a version-1 record is `done` with `legacy_unverified` = "leverage content not verified (record version 1)" — its own leverage is checked by key and completeness only — and `status`, study.md and the stage-2 manifest (`backtest_legacy_unverified`: count and dates) name such dates; recomputing a date records version 2 (re-recording recomputes its rows and standard errors under the current code; a leverage taken from the cache records no calibration), and a version-2 date that uses such a date's state checks that leverage's numbers. Once a date has an attempt at the current record version, the pointer rule never makes an older-version attempt current again, so adoption and `gc` never roll a version-2 result back. Verdicts chain (a changed input makes its date and every later date stale; recomputing a date into another state makes every date that used its state or its close stale); a date whose dependency is not stored yet is *pending*; a consistent read that cannot be had after the retries is *unsettled* and moves nothing. One pointer rule (`choose_pointer`), applied under the date lock from a fresh read, ranks the attempts of this config — done > pending ok > incomplete > pending incomplete > stale results > no results — so a failure or a skip never displaces results, and a done date moves only to another done attempt; a "later attempt failed" note is built from verified bytes and never changes a classification. `run` adopts a published attempt that protects more (what a writer killed between publishing and pointing leaves behind); `volsto-backtest gc` refuses another config's store and, under every date lock, removes non-current attempts only of dates whose full verdict is done (never a date's last results; symbolic links skipped) and only this host's dead staging directories. Staging ownership is the host and pid in the directory name, liveness `os.kill(pid, 0)`: a dead writer's pid reused by another live process keeps its staging directory until a later `gc` (a known limit, seen once under a saturated machine on 2026-09-22; a lock-held token would close it). **All refusals come before any write** (the migration refusal included: `run` checks it with its other refusals, before it checks writability or writes the header; the header is written after the migration, `--force` binding it first; without a header every stored date is judged — calendar dates by their verdict, the others by the config hash they record — and refusals and verdict reasons name the config that computed the outcome, or that started an interrupted migration) (header binding, migration, skips, adoption, pointer moves), are evaluated on flat files too, and print runnable commands: a store header of another config and results computed under another config — one judgement, `computed_under_another_config`, behind the `--force` refusal and stage 2's commands — and, under `--no-calibrate`, a missing leverage of a date to compute or of a previous date whose results or next-date P&L need it. `dry-run` writes `probe.json` only into a store whose header is this config's, and none when `run` would refuse. A blank path override is refused. Snapshots are reused only while their import record (`<snapshot>.import.json`) binds their content digest to the current day-file and manifest digests. Since 2026-09-22 a snapshot is also bound to the importer that wrote it: its `provenance.importer_tag` must equal `volsto.market.import_hdn.IMPORTER_TAG` (bumped whenever a change moves any snapshot; guarded by a source hash like the calibration code tag), and a date whose intact snapshot carries another tag is `stale` — "its snapshot was imported by importer … (current …)" — so `run --resume` re-imports it and recomputes the date and its successors. The third occurrence of a stored artefact outliving its code (the calibration code tag, the golden legacy store, this): the 25 proof-of-concept dates read `done` under the 2026-09-22 importer until this check. Stores in the earlier flat layout are migrated in place under their own config only (a headerless store whose flat dates another config computed is refused, and so is an interrupted migration another config started — the journal records the config hash; an unreadable `migrations.json` is kept byte for byte and never overwritten) — journalled (`migrations.json`: an `in_progress` entry, per-date reports, resumable snapshot binding), through the pointer rule only (a torn `done.json` becomes an attempt that is never made current), binding a snapshot only when a fresh vendor import reproduces it, quarantining files no attempt holds; a non-writable flat store is a clear error before any lock; stage 2 only reads and prints the `migrate` command. The flat-layout code and this code must never write the same store. The code version that wrote an attempt is recorded, not judged. A walking test mutates every recorded input; a crash matrix injects an exception, an interrupt and a kill at every step of the commit, pointer and migration paths (plus real process kills), with concurrent writers and `gc`; the verifier's systematic interleavings (408) and a 90-s stress (two writers, `gc`, adoption and readers on dependent dates) found no violation after the fifth pass, and the sixth pass's cases are regression tests. **History of this class (six passes):** the first version committed dates whose previous date was missing as `ok` with NaN P&L and hashed the base spec's path; the second left a date done after its predecessor was recomputed, reused an edited snapshot, ignored a dropped row, and let a wrong `--cache` empty a date's rows; the third let the `--no-calibrate` refusal skip dates without stored results, did not roll back an interrupt between two commit renames, and still reused an edited snapshot — replaced by the immutable-attempt design; the fourth found `gc` and read races (a verdict re-reading files after verifying them; `gc` deleting results whenever the pointer verified; no config check); the fifth found refusals after header binding and migration, an unjournalled migration, a transient import error turned into a permanent skip, an unverified note changing stage 2's commands, a newer pointer version overwritten, and the manifest pinning a different pointer than the one read; the sixth found a commit landing in a date directory replaced while it was locked, a headerless store and an interrupted migration adopted under another config, a pointer published before the foreign-pointer check, `gc` crashing on a read-only store, an unreadable journal overwritten, and a resume adopting a version-1 attempt over a version-2 one.

**Printed commands — one builder.** Every `volsto-backtest` command the code prints — refusals, `status` hints, verdict hints, stage-2 requirements, the command stored with a failed date — is built by `backtest_argv` and rendered shell-quoted by `command_line`, and carries explicit `--out`, `--cache` and `--snapshots`, so it runs as printed after a relocation, from paths with spaces and under `VOLSTO_BACKTEST_REQUIRE_PATHS=1`; a "write elsewhere" line names its store `NEW_STORE` with the snapshots inside it, so following it never touches the refused store. A walking test records every printed line on stores whose paths contain spaces, with the guard set, parses each one and executes it. (The third occurrence of printed commands that did not work as printed — no command, a placeholder with a space, a missing `--force`, unquoted paths, and an "elsewhere" line re-importing into the refused store's snapshots — closed by this invariant.)

**Missing closes.** `data.missing_close` is required. The calendar is the vendor's day files plus the dates its manifest lists as missing (a day neither present nor listed stays invisible: there is no exchange calendar to check against). `fail`: the bad date fails with a message naming it and the fix; trades whose history spans it are `unpriced` (their dates `incomplete`), trades struck after it proceed. `skip_date`: only a manifest-listed date whose day file is absent is skipped (a pure check before any write; the cause is recorded and re-derived by the verdict — an import that raises is a date failure retried by `--resume`, never a permanent gap); the date is dropped before the shard blocks are cut, so the 252-day fixing grid runs over the remaining vendor dates — later fixings of a spanning trade land one exchange day late and the return across the gap counts as one daily return; rows carry a `gaps` column and study.md states it. The PoC configs use `fail`.

**Calendar, book, shards.** The calendar is the vendor's day files; the k-th date after inception is trading index k (§6.10), and the realised close is the snapshot's spot. Fixed book: autocall 3y and Phoenix 3y (the M6 headline terms — equal to `headline_products` at 3y / 3 observations, tested; coupons are per annum, c·T_i for the growing autocall coupon and c·ΔT_i per Phoenix period, so shorter variants stay on spec), cliquet 1y, 12m VKO put (vol_ko 30 %), 1y KO variance swap (barrier 110 %) and 1y variance swap — the two variance products struck at the inception surface's log-contract strike with variance notional 1/(2 K_vol) (vega notional 1, values in vol points × 100; M8b used strike 0 in variance units — stated in the configs and in study.md) — all struck on the first date; the rolling book strikes the same trades on the first date of each month. Trades are seasoned from the fresh product with the as-of date's discount curve. `--only-dates` takes `YYYY-MM-DD` dates and `A..B` ranges (both ends included). `--shard i/n` takes the i-th of n contiguous blocks of that selection (the calendar without one), cut before `--resume` and `--limit`, so a shard names the same dates on every run and the union of the shards is the unsharded selection; blocks are contiguous because date d's attribution needs d−1's leverage (one duplicate calibration per block boundary). The previous date's state is refit from its stored snapshot (deterministic — identical parameters and keys, tested).

**Configurations.** `configs/backtest/hdn_2022h2.yaml` is the cheapest configuration that delivers every output the owner listed: ladders on the five fitted pillars (3m, 6m, 1y, 2y, 3y), the rolling book priced on its inception dates only (the fixed book carries the mark-to-market drift), 2·10⁴ paths with one seed, `max_halvings: 6` (§13.2). `hdn_2022h2_full.yaml` adds the risk engine's nine ladder pillars and daily marks of the rolling book.

**Model builds and the ξ₀ memo.** `build_market` reuses the ξ₀ strip through `XI0_MEMO`, a process-local LRU of 256 curves keyed by the SHA-256 of the market, surface, perturbation and strip horizon — the only inputs of the strip. It is exact (memo on equals memo off bit for bit on 59 real ladder-date specs; cache keys unchanged; the toy backtest's rows identical in all 128 non-timing columns); parameter and particle bumps share their state's strip, and a date's valuation state is a hit when the next date revisits it. Measured 2026-09-16 on a ladders date with 9 pillars: 59 specs, 48 strips, 126 s cold with the memo against 146 s without, 0.04 s warm; toy per attributed date 54 s → 31 s. The memo lives in each process (each shard warms its own); callers that build ξ₀ outside `build_market` (the marking fit, the ladders' bucket strip, the hedger) are not memoised. The attribution engines `FrozenLeverageEngine`, `DryRunEngine` and `DryRunBuilder` are public in `volsto.risk.attribution` and exported from `volsto.risk` (a stray `"["` entry in `volsto.risk.__all__`, present since before M10, made `from volsto.risk import *` raise; removed, tested).

**Attribution.** `explain(mode="sticky_leverage")` with dt = 1/252, `product_1` = the trade seasoned to d, `product_theta` = the trade seasoned with d's close held at d−1's value; the P&L is V(d) − V(d−1) + the flows dated d. Buckets: delta, gamma, parallel vega or the vega-T / skew-T / curvature-T ladders, rho, repo, the parameters, decay, carry, recalibration and residual, plus cash flows and settlement; they sum to the P&L to 1e-14. A bucket's stderr is the paired stderr of the per-path sum of its items (`Explain.bucket_stderrs`: a ladder bucket's pillars share the seed; |move| × the Greek's for a single item; decay and carry use theta's own errors); the P&L's is the direct paired stderr of V(d) − V(d−1) and the residual's is paired per path (§7.12.1), both at no pricing cost. The start-of-period Greeks are stored from the engine's memo at no pricing cost (this is the backtest's risk report: a full §7.13 report is 80–100 calibrations per product per date). Trades that are not attributed get the paired seeded difference. On real consecutive SPX dates the attribution costs 26 pricings per trade (parallel) and 65 (ladders on 9 pillars), because all six free parameters and both curves move every day; the HDN implied dividend curve is noisy day to day, so the repo bucket is noisy.

**Sign.** Stored rows keep the holder's sign (V(d) − V(d−1) + flows); every stage-2 table, figure and narrative shows the **desk P&L** (the desk short the book — the M7/M8b convention), labelled in the headers and stated once in study.md and the config headers.

**Series** (stage 2, from the stored rows only). Values and P&L are shown ×100 in each trade's own unit (% of notional, % of inception spot, vol points of vega notional), in the desk sign; a book total adds only fixed trades quoted in one unit (`book (fixed, <unit>)`), and tables and figures are split by unit — no mixed-unit number is shown. Every aggregate error comes from one helper, `aggregate()`, and is labelled: a trade's cumulative P&L takes the stored direct paired error of V(d) − V(inception) + flows (`cum_pnl_stderr`, one extra pricing per attributed trade per date) when every P&L date since inception carries it; a single row's bucket group takes its stored paired group error (`grp_<group>_stderr`: spot, rates, surface, params, time, Greeks, explained); books, buckets, months, the daily book and stores written before these columns show the root sum of squares, labelled "correlated daily errors — not the error of the cumulative P&L" (and, for older stores' ladder buckets, "correlated pillar errors"). On the toy store the paired cumulative error is 0.60–0.92 of that root sum of squares across the six fixed trades. Daily explained sums are Monte Carlo values with their errors; a trade with no attributed day shows its explained cell as missing, with the reason, never 0 ± 0. The marking parameters are shown with `flag_unidentified` — the marking fit returns no standard errors by construction, so that frame cannot flag (an unflaggable parameter prints `--`) — and the §15 Part 4 flags come from the historical-mode `rolling_fit` (configured by `stability.fit`) on the stored pillar history, after a burn-in of max(`stability.window_vol`, `stability.window_ssr`) dates (both required; 60 in the shipped configs), so the fit needs that many dates plus two (62). Realised SSR over `ssr.window` (NaN with the reason before window + 1 dates) is shown against the target and the fit's first-order SSR; the VKO's marked price against its realised vol, knock-out flag and budget (its 12m payoff is not observable in the 2022 H2 window); fit statuses and cost. study.md describes the window from the data (dates, first and last close, return, realised vol — no regime adjective), says whether it contains calendar-repaired days, and states that a single short window is a proof of concept, too short for a conclusion.

**Projection** (printed before every run; `dry-run` only). A two-date probe measures import, fit and a model build, and times a pricing of every product at min(n_paths, 2·10⁴) and one fifth of that (best of two timings), extrapolated to the configured paths by the affine fit through the two sizes (slope and intercept floored at 0); a dry run of `explain` counts pricings, states and new strips; a date's builds cost states × a build on a memo hit + new strips × the strip cost; calibration is the cache manifest's median at the configured size (138 s at 8·10⁵ particles, 3y), otherwise the nearest size or the 140 s reference at 8·10⁵ / 3y scaled by particles × steps (3 s floor), and zero under `--no-calibrate` (the first version charged 140 s to every toy calibration: 15 min projected for a 3-min run); dates that cannot be marked are skipped. Shard lines cover 1, 2, 4, 8 and the requested count over the selection. **Measured 2026-09-16 (2 threads, machine shared with a Monte Carlo study)** — the owner's ~4 h estimate counted the 127 calibrations only:

| configuration | one process | 2 shards | 4 shards |
|---|---|---|---|
| first written (ladders on 9 pillars, rolling book marked daily, no memo) | 25.9 h (calibration 4.9, builds 3.7, attribution 15.5, valuations 1.7) | — | 6.7 h |
| default `hdn_2022h2.yaml`, 127 dates | 18.35 h (calibration 4.87, builds 1.85, attribution 11.51, import + fit 0.1) | 9.21 h | 4.71 h |
| default, 2022-07-01..2022-08-05 (25 dates) | 3.54 h (calibration 0.96, builds 0.36, attribution 2.19) | 1.79 h | — |
| `hdn_2022h2_full.yaml`, 127 dates | 25.15 h | — | — |

At 2000 paths (toy) the paired daily P&L error is 36 % of the average daily move for the autocall and 61 % for the Phoenix (≈ 16 % and 27 % scaled to 10⁴ paths), which is why the default keeps 2·10⁴. Toy (5 dates, 2·10⁴ particles, empty cache): 142 s projected, 163 s measured.

The cumulative P&L costs one pricing per attributed trade per date (none the day after inception), and a shard block start is charged the fit and calibration of every earlier inception of an attributed trade live in it: `hdn_2022h2.yaml` (dry-run 2026-09-17, 2 threads) +6.0 s per date (+13 min over 127 dates), 18.00 h in one process, 4 shards 4.66 h wall clock (18.28 h of process time). The table's rows predate this pricing; projections move with the probe, so run `dry-run` for the current figure.

**Tests** (`tests/test_backtest.py`, 80; the toy fixture — 5 dates across a month end, 2·10⁴ particles, 1y horizon, half-year terms, 2000 paths — builds in ≈ 165 s; the module runs in ≈ 4.5 min at `-n 2`; only an absent HDN sample skips, a failed build fails): strict config and hash rules; the book equals the M6 headline notes; contiguous shards; ladders attribution on a Black–Scholes builder (Greek × move reproduces every bucket at no extra pricing); historical-mode stability and realised SSR on a synthetic history; one calibration per date; `--shard 1/2` + `2/2` under `--no-calibrate` reproduce the unsharded rows exactly; a second `--resume` computes and writes nothing; a changed hash and a missing leverage exit 2 (with the command); a corrupt vendor file is recorded and the run goes on; the projection prints before any work; stage 2 through the runner (recalibrated false, cache and store untouched, re-render identical, a missing date exits 2 with the `volsto-backtest` line); buckets sum to the P&L; `price_0` equals the stored previous value; the inception value equals a fresh pricing bit for bit; the VKO's realised state matches closes recomputed from the snapshots; SSR and stability NaN with the reason before enough history; `--only-dates` ranges and sharded selections; the ξ₀ memo's exactness, unchanged keys and float normalisation; the `volsto.risk` exports; twin trades with equal labels; incomplete dates recomputed on resume; stale dates after a base-spec, calendar or rates change; both missing-close policies; the rates step on a non-parallel curve move of unchanged mean (the mean-shift version explains 0, the new one the step within 5 %); parameter jitter; per-annum coupons; the desk-sign presentation; the integrity walking test; the storage crash matrix and the concurrent-commit/gc test; the in-place migration of a flat-layout store (golden `tests/golden/backtest_store_r2.tar.gz`); the up-front refusals; the paired theta carry and roll-down; the shared session-build lock (`tests/_locks.py::shared_build`) waiting only for a live builder; the headerless-store and torn-journal migration refusals; the moved-directory commit refusal; the version-1 flag and the version-2 check of a version-1 leverage; the printed-command walking test; an autouse fixture that clears the guard and calibration environment variables for every test.

**Proof of concept, measured (2026-09-16 23:47 UTC – 2026-09-17 03:08 UTC; numba 12 threads, per `probe.json` and every `done.json`).** `volsto-backtest run configs/backtest/hdn_2022h2.yaml --only-dates 2022-07-01..2022-08-05`: 25 dates ok, 0 incomplete, 0 failed; 25 leverage calibrations at 8·10⁵ particles (median 143 s); pricing 8206 s (6936 pricings); wall clock 11 880 s (3.30 h) against 10 248 s projected at 12 threads (other agents shared the machine); recalibrated: yes (the date leverages, by design — stage 1 is the calibrating path). The store was written in the flat layout of the code of that evening and migrated in place (`volsto-backtest migrate`, 25 dates, 25 snapshots bound by a fresh vendor import; a copy was migrated and checked first). `configs/backtest/hdn_2022h2_poc.yaml` is the same configuration with `dates.end` = 2022-08-05 (the same config hash), and `configs/studies/catalogue/backtest_2022h2_poc.yaml` renders it (stage 2 at the M10 commit: 1698 numbers, 14 tables, 6 figures, 7 s, recalibrated: no; after the follow-up — per-unit tables, labelled aggregate errors, the version-1 flag — 2453 numbers, 22 tables, 6 figures, and study.md states that its 25 dates carry record version 1). Over the window SPX went from 3825.33 to 4145.19 (+8.36 %, realised vol 18.7 %); the marking fit is `binding` on all 25 dates and ν reaches the 3.5 cap; the realised SSR of the last window is 1.07 ± 0.13 / 0.96 ± 0.11 / 0.92 ± 0.09 at 3m / 6m / 1y against the target 1; the 12m VKO's realised vol to date is 18.68 vol pts against its 30 barrier (not knocked out; the payoff is not observable in the window); the historical-mode stability fit needs 62 dates and did not run on 25.

**Recompute under the 2026-09-22 importer (2026-09-23; owner's decision: five dates instead of the 25).** `volsto-backtest run configs/backtest/hdn_2022h2_poc.yaml --only-dates 2022-07-01..2022-07-08 --resume`: the store's importer-tag check (§10.3) read every date as stale, the five dates were re-imported and recomputed — 5 ok, 5 leverage calibrations at 8·10⁵ particles (141–145 s), pricing 356–367 s per date with attribution (294–300 pricings; the first date 150 s), wall clock 2 207 s against 1 196 s projected (the M8b chain shared the machine); record version 2, strikes and fixings at the official close, the pricing state at the option-implied spot. `status` now reads *done 5, stale 20*: 2022-07-11 "the marked state of 2022-07-01, which it depends on, changed" and every later date through it. The 20 are left to the 127-date VM run, which covers them; the stage-2 study `outputs/studies/backtest_2022h2_poc` is not re-rendered (its requirements print the run command for the stale dates) and its stored numbers above are the pre-2026-09-22 importer's. The old attempts stay on disk (immutable; `gc` lists 5 non-current).

**Open after the M10 follow-up (2026-09-17).** (1) The proof-of-concept store was written before the cumulative-P&L and paired-group columns and with record version 1 (no leverage content digest): its cumulative errors stay labelled root sum of squares until those dates are recomputed. (2) Within-date book errors across trades stay labelled root sum of squares (a paired book error needs unit-homogeneous notionals). (3) The remaining 102 of the 127 dates (2022-08-08..2022-12-30) are not computed: `volsto-backtest run configs/backtest/hdn_2022h2.yaml --resume` (≈ 15 h in one process, ≈ 4 h on 4 shards); until then the historical-mode stability flags (62 dates) are empty, and no calendar-repaired day (2022-10-21..2022-12-30) is exercised. (4) Catalogue deliverables that need precomputed data (each full config exits 2 with its command): S1 across the 1F ν grid (`s1_grid.yaml`, 87 leverages, the default-grid VM run, §9.2); S3's KO sweep against ν, ρ, θ (`volsto-precompute --grid configs/grids/s3_ko_var.yaml`, 31 leverages, 4.46 h at 2 threads); S5 (the default grid's 36 marking points); S4's LSV forward-skew exposure (`--risk light` on the LSV points, ≈ 0.8–1.6 h each). (5) The SPX 2022-12-30 snapshot used by the default grid, M8b and S5–S7 is the plain-SSVI one (owner's decision whether to switch to the repaired eSSVI).

## 11. Conventions and quality bar

- Every equation implemented has a docstring stating the formula, its source (derived / Bergomi / Gatheral / Guyon–HL), and the test that checks it.
- No silent defaults for model parameters; configs are explicit YAML, validated with clear errors.
- Numba kernels are pure functions on arrays; Python wrappers do validation.
- Standard errors everywhere; no MC number printed without one.
- Logging via `logging`, not print. A `--profile` flag on the study runner.
- Reproducibility: every artefact (cache entry, study output) records the git commit, config hash and seed.
- Particle counts (owner decision, M4b): production numbers — headline tables, regression baselines, viewer precompute — use 8·10⁵ particles, single seed; development and fast paths use 2·10⁵. Baseline files state the particle count.
- Style: black, ruff, mypy (strict on `volsto/`), 100-char lines.
- README with a 20-line quickstart that calibrates a 1F LSV on the reference surface and prices the study cliquet.
