## 13. Market data import (M3b, implemented)

`market/import_hdn.py`: importer for the HistoricalData.net EOD option-chain CSV (34 columns, one file per trading day; the free `options_sample_2022H2.zip` in `./data/hdn_sample/`, git-ignored, uses the paid format). Pipeline: `load_day` (SPX and SPXW roots for the index surface, both quotes required, AM/PM settlement in the time-to-expiry convention, manifest Treasury curve); `implied_forward` (regression of C − P on K near the money; the vendor's parity forward as cross-check — their iv and Greeks are never inputs, since quotes across contracts are not synchronised snapshots); `to_grid_surface` (OTM mids, liquidity filter using iv_bid/iv_ask, butterfly and calendar pruning); `fit_ssvi` (θ at the SPEC pillars by isotonic least squares, global (ρ, η, γ) constrained; **eSSVI per-pillar ρ is the default for imported surfaces**, `--ssvi` opts out); `snapshot_config` (dated YAML with provenance: vendor, checksum, filters). CLI `volsto-import --vendor hdn --date … --underlying SPX`. Slices are grouped by (root, expiration) because SPX AM and SPXW PM share dates; fits use expiries from 3 weeks and |k| ≤ 0.25, vega-weighted; expiries under 3m are reported outside the acceptance region. `scripts/capture_yfinance.py` writes today's SPX/SPY chain in the same layout for daily accumulation.

Measured on 2022-09-15: implied forwards within 0.7 bp (≤ 6m) / 3.5 bp (2y) of the vendor's; SSVI RMS 0.20 vp inside ±20% from 3m; eSSVI RMS 0.17 / 0.08 vp (3m–2y / 6m–2y); 1–2m weeklies 1–4 vp off under SSVI; ≈ 1 s per day.

Implementation notes (M3b, measured on the 2022 H2 SPX sample): contracts are grouped by (root, expiration) as the vendor does — SPX (AM) and SPXW (PM) share expiration dates but differ by one day in `T`; the implied forward is the regression `C − P = a + b K` on two-sided pairs within ±10% of spot (`F = −a/b`, discount `−b`, delta-method standard error), which agrees with the vendor's closest-strike parity forward within 0.7 bp to 6m and 3.5 bp at 2y (the vendor discounts at the Treasury rate, the market-implied rate was 0.4–0.6% higher); our Black-76 inversion reproduces the vendor's `iv` on `iv_flag = 0` rows to a median 0.1 vp near the money. Butterfly pruning is iterative convexity of OTM prices in strike; the calendar check runs on the common quoted `k` range of consecutive slices (a global fixed point dropping the worst violating quote), which is also what `GridSurface` enforces. `θ_T` is fitted at the SPEC pillar tenors (1m, 3m, 6m, 1y, 18m, 2y, 3y) by isotonic least squares through the slices' ATM total variances; the global `(ρ, η, γ)` least squares (vega-weighted, |k| ≤ 0.25, expiries ≥ 3 weeks) enforces both SSVI butterfly conditions through a bounded reparametrisation of `η`; imported surfaces default to eSSVI — `ρ` fitted at the pillars (piecewise-linear `ρ_T`, `ESSVISurface`) — with `--ssvi` as the single-`ρ` opt-out; synthetic surfaces (`configs/surfaces/reference_ssvi.yaml`, the study parameters) stay plain SSVI. Expiries under 3m are reported in the residual tables but sit outside the acceptance region. Fit quality on four sample days, inside ±20% moneyness: RMS 0.12–0.22 vp and max 0.4–1.0 vp from 3m to 2y (eSSVI RMS 0.08–0.16 from 6m); the 1–2 month weeklies of these high-volatility days are 1–7 vp off — a single power-law φ cannot follow them, so the §13 target of 0.3 vp holds in RMS from 3m but not as a maximum below 3m. The snapshot YAML has `market` + `ssvi` sections (loadable by `load_ssvi_surface`) and a `provenance` section (vendor, file and manifest SHA-256, filters, forwards, rate curve, fit residuals, code version). `scripts/capture_yfinance.py` writes the same 34-column layout (blank calculated columns, `iv_flag = 7`) plus a manifest with a user-supplied Treasury curve.

Data sourcing decisions: build against the free sample; buy the HistoricalData.net full archive ($799 one-time, daily updates $79/month) for a multi-year backtest; ThetaData's free tier (1y EOD) as a second-source check; rates from FRED/SOFR or the desk's OIS curve; forwards and dividends always implied per expiry from put-call parity on the chain.

### 13.1 M10 Part 0 — eSSVI calendar repair: the invariant, enforced by construction (2026-09-16)

**Invariant.** For every eSSVI surface the importer returns WITHOUT a fallback (`calendar_fallback is None` — every one of the 127 sample days), the exact derivative `∂_T w(k, T) ≥ margin = 1e-4` per year holds for every `|k| ≤ 3` and every `T ∈ [1/365, max_maturity]`, both one-sided limits at every knot included — proven, not sampled. The escalation steps return weaker invariants, recorded on the result: `margin0` proves `∂_T w ≥ 0` only, `k_abs_1` proves it on `|k| ≤ 1` only, and `ssvi` returns a plain SSVI (single ρ, calendar-free for non-decreasing `θ_T`) that the certificate does not examine. Synthetic searches reach the fallback; the real sample never does.

**Why a third pass.** Twice the constraint was checked at points that missed where the violation lives:
- first, the pillars were missing from the grid;
- second, the secant over an edge step `h = 1e-3·segment` does not bound the one-sided limit (`secant = w'(b−) − (h/2) w_TT`, and `w` is concave in `T` near the short pillars).

Per the owner's rule, this pass enforces the invariant itself instead of densifying the grid again.

**Exact derivative** (`volsto.market.surface.essvi_dw_dt`). On each knot segment (knots `{1/365} ∪ pillars ∪ {max_maturity}`), `θ_T` and `ρ_T` are affine. With `x = kφ(θ)`, `S = √((x+ρ)² + 1 − ρ²)`, `g = 1 + ρx + S` and `χ = γ + (1−γ)θ/(1+θ)`:

`∂_T w = (θ'/2)·g·(1 − χ + χ/S) + (θρ'/2)·x·(1 + 1/S)`

At a knot, each one-sided limit uses the slopes of the segment on that side. The constructor check, the repair, the certificate and the dense check all use this formula.

**Certificate** (`certify_calendar`). Branch and bound on the exact derivative:
- The exact value is computed on 121 `k` × 48 `T` per segment.
- Each cell between those points gets a lower bound (`dw_dt_lower_bound`). Every factor's range on the cell is exact, from monotonicity:
  - `θ` and `ρ` are affine;
  - `φ` decreases in `θ`, and `χ` increases;
  - `S²` is linear in `ρ` and convex in `x`;
  - `g` is monotone in `ρ` and convex in `x`.

  The factors are then combined with interval arithmetic.
- Cells whose bound reaches the floor are proven. The others are bisected, and their centres evaluated.
- The outcome is `certified`, `violated` (with the points) or `inconclusive` (budget exhausted; never a pass).
- Soundness was checked on 2·10⁵ random cells.

**Repair** (`repair_calendar`), an exchange method:
- It imposes `∂_T w ≥ margin + headroom` (1.1e-4) on the initial point set, knot limits included.
- After each feasible solve, the certificate either proves `≥ margin` on the continuous range, or returns the points below `margin + headroom/2`. Those points join the constraint set.
- An input that is already proven is returned bit-identical.
- Unchanged: θ frozen; ρ_T and γ free inside their boxes; only η capped; the escalation order margin 0 → ±1 → plain SSVI.

**Constructor.** It refuses when the exact `∂_T w` on its ±1 grid (pillars included, both limits) is below −1e-10. The unrepaired importer now passes 108/127 days, against 109 under the second pass's step check and 112 under M3b's; 2022-10-31 is newly refused. Both committed eSSVI snapshots load.

**Proof in tests.**
- 500 seeded violating draws. If 5% of draws failed, all 500 would pass with probability 7·10⁻¹².
- Each is checked three ways: the repair's proof, an independent certificate of the constructed surface, and the exact derivative on 1201 × 3000 plus both limits at every knot.
- Plus the 29 draws the second-pass verifier found failing, and a case that only the certificate's cuts can repair.

**Gate at M10 Part 0** (before the θ fix of 2026-09-17 below, which supersedes its fit-error, skew and repaired-day numbers; `scripts/essvi_calendar_gate.py`; 134 s, single core (`outputs/essvi_gate/gate_run.log`); recalibrated: no; no Monte Carlo).
- Repaired eSSVI on 127/127 days: 25 repaired (7–9 solves, 0 cuts) and 102 bit-equal; no fallback.
- All 127 are proven `∂_T w ≥ 1e-4` (smallest certificate bound 1.000e-4).
- Dense exact minimum +1.099e-4 per year, against +2.886e-5 in the second pass (`outputs/essvi_gate_second_pass_2026-09-16/essvi_gate.md`). Unrepaired: 25 negative days, worst −2.113.
- Fit error medians, |k| ≤ 0.2, 3m–3y (fit's points / surface returned):

  | variant | RMS | max |
  |---|---|---|
  | eSSVI, repaired | 0.1957 / 0.1384 | 1.3455 / 0.6108 |
  | SSVI | 0.2375 / 0.1939 | 1.4506 / 0.7520 |

- On the 25 repaired days: worst RMS 0.3644 (2022-12-28), worst max 2.0324 (2022-12-29).
- Against the second pass the cost is nil (`outputs/essvi_gate_vs_second_pass/essvi_gate.md`, the committed gate re-run with the stored second pass as its baseline, 132.5 s): the same 25 days repaired, median RMS 0.1957 → 0.1957, the largest max increase +0.0003 and the largest RMS change below 5·10⁻⁵ (2022-12-15). Against the first pass (`outputs/essvi_gate/essvi_gate.md`, baseline `outputs/essvi_gate_prefix_2026-09-16`: 112/127, 21 repaired) 21 → 25 days are repaired, with the largest max increase +0.0184 and the largest RMS increase +0.0012 (both 2022-12-15).
- ATMF skew, medians, 1y / 2y / 3y:

  | variant | 1y | 2y | 3y |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | eSSVI, repaired | −0.2897 | −0.1966 | −0.1626 |
  | SSVI | −0.2875 | −0.2240 | −0.1910 |

- Anchor 2022-12-30:
  - 2y skew −0.1940 against −0.2181 with SSVI; 3y skew −0.1660 against −0.1910;
  - RMS 0.2605 → 0.2684;
  - proven floor 1e-4 (lower bound 1.003e-4).
- History rerun: 127/127 imported (139 s, `outputs/essvi_gate/history_run.log`). The 127 snapshots, reloaded: all proven, 0 negative.
- Discriminator (115 s): fitted 60-day SSR 0.874 ± 0.117 / 0.800 ± 0.080 / 0.799 ± 0.069 / 0.834 ± 0.068 at 1m / 3m / 6m / 1y. Gate verdict unchanged: "surface artefact" (the owner's gate label), from the below-one leg at 1m only; no fit artefact detected — raw and fitted SSR agree within 2 se at every pillar and window (|z| ≤ 0.72).

**Where the owner's Part 0 specification and the measurements disagree** (implemented as measured; reported 2026-09-16):
1. *Constraint range.* A repair on the trusted strike range repairs nothing — inside `|k| ≤ 0.25` there is no violation on any of the 127 days. The violations live in the wings. Under the exact certificate, 25 of the 127 unrepaired fits violate on the Dupire range `|k| ≤ 3` against 19 on the constructor's `|k| ≤ 1`: six days pass a `±1` check while Dupire would silently floor a negative `∂_T w` (the pre-M10 point check raised on 15 days, hence the 112-date history). The objective stays on the trusted range; the constraint is on `±3`.
2. *What moves.* Only `ρ_T` (with `γ` inside its box and `η` capped); `θ_T` is frozen, not projected. The violation comes from `θ_T(1 + ρ_T)` and `θ_T` is already made non-decreasing twice; a `ρ`-only refit is feasible on 127/127 days.
3. *"The 2y–3y skew is no longer extrapolated" cannot be met by any repair.* The pillar counts are P = 5 / 6 / 7 on 1 / 117 / 9 days: on 118 of the 127 days the last pillar is at or below 2y, so the 3y skew is flat-forward-variance extrapolation whatever the repair (only one day quotes at or beyond 3y; the median longest quoted maturity is 2.25y). What the repair delivers is that on every passing day the 2y/3y skew comes from the eSSVI `ρ_T` instead of the single-`ρ` fallback (medians −0.1966 / −0.1626 against −0.2240 / −0.1910).
4. *The discriminator cannot give a verdict on ~10 days.* Its estimator needs at least 21 dates (windows of 60 and 100 business days; `MIN_INCREMENTS = 20`), so it was re-run on all 127 repaired days, plus a 21-day smoke run with 20-day windows. **Answer to the owner's question: the SSR below 1 is real (quote-level) at 3m–1y and undecided at 1m; the eSSVI fit does not create it.** The gate's label stays "surface artefact" because the owner's rule also requires the raw SSR to be significantly below 1 at every pillar ≤ 1y, and at 1m the raw SSR is 0.942 ± 0.153 (60-day window), and 0.942 + 2 × 0.153 = 1.249 is not below 1 (renaming that gate state, e.g. to "undecided", is the owner's call); on the fitted pillars (3m–1y) the raw and fitted SSR agree (0.80 / 0.79 / 0.77 raw against 0.80 / 0.80 / 0.83 fitted) and read significantly below 1. So the owner's gate keeps study B's historical world skipped: the 0.85 SSR is supported at 3m–1y and not established at 1m, where the raw estimator's error is too large to decide.

**The fit's θ clamp — fixed (owner's decision, 2026-09-17).** Until then `fit_ssvi`'s residuals clamped `θ_T` beyond the last pillar while the surface it returned extrapolated the last forward variance, so the reported errors described a different surface (median RMS 0.1957 vp reported against 0.1384 vp on the surface returned; max 1.3455 against 0.6108).
- **Fix.** The fit evaluates the surfaces' own `ssvi_theta`, `pillar_rho` and `ssvi_total_variance`: the (e)SSVI formula has one implementation (`tests/test_surface.py::test_ssvi_formula_has_one_implementation` walks `volsto/` and `scripts/`). This is the third occurrence of "a fit or a check evaluates a different surface than the one returned", after the repair's θ (Part 0) and the flattened eSSVI (§13.2), so the invariant is enforced in one place.
- **η cap.** The fit and the repair now cap η on one θ range (`_eta_theta_range`): the pillar range widened to the constructor's butterfly range `[θ(1/365), θ(max_maturity)]`. The unrepaired fit used to cap on the pillar range only; no 2022 H2 day came near the limit (largest θφ(1+|ρ|) 0.86 and θφ²(1+|ρ|) 2.05, against 4), but a day with γ > 1/2 and a steep short end would have failed to construct.
- **Invariant, measured.** On all 127 days and the three variants (repaired, unrepaired, plain SSVI), the fit's reported vols equal the returned surface's at every retained quote (largest difference 0.0). `tests/test_import_hdn.py::test_fit_points_are_the_returned_surface` pins it on a bit-equal, two repaired, an unrepaired and a plain-SSVI fit, and on the forced SSVI fallback.
- **What does not move.** The surfaces' own numbers are bit-identical (`test_ssvi_surfaces_are_bit_identical_to_the_written_formula`), so no surface built from a config moves. Every fitted snapshot does.

Gate re-run (`outputs/essvi_gate_theta_fix`, 161 s of process time; recalibrated: no; baseline `outputs/essvi_gate`; the θ fix alone, without the η-cap change: `outputs/essvi_gate_theta_only`):

| | M10 Part 0 | after the fix |
|---|---|---|
| repaired eSSVI passing, proven `∂_T w ≥ 1e-4` on \|k\| ≤ 3 | 127/127 | 127/127 (smallest bound 1.000017e-4), no fallback |
| repaired / bit-equal days | 25 / 102 | 30 / 97 (6–9 solves, 0 cuts) |
| unrepaired eSSVI passing the constructor | 108 | 105 |
| unrepaired days negative on \|k\| ≤ 3 (worst `∂_T w`) | 25 (−2.113) | 30 (−2.045) |
| repaired fit error, median RMS / max, fit's points | 0.1957 / 1.3455 | 0.1304 / 0.5071 |
| same, surface returned | 0.1384 / 0.6108 | 0.1304 / 0.5071 |
| plain SSVI, surface returned | 0.1939 / 0.7520 | 0.1901 / 0.7529 |
| ATMF skew medians 1y / 2y / 3y, repaired | −0.2897 / −0.1966 / −0.1626 | −0.2885 / −0.2059 / −0.1702 |
| same, plain SSVI | −0.2875 / −0.2240 / −0.1910 | −0.2881 / −0.2240 / −0.1911 |

- **Which days moved.** The θ fix alone moves 118 days. The 9 days with a 3y pillar fit no quote beyond it and are bit-equal under the θ fix alone. The shared η cap then moves all 127 days, by at most 1.1e-6 vp in RMS and 3.3e-7 in skew (the reparametrised optimiser's path), the anchor included.
- **Changed calendar status.** Newly repaired: 2022-09-09, 09-12, 09-13, 10-18 and 11-03. Now refused by the unrepaired constructor: 09-13, 10-28 and 11-01.
- **Per day, on the returned surface.**
  - RMS change: median −0.0047 vp; largest decrease −0.0153 (2022-08-12); largest increase +0.0004 (2022-11-25).
  - Max: largest increase +0.046 (2022-11-25).
  - 2y skew change: median −0.017 (range −0.027 to +0.008). 3y skew change: median −0.0135.
- **The repair's own cost on the 30 repaired days.** Median RMS 0.1519 → 0.1515. Worst increase +0.037 RMS and +0.352 max, both on 2022-12-22. Worst day RMS 0.3644 (2022-12-28); worst day max 2.0324 (2022-12-29).
- **Anchor 2022-12-30.** RMS 0.2684; 2y / 3y skew −0.1940 / −0.1660 (unchanged at four decimals).
- **Unchanged.** Disagreement 3 below: P = 5 / 6 / 7 pillars on 1 / 117 / 9 days.
- **Discriminator** on the new repaired history (`outputs/essvi_gate_theta_fix/discriminator`; history 124 s and discriminator 101 s of process time; recalibrated: no). The fitted 60-day SSR is 0.873 ± 0.117 / 0.800 ± 0.080 / 0.800 ± 0.069 / 0.834 ± 0.068 at 1m / 3m / 6m / 1y (before: 0.874 / 0.800 / 0.799 / 0.834, same errors). The raw SSR is unchanged, raw and fitted agree within |z| ≤ 0.74, and the verdict stays "surface artefact" from the 1m leg. The canonical `outputs/essvi_gate` (read by S2, S7 and `studies/m8b`) is replaced with the re-runs.
- **Not re-run yet.** Every leverage, study and backtest date built on a fitted snapshot waits for the importer's spot/dividend decision (below). The ladder-halving counts of §13.2 were measured on the pre-fix surfaces.

**Short-end implied dividends — the owner's premise refuted (2026-09-17), the spot and the funding curve fixed (owner's decisions 2026-09-22).** The owner asked to smooth the implied dividend yield below about 1M from the first reliable tenor, reading the 49.6 % 7-day yield of 2022-10-27 as a parity-regression artefact. The regression is not the cause (`scratchpad` probe; 127 days):
- **The forwards are precise.** Standard error about 0.02 index points from 96–136 put–call pairs on that day, and the vendor's closest-strike forward agrees within 0.1 point.
- **The cause is a spot asynchrony.** The HDN `underlying_close` is the official 16:00 close, while SPX options quote until 16:15 (vendor README, N10/N11). On 2022-10-27 all nine expiries under two weeks share one offset: the options price an index about 0.9 % below the close. The largest offsets fall on evenings of large after-hours earnings releases (25 and 27 Oct: −38 and −92 bp; 26–28 Jul: +23, −24 and +26 bp).
- **Measuring the implied spot.** The option-implied spot S* is the intercept of `ln F − r T` on T over the expiries up to 45 days; its standard error is 0.45 bp (median), at most 1.0 bp. It differs from the close by more than 3 standard errors on 111 days and by more than 10 bp on 36; the median is +1.7 bp and the extremes −92 and +37 bp.
- **Against S*, the yields are ordinary.** 7-day yields lie in [−0.5 %, +2.7 %] against [−16.8 %, +49.6 %] measured from the close; 30-day yields in [0.0 %, +2.4 %] against [−2.6 %, +12.8 %]; 6-month yields in [0.9 %, 1.35 %] against [0.4 %, 3.2 %].
- **The clamp as specified would do harm.** It replaces forwards the options imply to 0.02 points by ones off by up to 90 bp (median 7 bp; more than 10 bp on 35 days), which moves the short-end smiles off their centre. It also leaves 1M–1y contaminated: on 2022-10-27 the 30-day reference yield is itself 12.8 %.

**Decisions (2026-09-22): (a) fix at the spot, and discount on the option-implied funding curve in the same recompute.** Implemented in `volsto/market/import_hdn.py`:
- **Funding curve** (`implied_funding_curve`). Every expiry's parity regression gives a discount factor with its standard error (`ForwardEstimate.discount`, `discount_stderr` — the box-spread rate of that expiry). Zero rates at the knots `FUNDING_KNOTS` = 1m, 3m, 6m, 1y, 2y, 3y (those up to the last quoted expiry) are fitted by weighted least squares to `−ln DF_i`, weights `1/var(ln DF_i)`, in `DiscountCurve`'s own interpolation (`ln DF` linear in `T` between the knots, the last slope extended), so the curve returned reprices every expiry the fit saw; a one-day expiry weighs `T²` less than a one-year one. The snapshot's `market.rate_curve` is this curve; the Treasury curve stays in `provenance.rate_curve_percent`, the fit in `provenance.funding` (knots, zero rates, Treasury zero rates, weighted RMS and largest residual of the per-expiry implied rates, the largest spread). A spread beyond 200 bp at a knot is logged.
- **Spot** (`implied_spot`). The intercept of `ln F_i − r_i T_i = ln S − q T_i` over the expiries within `SPOT_WINDOW_DAYS` = 45 (the earliest three when fewer), weighted by the forwards' regression errors, standard error inflated by `max(1, χ²/dof)`. `market.spot` is this level; **`market.close`** (new, `MarketConfig.close`, omitted while `None` so every existing config maps and hashes as before) keeps the vendor's official print. The close is a fixing, not a pricing input: `CalibrationSpec.key_payload` leaves it out. An offset beyond `SPOT_ASYNC_BP` = 10 bp and `SPOT_ASYNC_SE` = 3 standard errors is logged and recorded (`provenance.spot`: close, spot, stderr, offset, z, the window's flat carry, expiries used, the flag).
- **Carry curve.** `forward_curve_from_forwards(spot, rate_curve, forwards)`: `DF_q(T) = F(T) DF_r(T) / S` at the retained expiries, log-linear between — dividends, repo and whatever basis the funding curve does not carry. The forwards, the log-moneyness of every quote, the implied vols and the fitted surfaces are unchanged by construction.
- **Importer tag.** `IMPORTER_TAG` = 2026-09-22 (`volsto/market/import_hdn.py`, guarded by `importer_guard.json` and `test_importer_tag_guard` over the importer, surface and curve sources) is written into every snapshot's provenance; the backtest store treats a snapshot imported under another tag as stale (§10.3).
- **Who reads the close.** `SurfaceHistory.from_snapshots` (the realised `ln_spot` of the SSR history; a snapshot without `market.close` is refused), and the backtest: the realised fixings (`close()`), the history record's `ln_spot`, the level trades are struck at (`build_product(..., st.close, ...)`, the dry run included) and a `close` column in the fit rows; the pricing state keeps `spot`.

Measured on the 127 days (`outputs/essvi_gate/spot_funding_summary.csv`; the regeneration below):
- The fitted surfaces are identical to the θ-fix run to round-off (largest RMS change 1e-16 vp): 127/127 proven, 30 repaired, median RMS 0.1304 vp, the discriminator table and verdict unchanged.
- Spot offset `spot/close − 1`: median +2.1 bp, range −91.7 (2022-10-27) to +35.6 bp; 38 days beyond 10 bp, all 38 flagged; standard error median 0.45 bp, largest 2.2 bp.
- Carry, median [range]: 7d +1.78 % [−1.5, +3.3]; 30d +1.55 % [+0.25, +2.53]; 6m +1.55 % [+1.32, +1.98]; 1y +1.52 % [+1.37, +1.78] — against −16.8 % to +49.6 % at 7d and up to 3.2 % at 6m before.
- Funding minus Treasury zero rate, median [range], bp: 1m +16 [−65, +114]; 3m +24 [−90, +59]; 6m +49 [+20, +79]; 1y +35 [+13, +52]; 2y +30 [−11, +62]; 3y −1 [−15, +16]; the largest spread of a day is 54 bp at the median and 114 bp at most (no warning fired). The weighted RMS of the per-expiry implied rates is 7 bp at the median (34 bp at most); the largest single-expiry residual beyond one month is 41 bp at the median, with one expiry at 13 % (down-weighted by its own error). 81 days reach five knots (last expiry under 2.86y), 46 reach six.
- PVs discounted on the funding curve move by roughly the spread times the maturity: +35 bp at 1y is −0.35 % of a 1y premium.

**Anchor switch (owner's decision 2026-09-22).** S5–S7, M7, M8b, the discriminator and the default grid now read the repaired eSSVI 2022-12-30 snapshot `configs/surfaces/snapshots/hdn_2022H2/spx_2022-12-30.yaml` (the whole 127-day repaired set is committed there; `hdn_2022H2_ssvi/` stays the plain-SSVI set, regenerated by the same importer, for the plain-SSVI paths and tests; both carry the 2026-09-22 market section). The historical 2F fit and the discriminator default to the repaired history `outputs/essvi_gate/hdn_history_repaired.csv`. On the new anchor the SPX marking fit (first order, `fit_2f_marking`) reads: at `(ssr 1.0, ε 0.10)` ν 2.109, ρ₁₂ +0.650, ρ_SX1 −0.873, only the 1y skew point binding (steep edge) against ν 1.940, ρ₁₂ +0.740 with both points binding on the plain-SSVI anchor; at `(1.5, 0.05)` ν 1.735, ρ₁₂ +0.300 with the 3y point binding at the steep edge, and no ρ₁₂ collapse on either pair (it fired at +0.988 before); `(1.75, ε ≥ 0.2)` stays interior; `(2.0, 0.3)` binds at 1y on the flat edge. The M7 P1 marking artefacts, M8b studies A–D, the delta estimator and S6/S7 are re-run on it (§8.2 records what moved when each lands); the 25-date proof of concept is recomputed under the new importer.

**Provenance of the histories.** Before M10, `outputs/m7/hdn_history.csv` was an eSSVI history on 112 dates (the days whose eSSVI passed the check); only the committed snapshots under `configs/surfaces/snapshots/hdn_2022H2_ssvi/` and the M8b discriminator's fitted column were plain SSVI. The committed snapshots are not regenerated by this change; the gate and the repaired history are written under `outputs/` only.

### 13.2 M10 Part 3 — the eSSVI surface in the calibration spec (2026-09-16)

**Invariant.** A surface is built from a config only by `surface_from_config`, and a surface config is read from a file only by `load_surface_config`; `tests/test_surface_config.py` walks `volsto/` and `scripts/` for any other site. Before this pass `build_market` and `surface_of` built `SSVISurface.from_config` from the scalar `ssvi.rho` — the mean of the pillar ρ the importer writes — so an eSSVI snapshot reached every leverage calibration and every risk bump as its flattened SSVI; only `load_ssvi_surface` honoured the `essvi` section.

**Config.** `SSVIConfig.rhos` holds one ρ per ATM pillar (|ρ| < 1); with it set the factory returns `ESSVISurface(T_i, σ_i² T_i, ρ_i, η, γ)` (its constructor re-runs the exact ∂_T w check) and the scalar `rho` is informational. `load_surface_config` merges a snapshot's `essvi: {rhos}` section strictly; `snapshot_spec(base, path)` puts a snapshot's market and surface on a spec, and the viewers' grid, `studies/m8b` and the M7/M8b scripts go through it. `SSVISurface.from_config` is an alias of the factory.

**Keys.** `to_mapping` omits a field listed in `OMIT_WHEN_NONE` while it is `None` (`omitted_when_none`, the one place), so a plain SSVI config maps and hashes exactly as before and an eSSVI config hashes its pillar ρ — proven on recorded keys of the committed specs and grids and against the 291-row cache manifest. The default grid's two eSSVI LV point ids changed deliberately (nothing had stored them). The manifest schema is unchanged: an eSSVI entry's `surface_rho` is the scalar mean.

**Checks.** The importer's certificate re-proves on the rebuilt surface (2022-10-21, lower bound 1.002610e-4, identical). The flattening mattered — on 2022-10-21 the ATM skew at 1y / 2y / 3y is −0.2449 / −0.1668 / −0.1371 with the eSSVI against −0.2128 / −0.1546 / −0.1271 flattened, and the variance-swap vol 29.18 / 28.35 / 28.22 % against 28.85 / 28.15 / 27.99 %. Every perturbation kind layers on the eSSVI. The curvature ladder's butterfly bump needs more than the default 4 halvings on 62 of the 127 eSSVI days (51 need 5, 11 need 6; the 25 calendar-repaired days are all among them, the other 37 are unrepaired eSSVI days; measured on the surfaces before the θ fix of §13.1, to be re-measured with the re-runs), while every flattened surface passes with 4: the per-pillar ρ, not the repair, is the cause. A ladders risk run on eSSVI surfaces uses `max_halvings=6` (default unchanged, pinned by a test).

**Marking points.** A marking point's id names its surface, not its content: the precompute records `surface_digest` (market, surface including `rhos`, local vol, perturbation — the LV id is `lv:` + the same digest) in the point's manifest, and `--resume` recomputes a stored marking point whose digest differs or is missing.

## 15. Smile dynamics, SSR and fitting the 2F parameters (M7)

### Part 1 — smile-dynamics analytics
Numerical SSR at t = 0 for the LSV (book §12.4.3 joint-bump trick) and for pure SV (§9.8); the analytic decomposition eq. 12.52 with R^LV from 12.53–12.54 and R^SV from 9.21; the slow short-horizon regression estimator. Tests as §4.4, plus enabling the M5 test "model-regime ATM-vol shift per unit Δ = SSR_T·s_T". Vols of ATMF vols (eq. 12.56). Conditional smile after a spot move at horizon t; Var(V) decomposition.

### Part 2 — historical estimators from a surface history (`calibration/history.py`)
Input `SurfaceHistory`: dated snapshots (importer configs or synthetic) with forward curves; pillars at constant time-to-maturity T ∈ {1m, 3m, 6m, 1y, 2y, 3y}. Per date and pillar: `vs_vol` (log-contract strip), `atm_vol` (k = 0), `skew` (dσ/dk at k = 0), `ln_spot`. Estimators with Newey–West standard errors:
1. `volvol_hist(T) = sqrt(252)·std(Δ ln vs_vol(·,T))` and the cross-pillar correlation matrix of Δ ln vs_vol.
2. `SSR_hist(T) = slope(T) / mean skew(·,T)`, slope from regressing Δ atm_vol(·,T) on Δ ln_spot (book eq. 9.3 read historically); report R².
3. Mean and latest ATMF skew term structure.
4. Realised spot/vol correlation per pillar (diagnostic).
Windows: 250 days default for 1 and 3, 60 days for the SSR (regime-dependent), both reported; rolling versions for Part 4.

Test (identifiability): simulate the pure 2F model daily for 3 years (Table 8.2, flat 20% VS curve), compute each day's VS vols from the factor state and ATMF vols/skews via the mixing solution, feed the estimators: `volvol_hist` recovers eq. 7.39, `SSR_hist` recovers eq. 9.21 (order-one accuracy, 2 SE), cross-pillar correlations recover eq. 7.20.

### Part 3 — the P1 marking calibration by SABR break-evens (`analytics/reparam.py`, `analytics/breakeven.py`, `calibration/targets.py`, `calibration/fit_2f.py`, `risk/shadow_rotation.py`; owner's M7 Part 3 FINAL and the report decisions of 2026-09-14, supersedes the earlier Part 3 designs)

**Reparametrisation** (`reparam.py`, round trip to 1e-12): `ω1 = 2να_θ(1−θ)`, `ω2 = 2να_θθ`, `λ1 = ρ_SX ω1`, `λ2 = ρ_SY ω2`, `χ = (ρ_XY − ρ_SX ρ_SY)/(sqrt(1−ρ_SX²) sqrt(1−ρ_SY²))`, `α_θ = ((1−θ)² + θ² + 2ρ_XY θ(1−θ))^{−1/2}`; inverse `θ = ω2/(ω1+ω2)`, `ρ_Si = λ_i/ω_i`, `ρ_XY` from `χ`, `ν = (ω1+ω2)/(2α_θ)` (`χ = 0` where a factor is absent).

**Break-even observables** (absolute vol units — `dσ̂`, not `d ln σ̂`; `σ̂_T` the ATMF vol, `σ_0` the instantaneous vol `L(0,S_0) sqrt(ξ_0^0)` in the engine, the 1M ATMF vol as the market proxy in the targets, 0.2194 against 0.2200 on the reference surface): `SensiSpot = σ_0 ∂σ̂_T/∂ln S_0`, `SensiX_i = ∂σ̂_T/∂X_0^i`, `SpotVolCovar(T) = (1/(σ_0 dt))<d ln S, dσ̂_T> = SensiSpot + ρ_SX SensiX + ρ_SY SensiY`, `VolVar(T) = (1/dt)<dσ̂_T, dσ̂_T>` (the quadratic form with `ρ_XY`), `SSR_T = SpotVolCovar/(σ_0 Skew_T)`, `vovol_T = sqrt(VolVar)/σ̂_T`.

**Engine** (`breakeven.py`): most-likely-path level `σ̂_T² = (1/T)∫E[V_t | S_T = F_T] dt` with the Gaussian conditional expectation at order one (exponent in the module docstring, confirmed by a brute-force conditional Monte Carlo: the `½Σλ_i c̃_i(t)` term is there, the alternatives are 11–15 s.e. away); local volatility along the ATM path as `L² ≈ exp(a(t) + b(t) ln S)`, `b = ∂ ln L²/∂ ln S` at the forward (the ATM-skew-invariant choice; naked model `a = b = 0`; the `½ b² W` term of the first draft was removed after a Bachelier check showed it wrong at its own order). First-order sensitivities `SensiX_i = ½ ω_i A_i(T) σ̂_T` (eq. 7.38), `SensiSpot = σ_0 σ̂ B/(2 W_T)`, `Skew_T = λ1 J1 + λ2 J2 (+ Skew^LV)` with `J_i = ∫₀ᵀ V̂_t c̃_i(t) dt/(2σ̂³T²)` (eq. 8.54 — the owner's `σ̂²` is a transcription slip, `σ̂³` verified against the mixing skew); `SpotVolCovar` and `Skew` affine in `(λ1, λ2)` at fixed `(k1, k2)`, `VolVar` quadratic in `(ω1, ω2)` (to 1e-12); differentiating the closed form in `ln S_0, X_0, Y_0` is the route that never needs `∂²σ̂/∂ln K²`. Prefactor convention (measured, gate below): the sensitivities take the market ATMF vol as prefactor (the order-two level correction supplied by the market), the SV skew keeps the order-zero VS vol of eq. 8.54 — a `(σ̂_VS/σ̂)³` rescale of the skew was tried and removed (it moved the skew the wrong way by +5–10% / +16–29% / +47–84% at ν = 0.5 / 1 / 1.74 against the simulated skew, the plain order-one skew being within 0.6–7%). Key facts verified numerically: `SpotVolCovar` depends on `(k1, k2, λ1, λ2)` only; `VolVar` on all seven; `simulated_breakevens` (finite differences of the ATMF vol under spot and factor shocks on common paths, the Part 1 machinery) is the truth check.

**Gate — first-order closed forms against simulation** (`scripts/m7_breakeven_gate.py`, `tests/test_breakeven.py`; Table 8.2 and a `ρ12 = +0.5` set, flat and sloping curves, 2·10⁵ paths, `T ∈ {3M, 6M, 1Y, 2Y}`; relative discrepancy analytic/simulation − 1, mean over the grid):

| ν | SpotVolCovar | VolVar | Skew (order one) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.5 | −0.3% (−1.0 … +0.3) | +0.1% (−0.7 … +0.9) | +1.3% |
| 1.0 | −1.4% (−2.7 … 0.0) | −1.0% (−2.7 … +0.9) | +3.3% |
| 1.74 | −4.7% (−8.0 … −1.3) | −5.4% (−8.8 … −2.4) | +7.5% |
| 2.5 | −10.1% (−16.5 … −3.8) | −13.2% (−19.3 … −7.3) | +13.6% |

At ν = 0.5 every node is within 2 standard errors (+0.5% floor); the short-factor sensitivity carries the error (−13% / −21% at ν = 1.74 at 1Y / 2Y, the long factor within 2%); both estimates are first order in the vol of vol and the gap grows with ν as the owner anticipated. On the cached LSVs (1F ω = 1, 2, 3 and 2F, no calibration) the LSV-mode engine (`local_slope_from_leverage` with the market skew) is within ±7% on `SpotVolCovar` and ±13% on `VolVar` at 3M / 1Y except 1F ω = 3 at 1Y (−17% / −28%), the LSV's SSR 2.5 (3M) / 2.2 (1Y) on the reference SSVI.

**Owner's decisions (M7 Part 3 FINAL, 2026-09-14).** (1) The SSR dial is neither a naked-kernel nor a realised-LSV SSR target: `ssr_target` feeds the break-even formula `VoV_BE` only; the calibrated LSV's numerical SSR is a reported diagnostic. (2) `nu_cap` default 2.5, a config value, with the warning "nu at cap X; first-order break-even engine ~15% biased beyond ~4; raise only if stage-3 simulation validates" when a fit binds it. (3) `Corr_BE = ρ_SABR` always: VolVar rebuilt from the achieved covariance, never `|ρ| = 1` forced by a missed target. (4) A tight skew cannot make `|L − 1|` small on a steep short skew; no shape-only leverage targeting; `|L − 1|` reported. Confirmed and kept: `σ̂³` in `J`; `VoV_SABR` absolute (`ν_SABR atf`); the engine and its gate. The library is its own system (SSVI/eSSVI, its own conventions, SSR and skew tolerance as free inputs), a research / risk tool. **Report decisions (owner, on the M7 Part 3 report):** (i) the leverage-included P1 covariance reading is kept (production's definition; the naked reading is trivially satisfied by ρ = Corr_SABR); (ii) the engine's full quadratic VolVar is kept, the slide's half-weight cross-term coefficients recorded as an unresolved normalisation; (iii) step 0 as built plus the radicand guard `6 smi² + 3 atf cvx ≥ 6 smi² (1 − c)`, `c = 0.5` config, logged when it fires (never on the 127 SPX 2022 H2 snapshots nor the placeholder); (iv) SmoothBreakEven's log-log quadratic accepted; (v) the soft all-pillar penalty is the historical-mode default (`skew_mode="auto"`, weight 10), two-point stays the marking default, the recovery test un-xfailed under that default; (vi) the forward-skew ratio is a diagnostic (measured 1.0–1.17), not a target; (vii) the placeholder SSVI is unsuitable for marking work (ν past the cap, ρ = −1, ρ12 = +1, θ ≈ 0.06): marking studies and the shadow rotation use the SPX snapshots, the placeholder stays for the M4/M6 baselines; `|ρ12| > 0.9` is flagged as the two-factor structure collapsing; (viii) `nu_cap` 3.5 (warning kept), the stage-3 assertion (simulated SpotVolCovar and VolVar within 10% of the fit's targets, with the first-order miss and the engine bias separated in the message; restricted to the engine-bias term on the M7 Part 3 report of 2026-09-15 — the target miss is reported, not asserted — see the stage-3 paragraph below) and `iterate_against_simulation=k`; (ix) the shadow rotation under both recalibration policies — measured below.

**Step 0 — SABR reduction** (`targets.py`; ACT/365, `k = ln K/F`, `x = k/√T`): the library's quotes `Atf_365 = 100 σ̂(0,T)`, `Smile_365 = 100·2·∂σ̂/∂x`, `Convex_365 = 100·∂²σ̂/∂x²`, converted with the owner's formulas `atf = Atf_365/100`, `smi = Smile_365/(100·2√T) = ∂σ̂/∂k`, `cvx = Convex_365/(100·T·(atf/atf_ref)^p)` (`atf_ref = 0.3`, `p = SabrW_Power = 1` default); `ν_SABR = sqrt(6 smi² + 3 atf cvx)`, `VoV_SABR = atf ν_SABR`, `Corr_SABR = 2 smi/ν_SABR`, `Skew_SABR = smi`. *Reading (flagged):* in these units the owner's `sqrt(6 smi² + 3 atf cvx)` is the lognormal `ν_SABR` (Hagan β = 1 inversion, exact at `p = 0`, verified on quadratic smiles to 1e-6); the confirmed absolute `VoV_SABR` is the same formula in absolute smile units (`atf smi`, `atf² cvx`), and the owner's `Corr_SABR = −2 smi/VoV_SABR` is that formula with the smile quoted positive for a put skew; the library keeps `Skew_SABR = ∂σ̂/∂k` (negative, the units of the eq. 8.54 naked skew it is compared with). `p = 1` makes `3 atf cvx = 3 atf_ref ∂²σ̂/∂k²`; against `p = 0` it moves `ν_SABR` by +0.4% to +0.5% on the reference SSVI and −2.5% to −3.8% on SPX 2022-12-30 (negative ATM vol curvature there). Flags as before (negative radicand, `|ρ| > 1`, finite differences, `ν² T > 1`).

**Step 0 on (e)SSVI surfaces and the desk's SABRW (2026-09-26).**
- *Closed form.* On an SSVI-family smile at fixed `T` the ATM derivatives are `∂σ̂/∂k = θρφ/(2σ₀T)` and `∂²σ̂/∂k² = θφ²(1 − 2ρ²)/(4σ₀T)` (negative for `|ρ| > 1/√2`), so step 0's correlation depends on the pillar's `ρ_T` and ATM vol only: `Corr_SABR² = 4ρ² / (6ρ² + 3 (atf_ref/σ₀)^p (1 − 2ρ²))`, and with `p = 0` exactly `Corr_SABR = 2ρ/√3` — `θ`, `η` and `γ` cancel. Verified to four decimals against `marking_targets` at every pillar of both anchors. `|Corr| > 1` for `|ρ_T| > 0.80` at `σ₀ ≈ 0.22` (`p = 1`), `0.866` (`p = 0`). The plain-SSVI anchor (one `ρ = −0.760`) reads −0.90 at every pillar; the repaired eSSVI anchor's 1y pillar (`ρ_T = −0.844`) reads −1.048, clipped. On an SSVI surface the break-even correlation targets therefore carry no information on the market's curvature.
- *The market itself.* On 2022-12-30 the raw quotes' ATM derivatives (a local cubic on `|k| ≤ 0.15√T`, weights 1/half-spread) give `|Corr| > 1` at every expiry from 2.5 months (1y: −1.45 at `p = 1`, −1.17 at `p = 0`): the ATM implied-vol curvature is negative relative to the squared skew, beyond what a SABR smile can represent at a point (a flattened 2022 downside, §6.2, is a plausible reading — not measured). A SABR fit over SABRW's central zone (the 15-delta put to the smile's minimum; the short-maturity β = 1 formula) gives `ρ` −0.73 / −0.79 / −0.81 / −0.74 / −0.73 at 3m–3y, never at a bound, 0.2–0.4 vp RMS (on the eSSVI's smile: −0.79 / −0.86 / −0.91 / −0.87 / −0.88); the weighting (equal, 1/half-spread, vega) moves `ρ` by at most 0.05. The defect is the point reduction, not the surface.
- *The owner's documents* (photographs shared 2026-09-26: "P1 Calibration", "sabrw_slides_2023", "Equity illiquid volatility marking"). The desk's P1 break-evens are steps 0 and 1 of this section — the conversion with `SabrW Power` and `atf_ref = 0.3`, `VoV_SABR = sqrt(6 smi² + 3 atf cvx)`, `Corr_SABR = −2 smi/VoV_SABR`, `VoV_BE = (atf_3M/atf_T)(SSR/2) VoV_SABR`, `Corr_BE = Corr_SABR`, SSR target 1 (SABR dynamics are SSR 2; the SSR is not read from SABRW), MatMin 3M, SmoothBreakEven on — **but the triplet `(Atf, Smile, Convex)` is computed from the SABRW parameters stored per underlying (fits to the market) and interpolated to the tenors 3M, 6M, 1Y, 2Y, 3Y, 5Y, 7Y, 10Y**: its correlation is SABRW's fitted `ρ`, inside (−1, 1) by construction. volsto has no such store; at M7 it read the triplet off its own surface by finite differences at the money — the substitute that fails above. SABRW, the desk's in-house SABR: β = 1, the short-maturity smile `σ̂(k) = k / Σ_zones G` over the zones from 0 to `k`, `G(a, b) = [asinh((ρ + (ν/σ) h(b))/√(1 − ρ²)) − asinh((ρ + (ν/σ) h(a))/√(1 − ρ²))] / (h′((a + b)/2) ν)` — the integral of `1/g`, `g(x) = √(σ² + 2ρνσ h(x) + ν² h(x)²)` — with `h` continuous piecewise linear, slopes `EX_d, T_d, 1, T_u, EX_u` on `x < x_EXd`, `(x_EXd, x_Td)`, `(x_Td, x_Tu)`, `(x_Tu, x_EXu)`, `x > x_EXu`; `x_Td ≈ N⁻¹(0.15)·0.30·√T` (a 15-delta put at a 30 % reference vol), `x_Tu` the SABR smile's minimum, `x_EXd = 4 x_Td`, `x_EXu ≈ 2 x_Tu` (plain SABR in the central zone); fitted per slice by weighted non-linear least squares (Levenberg–Marquardt with bounds). By the slides' own account it is not arbitrage-free by design (calendar, butterfly) and sensitive to noise: it cannot replace the eSSVI under Dupire and the leverage; its place is step 0. Not specified by the documents (owner: not available): the tenor interpolation, the business-day to 365 conversion, the fit weights, and whether the convexity computed from the SABRW parameters carries the `(atf/atf_ref)^p` factor before the P1 conversion divides it out (with SABRW inputs the two readings differ by about 0.005 in `Corr` at `ρ ≈ −0.8`; they diverge only for `|ρ|` above about 0.9).
- *Owner's decisions (2026-09-26).* The skew band `ε` is the marker's input (unchanged); `k2` may be tuned; the skew band should also apply at the short end.
- *Preview* (first order: `fit_2f_marking` with step 0 fed by the central-zone fits at the eSSVI's ATM level — per-expiry SABR levels are not calendar-monotone and the fit refuses them): on 2022-12-30 the correlation targets move from −0.83…−1.00 to −0.70…−0.81 and the vol-of-vol targets rise 20–35 %, while the covariance targets (`= SSR · atf_3M · skew`: the curvature cancels) barely move; the fit reads `ν` 2.11 → 2.08, `θ` 0.12 → 0.15, `k1` 8.6 → 7.4, `ρ_SX1` −0.87 → −0.75, `ρ_SX2` −0.94 → −0.63, `ρ12` +0.65 → +0.11, `χ` −0.99 → −0.70, smallest eigenvalue 0.0006 → 0.075; the covariance misses keep their size and the 1y skew point still binds. On 2022-09-15 `ν` 3.17 → 2.72, on 2022-12-02 2.64 → 2.41. The skew band is not the main obstacle to the covariance targets (`ε` 0.10 → 1.00 cuts the largest miss from 10.7 to 5.4 % with SABRW inputs, still binding); `k2` fixed at 0.2 is (`k2` = 0.5: 4.7 %, 1.0: 5.0 % at `ε` = 0.10; 2.0 breaks the fit, `ν` at its cap). The band binds only at 1y and 3y (5y moved to 3y), and the model's skew is 1.3–1.6× the market's at 3m–6m. Measured by preview scripts; the SABRW module and a reproducible 127-day measurement of the options (SABRW step 0, `k2` free, the band on more pillars) are the next commits, the switch of the default and the recompute follow the owner's review.

**SABRW in volsto (`volsto/market/sabrw.py`, 2026-09-26).** The documents' smile as specified: `σ̂(k) = k / I(k)`, `I` the sum of the closed-form zone integrals (checked against adaptive quadrature to 1e-10 in every zone, both signs of `k`), plain SABR in the central zone (bit-equal), continuous and C¹ at the zone edges; `atm_triplet` gives `(σ, ½ρν, ν²(1 − 3ρ²/2)/(3σ))` and `breakeven_from_triplet` returns `(ν, ρ)` exactly (the step-0 reduction at `p = 0`). volsto's readings of the documents' "≈", flagged in the module: `x_Td` without the `½σ²T` term of the put delta; `x_Tu` the plain SABR smile's minimum on `k > 0` (the floor `0.01√T`, flagged, when there is none); `x_EXu = 2 x_Tu`. The fit (`fit_sabrw`): weighted least squares with the caller's weights (volsto's measurement: the half bid–ask spread in vol, floored at 0.05 vp), scipy's bounded trust-region solver (the documents: Levenberg–Marquardt with bounds), the box `σ ∈ [0.01, 3]`, `ρ ∈ [−0.999, 0.999]`, `ν ∈ [0.001, 20]`, slopes `∈ [0.05, 10]`; a slope whose zone holds fewer than 3 quotes at the initial parameters is held at 1 (plain SABR) and flagged, as are a change of zone coverage during the fit and a parameter within 0.2 % of its box width. On the HDN sample (quotes to `|k| ≤ 0.6`) the extreme zones rarely hold quotes (`EX_d` never from 3m on) and the upside slope `T_u` is weakly determined (at its bound at 3m and 6m on 2022-12-30) — the documents' own warning about the configuration; the central parameters are stable: on 2022-12-30, `ρ` −0.758 / −0.785 / −0.827 / −0.715 / −0.717 and `ν` 0.94 / 0.82 / 0.61 / 0.52 / 0.45 at 3m–3y, fit error 0.92 / 0.53 / 0.23 / 0.35 / 0.38 vp over all the expiry's quotes. Step 0 reads it through the option below (*Step 0 from the desk's SABRW fits*).

**`k2` fitted — option (2026-09-26; owner: `k2` may be tuned).** `BreakEvenFitConfig.k2_bounds` (default `None`: `k2` fixed at 0.2, unchanged): the first minimisation fits `k2` on a geometric grid of `k2_grid` = 9 points, plus the fixed value when inside the bounds, then a bounded scalar refinement; every candidate runs the `k1` search with `k1 > k2 + k1_min_gap` on the same pillar quadratures, so the fitted objective never exceeds the fixed-`k2` one. On the SPX anchor at `(1.0, 0.10)` with today's step 0: `k2` 0.889 in (0.05, 1.5); covariance misses +11.5 / −14.4 / −8.0 / +6.1 / +10.4 % → +1.6 / −5.0 / +4.4 / +2.7 / −4.2 % at 3m–3y (objective 0.055 → 0.0072); `ν` 2.11 → 2.51, `k1` 8.6 → 14.8, `θ` 0.12 → 0.18; smallest correlation eigenvalue 0.0006 → 0.0069 (still flagged: the correlation targets are step 0's); still binding at 1y; 1.9 s → 3.0 s.

**The skew band on more pillars — option (2026-09-26; owner: the band should also apply at the short end).** `skew_pillars` takes any increasing list of maturities and `skew_eps` one value for all or one per pillar (default `(1, 5)` and 0.10: the two-point band, its names `T_s` / `T_l`, `eps_pair` and its mapping unchanged); each point is a slab on the model's naked skew in the first minimisation, and the free short end is 1M and the fitted pillars below the first constrained one. On the SPX anchor at `(1.0, 0.10)` with `(0.25, 0.5, 1, 3)` the fit is feasible with the model's skew inside ±10 % at all four points (binding at 3M on the steep edge and at 1y on the flat one) — but the covariance targets are then missed by +27 / +35 / +57 / +71 / +67 % at 3m–3y (the two-point band: at most 14 %), and fitting `k2` (0.63) only brings the worst to 51 %: with SSR 1, pinning the model's skew at every pillar and meeting the covariance targets pull against each other. The trade-off (`ε` by pillar, `k2`, step 0's input) is part of the 127-day measurement.

**Step 0 from the desk's SABRW fits — option (2026-09-26).** `marking_targets` and `fit_2f_marking` take `step0`, a triplet source (`Step0Triplets`; default `None`: the surface's ATM derivatives, unchanged). `import_hdn.sabrw_fits(points, t_min=, t_max=)` fits SABRW to every retained expiry with at least seven quotes (weights the half bid–ask spread in vol, floored at 0.05 vp) and `SabrwTermStructure.from_fits(fits, atm_vol)` makes the source: at `T` the ATM level is the pricing surface's (per-expiry SABR levels are not calendar-monotone), the skew and curvature are the fits' central-zone terms, interpolated linearly in the desk's 365-day quotes `Smile_365`, `Convex_365` and held flat beyond the fitted expiries (volsto's rule: the owner has no details of the desk's tenor interpolation); two fits on one maturity keep the one with more quotes. The reduction runs at `p = 0` — the triplet is physical, so the `(atf/atf_ref)^p` normalisation of the desk's stored convexity cancels in its round trip — and returns the fits' own `(ν, ρ)` at the surface's ATM level. The skew the band compares with is the source's; `σ_0`, the 3M anchor and the ATM term structure stay the surface's; the flags name the source. On 2022-12-30 (42 expiries from 0.05y to 2.97y fitted in 1.5 s; every fit holds at least one wing slope its quotes do not reach and `T_u` is at its bound in 14, `ρ` and `ν` never at a bound), SSR 1, the two-point band at 0.10:

| step 0 | `k2` | `Corr_BE` 3m / 6m / 1y / 2y / 3y | `ν` | `ρ12` | `χ` | smallest eigenvalue | covariance misses 3m–3y |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| surface | 0.2 | −0.833 / −0.954 / −1.000 (clipped) / −0.970 / −0.997 | 2.11 | +0.650 | −0.990 (bound) | 0.0006 | +11.5 / −14.4 / −8.0 / +6.1 / +10.4 % |
| SABRW | 0.2 | −0.757 / −0.785 / −0.825 / −0.714 / −0.717 | 2.22 | +0.198 | −0.699 | 0.064 | +9.5 / −10.3 / −13.2 / +6.8 / +12.7 % |
| surface | 0.889 (fitted) | as the first row | 2.51 | +0.569 | −0.858 | 0.007 | +1.6 / −5.0 / +4.4 / +2.7 / −4.2 % |
| SABRW | 0.870 (fitted) | as the second row | 3.06 | −0.175 | −0.849 | 0.053 | +0.1 / +0.1 / −1.3 / +3.2 / −2.5 % |

The fits' `ν_SABR` is 4–33 % above the surface's ATM reading at a skew 1–6 % smaller in magnitude, hence `VoV_BE` 6–32 % higher and `|Corr_BE|` lower. With `k2` fixed the source alone does not reduce the covariance misses; it removes the degenerate correlation (no clipped pillar, `χ` off its bound, the smallest eigenvalue ×100). With `k2` fitted as well the covariance targets are met to 3.2 % with a well-conditioned correlation. One date: the 127-day measurement decides. Default unchanged: the importer does not store the fits yet and no stored result moves.

**Step 1 — targets:** `VoV_BE(T) = (atf_3M/atf_T)(ssr_target(T)/2) VoV_SABR(T)`, `Corr_BE = Corr_SABR`, `SpotVolCovar_target = Corr_BE VoV_BE`, `VolVar_target = VoV_BE²`; `ssr_target` scalar, per-pillar or a curve (default 1). SABR dynamics are SSR 2 (a 1F `k → 0` Bergomi model matching a SABR pillar meets both targets at `ssr = 2`, 1e-5, `tests/test_breakeven.py`); `ssr = 1` halves `VoV_SABR` (sticky strike). The SSR the targets imply is `ssr · atf_3M/σ_0` before smoothing: 0.955 on the reference SSVI, 1.084 on SPX. **SmoothBreakEven** (library definition, default on): `ln VoV_BE` replaced by its least-squares polynomial in `ln T` of degree `min(2, n − 2)` (adjustments within 0.8% on the reference SSVI, 0.1% on SPX; the raw curve kept; flagged beyond 5%). **MatMin** 3M (sub-3M pillars dropped with a flag); pillars 3M–10Y inside the surface (SPX is quoted to 3Y: 5Y / 10Y dropped with a flag). `σ_0 = atf(1M)`. Policy check: at `ssr = 1` the raw `VoV_BE` equals `½ VoV_SABR atf_3M/atf_T` (absolute reading) to 1e-12. Historical mode (non-default): `VolVar = (atf volvol_hist)²`, `SpotVolCovar = SSR_hist σ_0 skew`, `Corr = SpotVolCovar/sqrt(VolVar)`.

**P1 break-evens** (`fit_2f.py`, first order, market ATMF prefactor): `SensiX_i = ½ ω_i A_i atf`, `Skew_naked = λ1 J1 + λ2 J2` (eq. 8.54), `SensiSpot = σ_0 [(S_T − λ·J_T) + (1/T)∫₀ᵀ f(t)(S_t − λ·J_t) dt]` (the spot sensitivity the leverage generates by absorbing the skew residual, eq. 12.52 in covariance form, `f = σ²(t)/(σ̂_t σ̂_T)` on the market ATMF curve), `SpotVolCovar_P1 = SensiSpot + ½ atf λ·A`, `VolVar_P1 = SensiSpot² + 2 SensiSpot ½ atf λ·A + SensiX² + SensiY² + 2 ρ_XY SensiX SensiY`. *Reading (flagged):* the owner's `VolVar_P1` carries `σ_0² f(T)²` and `σ_0 f(T) A` terms, i.e. `SensiSpot = σ_0 f(T) ≠ 0` — the leverage-included break-even (the "SVC_PILV" of the previous message), not the naked kernel's (`SensiSpot = 0`, reported as `svc_naked`). The owner's `VolVar_P1` has both cross terms at half weight (`(λ_i/2) σ_0 f A_i`, `(ω1ω2/4) ρ_XY A1 A2`); the full quadratic form of the gate-validated engine is used (`tests/test_fit_2f.py::test_volvar_p1_quadratic_form`). Identities: affine in `λ`; `λ = 0` gives `σ_0 S R^LV(Mkt)` (order-one `R^LV` 3.007 / 3.014 / 3.027 at 3M / 6M / 1Y on the reference SSVI); the Table 8.2 kernel's P1 SSR 2.641 / 2.352 / 2.082 against the cached LSV's numerical 2.48 / 2.14 (3M / 1Y, within 10%); reduces to the naked form when the market skew is the kernel's own. Historical mode interpolates the residual `S − λ·J` linearly between pillars (zero leverage term when the naked skew matches the pillars).

**Step 2 — first minimisation:** `min Σ w_i (SpotVolCovar_P1 − target)²` over `(k1, λ1, λ2)`, `k2 = 0.2` fixed, `w` relative (`1/target²`, default; uniform or explicit per pillar), subject to the **two-point hard skew constraint** `Skew_naked(T) ∈ [(1 ∓ eps)·Skew_SABR(T)]` at `T_s = 1Y` and `T_l = 5Y` only (`skew_eps` a float or `(eps_s, eps_l)`, default 0.10; a constraint maturity that is not a fitted pillar moves to the nearest pillar with a note — 3Y on SPX), and the ν box `|λ1| + |λ2| ≤ 2 ν_cap` (the step-3 feasibility: `min ν = |λ1 + λ2|/2`). Exact 2-D QP per `k1` (candidate enumeration, checked against SLSQP), geometric `k1` grid plus bounded refinement. `skew_mode="soft"` (all-pillar penalty `skew_weight Σ (Skew_naked/Skew_SABR − 1)²`, box only) is available, not the default. **Binding / infeasibility (never a silent railed fit):** the two skew slabs always intersect for `eps ≥ 0`, so `ssr_target` cannot empty the constraint set; an incompatible `(ssr_target, skew_eps)` pair **binds**, and the fit carries the message "skew constraint binds at T=… (lower/upper edge: naked skew = (1±eps) x Skew_SABR = …): naked skew … vs market … (gap, eps …); SpotVolCovar achieved vs target: …" per binding point. Infeasibility needs the ν box (e.g. SPX with `nu_cap = 0.3`): the skew rows are relaxed by the smallest uniform `δ` (a linear programme, box kept hard) and the fit returns with "infeasible: no (lambda1, lambda2) meets the two-point skew constraint … inside |lambda1| + |lambda2| <= 2 nu_cap = … at any k1 …". `FitResult.status` ∈ {interior, binding, infeasible}. Binding map on SPX (`ssr ∈ {1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2} × eps ∈ {0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3}`): every pair binds except `ssr 1.75` with `eps ≥ 0.2` (interior, every SpotVolCovar within 1.6%); at `ssr ≤ 1.25` the 1Y point binds at the steep edge (the P1 covariance needs a naked skew steeper than market to lower the LSV's SSR, eq. 12.52), at `ssr 2` at the flat edge.

**Step 3 — second minimisation:** `min Σ w_i (VolVar_P1 − VolVar_target)²` over `(ω1, ω2, χ)` with `ω_i ≥ |λ_i|`, `ν ≤ ν_cap` (SLSQP inequality, ten starts), `χ ∈ [−0.99, 0.99]`; then the inverse reparametrisation. Marking: `VolVar_target = (SpotVolCovar_P1/Corr_BE)²` (= `VoV_BE²` when step 2 meets its target; the requested value is reported). Historical: the empirical VolVar is kept (the empirical correlation reaches −1.02 at 3M on the one-year synthetic history and the rebuild from it biased ν by −20%). The ν-cap warning is logged and attached when step 2's box or step 3's cap binds.

**Always reported:** parameters; naked skew at 1Y / 5Y vs market (within eps or the binding message); the free short-end naked skew (1M, 3M, 6M); per pillar SpotVolCovar / VolVar targets and achieved, `Corr` target and model, first-order P1 SSR, the SSR the targets imply; stage 3 (`stage3_validation`): mean `|L − 1|`, the LSV's numerical SSR (diagnostic), the naked mixing skew, the LSV's simulated SpotVolCovar / VolVar, the forward 90/110 skew at 1y-into-1y and 2y-into-1y against the spot 1y 90/110 skew.

**Stage-3 assertion restricted to the engine-bias term (owner, on the M7 Part 3 report; re-run 2026-09-15 `scripts/m7_p1_marking.py --surfaces spx,reference --no-rotation`, 350 s, four cache hits, recalibrated: no).** The 10% assertion applies to the engine bias only — the simulated break-evens against the analytic (first-order) break-evens evaluated at the fitted parameters; the miss of the targets by a binding fit is already reported by the binding status and is carried in the check table (`gap_vs_target`, `first_order_miss`) and in one sentence of the failure message as reported, not asserted. Re-stated verdicts: placeholder (1.0, 0.10) **FAIL** (engine bias SVC +8% / +23% at 3M / 1Y, VolVar +17% / +52%; misses SVC +97%, VolVar −35% reported), placeholder (1.5, 0.05) **FAIL** (VolVar +9% / +17%, SVC +4% / +8%; misses +31% / −35% reported), SPX (1.0, 0.10) **FAIL** (ν 1.94: SVC +10% / +17%, VolVar +17% / +29%; the 3M covariance miss +11% reported), SPX (1.5, 0.05) (ν 1.39) **PASS** (SVC ≤ 5.5%, VolVar ≤ 9.5%, misses ≤ 2.6%). The letters are unchanged from the previous rule but the cause is now the engine alone: at ν ≥ 1.9 the first-order VolVar is 17–52% below the simulation and the 1Y SpotVolCovar 17–23%, i.e. the bias sets in well below the ν ≈ 4 of the ν-cap warning on these fits; `iterate_against_simulation` stays interior-only with its divergence guard, as built. Raising the cap lowers the placeholder LSV's SSR (2.32 → 2.06 at 3M, 1.49 → 1.34 at 3Y) at the price of |L − 1| 0.35 → 0.42 and a collapsed two-factor structure. *Iteration against simulation* on the binding SPX (1.0, 0.10), `k = 2` (3 calibrations, 477 s): it **diverges** — ν 1.94 → 2.08 → 2.28, ρ → −1 / −1, ρ12 → +1, the SpotVolCovar engine bias 17% → 27%, the VolVar gap vs target 0.31 → 1.53 — dividing the targets by the simulated/analytic ratio cannot move a constrained covariance and feeds the free direction; the loop now stops and keeps the best iteration when the worst gap grows, with a note. On the interior SPX pair `(1.75, 0.20)` (`k = 2`, 451 s, ν 1.087, θ 0.389, k1 10.2, ρ −0.85 / −0.87, ρ12 +0.997 — collapse flag) iteration 0 already sits at the assertion edge (SpotVolCovar gap 5.1%, VolVar 10.0%; engine bias 5% / 9%); the first correction overshoots the VolVar (18.6%, ν 1.24) and the guard reverts to iteration 0. The raw ratio iteration therefore does not converge in one step even on an interior fit at this ν; a damped step is the obvious next thing to try and was not built.

**Shadow rotation under the recalibration policies, in the declared convention** (report decision ix and the owner's convention decision of 2026-09-15 — "a convention issue, not a model result"; SPX 2022-12-30, `(ssr 1, eps 0.10)`, M6 headline 3y autocall, 2·10⁵ paths (seed 2024), ±1 rota; % of notional per rota with standard errors; re-run 2026-09-15 in 517 s wall clock from the cache, nothing recalibrated (`scripts/m7_p1_marking.py --no-stage3`, records in `configs/studies/m7_p1_marking/rotation_spx_<policy>.yaml`); the `sticky_breakeven_skew` row is the implementer's diagnostic variant, not one of the owner's two). **Convention** (`ROTATION_CONVENTION`, printed by every report): rota +1 = the 6M 90/110 skew steepens by 0.56 vp (`2/√T` vp per unit log-moneyness); fee = P1 price − LV price; desk P&L per +1 rota for a SHORT position = −(d fee). Levels: P1 92.837 ± 0.045, LV 92.110 ± 0.047 (pure local vol of the same surface, `LVBuilder`, same seed), fee 0.726 ± 0.027 % of notional. The LV rotation is −0.0917 ± 0.0048 under every policy (same three surfaces); the fee's usual rotation `P1 usual − LV rotation` is −0.0055 ± 0.0066 — zero within one standard error: with the P1 set held, the LSV and the local vol reprice a rotation alike, so the fee moves only through the recalibration, and `fee recalibrated = fee shadow` within noise.

The previously reported "positive shadow" was the fee shadow: `shadow = recalibrated − usual` on the P1 price equals the fee's shadow (the LV rotation cancels path by path), so +0.074 means the fee **rises** by 0.074 % of notional per +1 rota when the marking set follows the skew — a **negative** desk P&L for the short note, the deck's "fee increases, negative P&L" claim, reproduced with its ordering: `|desk P&L shadow|` sticky_breakeven 1.37× sabr_linked (10 and 8 standard errors from zero; the variant 4.5). The sign question was a reporting convention, not a model result; the mechanism is unchanged — under every policy the refit set partly offsets the usual P1 rotation, most under sticky break-evens, whose refit moves ν the most per rota (+0.33: the held covariance target against a steeper market skew is met with a larger vol of vol). `tests/test_shadow_rotation.py` asserts on the recorded study: the desk-P&L shadow negative under sticky_breakeven (and reports it under sabr_linked), the magnitude ordering, `fee_shadow` equal to the P1 shadow, the LV rotation recorded with its stderr and the convention string present.

**Reading alternative, measured at first order only (not shipped):** with `SensiSpot = 0` (naked kernel covariance) the same pairs give on the reference SSVI ν 1.32 / 2.12, `ρ_SX1 = ρ_SX2 = −0.804 = Corr_SABR`, `ρ12` 0.996 (χ on its upper bound), and on SPX ν 2.08 / 2.07 with `k1` on its bound 20: without the leverage term, a correlation target flat across pillars can only be met by one perfectly correlated factor. A one-line change of `P1Maps.rows` if the owner prefers it.

**Shadow-rotation greek** (`risk/shadow_rotation.py`): 1 rota = ATM-skew move of `2/√T` vol points per unit log-moneyness (`2/√T ln(110/90)` vp of 90/110 skew: 0.568 at 6M linear, 0.560 with the `k_cap = 0.5` saturation; maturity floored at 1M; positive = puts up), surface perturbation kind `rotation`. Usual rotation = surface rotated, P1 set held, leverage recalibrated; recalibrated rotation = SABR reduction → break-evens → P1 refit → leverage recalibrated; shadow = recalibrated − usual (the P1-price shadow, equal to the fee shadow of the declared convention above; the `fee` column of this FINAL-run table is the P1 price *level* in that run's naming), central differences at ±1 rota under common random numbers through the M5 risk engine (`LSVBuilder` gained `allow_calibrate`).
