**Historical recovery** (three-year mixing history, MatMin 3M, `k2 = 0.28`, windows 250 / 250; owner's tolerances ν, θ, k1 10%, ρ 0.05): the soft all-pillar skew penalty at weight 10 passes (ν 1.798, θ 0.245, k1 5.783, ρ −0.716 / −0.461; weight 1 misses ρ_SX1 by 0.073); the **default two-point configuration misses ρ_SX1** (ν 1.780, θ 0.249, k1 5.801 inside, ρ_SX1 −0.664, off 0.095; ρ_SX2 −0.499) — strict xfail. `eps = 0.02` does not fix it (−0.670); keeping the 1M pillar moves the miss to k1 (+16%) and ρ_SX2 (0.062). One-year order-one history: soft weight 10 ν 1.602, k1 5.22, ρ −0.789 / −0.508; default two-point ν 1.422 (−18%), k1 4.47 with every SpotVolCovar within 2% — five covariance targets do not pin `(k1, λ)` once the short-end skew is free.

**Tests** (`tests/test_breakeven.py` targets, `tests/test_fit_2f.py`, `tests/test_shadow_rotation.py`): the radicand guard (fires on a concave test smile, logged, never on the reference SSVI), the `auto` skew mode, the ν-cap 3.5 warning, the ρ12 collapse note, the stage-3 assertion machinery (`breakeven_check` on synthetic tables and on the cached Table 8.2 LSV: the engine bias asserted, the SpotVolCovar first-order misses of +177% / +118% reported, the VolVar rows without a first-order value judged on the target gap and flagged; `BreakEvenValidationError` carries the result, the iteration plumbing), the recorded study fits' assertion verdicts reported; the break-even formula reproduces the policy at `ssr_target = 1`; the two-point constraint binds only at 1Y / 5Y (3Y on SPX, with the note) and leaves the short end free; `ssr_target` (scalar, curve) and `skew_eps` (scalar, pair) both exercised, soft mode; the binding message on the incompatible SPX `(1.0, 0.10)` pair and no message on `(1.75, 0.20)`; the infeasible message with `nu_cap = 0.3`; the ν-cap warning logged; `Corr_BE = ρ_SABR` kept; the realised LSV SSR of the cached study fits reported, not asserted equal to 1; the engine gate, reparametrisation, historical recovery under the default (soft weight 10, un-xfailed; the explicit two-point configuration a strict xfail), the rotation surface round trip and the refits under the three policies, d(fee)/d(rota) on the cached calibrations with shadow = recalibrated − usual path by path, and the sign test on the recorded study numbers.

**Open questions (owner) after the report decisions.** The seven questions of the first report were decided (above). Left open by the measurements of the decisions: (a) the shadow-rotation sign — **resolved by the declared convention** (2026-09-15, table above): the positive P1 shadow is the fee rising on a +1 rota, i.e. a negative desk P&L for the short note under every policy, the deck's claim and its ordering (sticky 1.37× SABR-linked); no model question remains; (b) `iterate_against_simulation` is only meaningful on interior fits (it diverges on binding ones and is stopped); (c) decided (2026-09-15): the stage-3 assertion is restricted to the engine-bias term, the target miss of a binding fit is reported, not asserted (verdicts re-stated above); what remains open is the engine bias itself — 17–52% on the VolVar at ν ≥ 1.9 against the ν ≈ 4 threshold of the ν-cap warning — so the threshold or a bias-corrected first-order engine is the next question.

*Superseded Part 3 designs:* the staged fitter (M7, f4c7cc7), the hard-skew break-even fitter with four inequalities (adb673a) and the SSR target with a soft skew penalty and floor messages (2ff45dc; its trade-off tables and SPX runs are in that commit's SPEC).

### Part 4 — stability (`calibration/stability.py`)
Rolling refits (stage 1 monthly on a trailing 250-day window, stage 2 daily) → parameter time series with SEs; flag parameters whose day-to-day changes exceed their SE band consistently (unidentified, not informative); plot the objective along the ν ⟷ correlations degeneracy direction. Used by the backtest study. **M7 addendum:** `rolling_fit` rolls the break-even fit in historical mode (`fit_2f_historical` per date) with the status, messages, constraint gaps and SpotVolCovar miss per date, and `flag_unidentified` reads the break-even columns `(k1, λ1, λ2, ω1, ω2, χ)`; the ν ⟷ correlations profile of the staged fitter is replaced by the `k1` profile (the method pins `λ` by `SpotVolCovar` and `ω` by `VolVar`).

### Part 5 — VIX in the 2F model (`analytics/vix.py`, check only)
`VIX²_T = (1/Δ) ∫_T^{T+Δ} ξ_T^u du`, Δ = 30/365; in the 2F model `ξ_T^u` is explicit in (X¹_T, X²_T) (eqs. 7.30–7.35), so VIX futures and calls price by 2D Gauss–Hermite quadrature (book §7.7.2). Implied vol-of-vol at horizon T from the ATM VIX option. Tests: quadrature vs factor Monte Carlo within 2 stderr; VIX² futures vs forward 1m VS variance up to the convexity term; short-horizon implied vol-of-vol → eq. 7.39 as T → 0. Optional stage-1 target for SPX only, fitting futures and ATM VIX vols never the wings (book §7.7.4: the lognormal 2F model under-produces the VIX smile's upward skew).

### M7 implementation notes (2026-09-13; Parts 1–5 built; the Part 3 paragraph below records the superseded staged fitter — the P1 marking calibration of 2026-09-14 is Part 3 above)
Built inline (the phase-1 agent workflow failed on the account's spend limit; its partial modules were reviewed, completed and tested here).

**Part 1 — `analytics/smile_dynamics.py`, `analytics/var_decomp.py` (`tests/test_smile_dynamics.py`).** Everything holds the leverage fixed (book §12.3.2). `ssr_numerical` is the state-bump estimator of §4.4 (joint bump `(ln S_0 + ε σ_0, X_i + ε ρ_SXi)`, central scheme, ATMF option repriced under CRN, the model's own skew from ±1% strikes on the same paths; `kind="spot"` / `"factors"` for the two terms); `ssr_decomposition` is eq. 12.52 with `R^LV` from 12.53–12.54 (flat, ATMF-term-structure or VS-curve expansions; `S^SV` at order one or from the mixing solution); `ssr_short_horizon` the independent regression estimator (slow test); `atmf_vol_of_vol` eq. 12.56 and `volvol_term_structure` the same partials applied to the model's VS strip; `conditional_smile` the smile at horizon `t` given a spot move; `var_decomposition` the SV / leverage / cross split of `Var(∫V)` with the exact discrete moments of the accumulator. Measured (pure 1F, flat 20% VS, 4·10⁴ paths): `R_1y` = 1.500 ± 0.045 against eq. 9.21 = 1.523 at ν = 0.6; `R_1w` = 1.999 ± 0.016 (the book's `R_0 = 2`); Table 8.2 at 1y / 3y inside 1.3–1.7 (the ≈ 1.5 shoulder); joint = factor-only bump for pure SV and the spot term is zero; ε-independent (0.02 vs 0.05). ATMF vol of vol at 1m 0.2760 ± 0.0016 against eq. 7.39 = 0.2763; the state-bump VS vol-of-vol term structure equals eq. 7.39 to 1·10⁻⁵ at 3m / 1y; the conditional ATMF vol moves by `R_T S_T` per unit spot move at one week (3 stderr); `Var(∫V)` by Monte Carlo 5.70 ± 0.06·10⁻⁵ against the exact discrete moment 5.62·10⁻⁵ (the continuous form 5.62·10⁻⁵), leverage terms identically zero for pure SV. The decomposition's hand checks: a market skew equal to the kernel's order-one skew gives `R = R^SV` exactly, a `1/√t` skew gives `R^LV(Mkt) = 3`, ν → 0 gives `R = R^LV(Mkt)`. **M5 regime identity (enabled):** the risk layer's `"model"` delta regime bumps the spot with the leverage held and the factors at zero — its ATM-vol shift per unit `Δ ln S` is the spot-partial term of eq. 12.50 alone (`ssr_numerical(kind="spot").slope`, zero for pure SV), while `SSR_T · S_T` is the slope of the joint (conditional) move; both are asserted, the joint one against the conditional smile — the identity "model-regime shift = SSR_T·s_T" holds for the conditional move, not for the spot-only regime, and the difference is the factor terms (recorded as the reading of the M5 note).

**Part 2 — `calibration/history.py` (`tests/test_history.py`).** `SurfaceHistory` (tidy frame, validation naming date and pillar, wide views, windows), the estimators with Newey–West standard errors (Bartlett, lag rule `floor(4 (n/100)^{2/9})`, delta method for `sqrt` and ratios, Fisher-z and HAC for correlations), `estimate_history` (windows 250 / 60, the SSR also on the long window), the rolling versions, `synthetic_2f_history` (one daily kernel path; VS vols from the factor state; ATMF vols and skews by a per-day mixing batch sharing the factor paths — the ATMF skew by the exact strike derivative, 20× less noisy than an OTM difference — or at order one), `build_hdn_history` (importer per day). Gaussian synthetic recovery within 3 SE. **Identifiability (measured, Table 8.2, flat 20%):** a 30-year daily path gives realised / instantaneous RMS vol of VS vols of 0.996–0.999 at every pillar (no bias, daily discretisation included); on 250-day windows the ratio has a dispersion of about 6% (14 three-year paths: 0.87–1.09, common to all pillars — one draw of the window's vol-of-vol regime) of which the Newey–West standard error captures 3.8% (the iid part); 2 of 14 paths sit at −3σ on their last window (seeds 2 and 11). The SPEC test runs on a typical path (seed 13, 3 years, mixing ATM source, slow: `volvol_hist` within 2 SE + 5% of the in-sample eq. 7.39 target, `SSR_hist` within 2 SE + 0.15 of the order-one target, correlations within 3 Fisher SE + 0.05 of eq. 7.20); a one-year order-one variant (seed 8) runs in the fast suite. Finding on the order-one in-sample target: on a path whose instantaneous variance collapses (seed 7: `ξ_t^t` down to 0.0002, 1.4% vol) the ξ-weighted order-one SSR target at 2y–3y reads 2.4–2.6 while `SSR_hist` gives 1.8 ± 0.3 and the flat formula 1.47 — the order-one target is not robust to extreme term structures; the estimator is. Cost: the per-day order-one skews (quadrature on each day's curve) are 25 s per simulated year, the mixing batch 5 s.

**Part 3 — `calibration/fit_2f.py` (`tests/test_fit_2f.py`) — SUPERSEDED by the P1 marking calibration (Part 3 above); kept as the record of the staged method's measurements.** As specified: stage 1 weighted LS in `log volvol` with `1/SE²` weights on eq. 7.39 (flat) or on the window's mean VS curve (eqs. 7.36–7.38), bounds and the `k1 > k2` wall, ρ12 fixed or from the 3m/2y correlation of `Δ ln vs_vol` (two alternations, root in `(−0.99, 0.99)`), Jacobian singular values, standard errors from `(JᵀJ)⁻¹`, `k2` flagged when on a bound / the weakest direction / SE > 50%, the fit rerun from Table 7.1 Sets I–III with every optimum reported and a `unique` flag; stage 2 in the eq. 8.56 form `(ρ_SX1, χ)` on the pricing date's skew term structure and `SSR_hist`, with the refinement and the naked-vs-market skew table; `skew_scale_degeneracy` along ν → cν, ρ → ρ/c; stage 3 (`stage3_validation`) calibrates the leverage on the pricing date's surface and reports mean |L − 1| inside ±2 sd, the numerical SSR and the leverage-included vol of vol against the historical estimates, the 1y-into-1y and 2y-into-1y forward ATM vol and 90/110 skew, optionally the M6 headline; `FitResult.config_yaml` is loadable (`model` = `BergomiParams`) with the provenance. Deviations and findings: (i) SSR residuals are scaled by `max(0.1, SE)` rather than 0.1 — a noisy 60-day window otherwise dominates the skew term structure (on the one-year synthetic history the 60-day 1m SSR read 2.36 ± 0.16 against a true 1.9 and pulled `ρ_SX2` to −0.9); (ii) stage 2 is multi-start (12 starts): the objective is not convex in `(ρ_SX1, χ)` and a second basin with `ρ_SX2 > 0` exists; the best optimum is kept and the number of distinct optima reported; (iii) the order-one skew and SSR are linear / rational in the correlations, so their curve integrals are computed once per stage-2 call (`stage2_coefficients`) — 280 s → 2 s; (iv) the mixing refinement uses the exact strike derivative of the per-day mixing batch (`mixing_atmf_batch`), not `mixing_atmf_skew`'s OTM difference whose standard error of 0.035–0.065 per pillar at 10⁵ paths swamped the fit (0.002–0.01 with the derivative); (v) `refine_ssr` (default on): the order-one SSR formula overstates the model's own SSR by ≈ 0.1 at ν ≈ 1.7 (numerical SSR of the naked kernel, `ssr_numerical_many`), so the SSR is refined by the same multiplicative correction as the skew — without it the fitted correlations drift (−0.868 / −0.273 against −0.759 / −0.487 on the three-year history). Measured, one-year order-one synthetic history (seed 8, windows 200 / 200, correlation target): stage 1 from every Table 7.1 start converges to ν 1.654, θ 0.251, k1 5.39, k2 0.289, ρ12 −0.06 (true 1.74, 0.245, 5.35, 0.28, 0), `k2` identified; with ρ12 fixed at −0.7 / 0 / +0.7 the vol-of-vol curve is fit about as well by k1 spread over more than 20% — the Table 7.1 degeneracy, resolved by the correlation target; the flat-curve formula on this sloping realised curve biases k1 to 6.33 (+18%), hence the window-mean default. **Three-year mixing history (seed 13, 10⁵ mixing paths per day, windows 250 / 250, the SPEC recovery test, slow):** stage 1 ν 1.765, θ 0.247, k1 5.37, k2 0.287 (SE 0.19), ρ12 −0.011, unique across starts; stage 2 with the skew term structure alone (mixing skew) ρ_SX1 −0.776, ρ_SX2 −0.453 — within the SPEC's 0.05 — with skew residuals ≤ 0.006 and the numerical SSR of the fitted kernel reading `SSR_hist` within 1 SE at every pillar; with the SSR target at the SPEC's weight the fit gives −0.787 / −0.429 (0.03 / 0.06 off: the 250-day SSR's sampling noise, SE 0.06–0.08, moves ρ_SX2 by a few hundredths), and with the 60-day SSR window −0.613 / −0.754 — **deviation for the owner:** the test asserts 0.05 on the skew-only fit and 0.07 on the specified combined fit; the SSR target adds noise rather than information on the split at these sample lengths (and is infeasible on the 2022 H2 data, below), so its weight — or its role as a diagnostic only — is an open question.

**Part 4 — `calibration/stability.py`.** `rolling_fit` (stage 1 every 21 dates on the trailing window, stage 2 every date by default, standard errors carried), `flag_unidentified` (share of consecutive changes beyond the standard-error band; stage-1 parameters compared on refit dates), `degeneracy_profile` = `skew_scale_degeneracy`.

**Part 5 — `analytics/vix.py` (`tests/test_vix.py`).** `VIX_T² = (1/Δ)∫_T^{T+Δ} ξ_T^u du` with `ξ_T^u` explicit in the factor state; futures, calls / puts and Black implied vols by tensor Gauss–Hermite on `factor_covariance(T)` (60 nodes per factor; the future converges at once, the kinked payoffs algebraically: 0.4% on the ATM implied vol of vol between 20 and 60 nodes); `vix_monte_carlo` on the kernel's factor paths. Measured (Table 8.2, flat 20%): the 3m future 0.1791 (quadrature) against 0.1792 ± 0.0001 (2·10⁵ paths), calls within 2 stderr, `E[VIX²]` = the forward 1m VS variance exactly and the future below its root by the convexity gap (2.0 → 17.9 at 3m; grows with ν); the ATM implied vol of vol is the time average of the instantaneous vol of the forward-starting 30-day VS vol — at one week 1.377 against the RMS 1.38 between the `t = 0` value 1.32 and eq. 7.39 at Δ, 1.44 — and reaches eq. 7.39 at Δ within 1% at one day; the model's VIX smile has no upward skew (book §7.7.4): futures and the ATM VIX vol are the only admissible stage-1 targets.

**Real-data run (2022 H2 SPX, HDN sample; `scripts/m7_hdn_history.py --no-essvi`, `scripts/m7_fit_2f_hdn.py`, `outputs/m7/fit_2f_hdn.md`).** 127 trading days (2022-07-01 → 2022-12-30); the eSSVI importer fails the calendar-arbitrage check on 15 of them (total variance decreasing in `T` for some `k`), so the history uses plain SSVI snapshots for every day (130 s for the 127 imports, `configs/surfaces/snapshots/hdn_2022H2_ssvi`). Data finding: the snapshots quote ATM maturities to 1.5–3y only (median 2y), so the 2y / 3y pillars sit on the SSVI extrapolation and their daily changes are an artefact (annualised std of `Δ ln vs_vol` 0.46 / 0.62 against 0.24 at 1y, correlation with the 1y pillar 0.07 / −0.01, spot/vol correlation −0.13 / −0.06 against −0.77 at 1y): the fit uses the 1m–1y pillars, the correlation target 3m/1y; with 127 days the windows are 100 (vol of vol, correlations, mean skew) and 60 (SSR; also reported on 100) — no 250-day statistic exists in this sample. Estimates at 2022-12-30: vol of vol of VS vols 0.84 ± 0.07 (1m), 0.49 ± 0.04 (3m), 0.34 ± 0.02 (6m), 0.26 ± 0.02 (1y) — a decay exponent of about 0.47; cross-pillar correlations 0.80–0.98 within 1m–1y; spot/vol correlation −0.69 to −0.80; **SSR 0.81–0.84 ± 0.07–0.11 on 60 days and 0.86–0.92 on 100 days at every pillar to 1y** (R² 0.4–0.7; daily changes of the ATM vol against the spot: the slope is only 40–55% of the skew — a sticky-strike-like regime over H2 2022), below the floor `R ≥ 1` of both local vol and the 2F LSV (eq. 9.21 lies in `[1, 2]`; `R^LV(Mkt) ≥ 1` for a skew decaying in `T`); latest ATMF skew −0.46 (1m), −0.37, −0.32, −0.27 (1y), −0.22 (2y), −0.19 (3y), flatter in `T` than the model's order-one shape. Fit at 2022-12-30 (ρ12 from the 3m/1y correlation): **stage 1** ν 1.26 ± 0.35, θ 0.22 ± 0.08, k1 12.3 ± 9.0, k2 0.73 ± 1.09 (flagged: SE > 50% — four pillars on 100 days do not pin the mean reversions), ρ12 +0.19, exact fit of the four vol-of-vol points, the same optimum from all three Table 7.1 starts; **stage 2 as specified** (skew + SSR): the SSR target is infeasible, the optimiser drives χ to +1 (ρ_SX1 −0.95, ρ_SX2 +0.12) and the skew residuals reach 0.10–0.20 vol per unit `k` — stage 3 then has the leverage carry the skew, mean |L − 1| 0.26; **skew-only variant**: ρ_SX1 −0.81, ρ_SX2 −0.73 (χ at −1: the long skew asks for more than the fast mean reversions of stage 1 allow), skew residuals 0.02 (1m) to 0.10 (1y) — the naked 2F skew decays faster in `T` than the market's — mean |L − 1| 0.13; on both fits the calibrated LSV's numerical SSR is 1.53–1.63 at 3m / 1y against the historical 0.82–0.84, its vol of vol of VS vols with leverage 0.54–0.63 (3m) / 0.38–0.42 (1y) against 0.49 / 0.26 historical (the leverage adds vol of vol on top of the kernel's 0.48 / 0.25), forward ATM vol 1y-into-1y 22.9–23.1% and 2y-into-1y 23.3%, forward 90/110 skew 4.8–5.2 vol points; the ν ⟷ correlations profile shows stage 1 pinning the scale (objective 118 at ×1.5, 345 at ×2) with stage 2 flat; the rolling refits over the last 30 trading days keep stage 1 at one optimum and stage 2 at its bound (no parameter flagged, the span being 6 stage-2 dates). Reading: over this half-year the market's ATM vol moved less with the spot than any sticky-local-vol-type model allows (SSR < 1), so the SSR target cannot be met by construction and the skew term structure alone should drive the correlations here; the long-maturity skew (flat in `T`) is carried by the leverage — both are properties of the sample and the model class, not of the fitter, and the numbers above are the measured values of a 127-day run, not baselines. Slow test `test_real_data_end_to_end` (sanity only, no fixed numbers) runs when the history CSV is present.

**Raw-slice discriminator (M8b, 2026-09-15; `calibration/raw_history.py`, `scripts/m8b_discriminator.py`, `outputs/m8b/discriminator.md`, `tests/test_raw_history.py`).** Is the SSR < 1 above a property of the quotes or of the SSVI fit?  The pillar history was rebuilt with no surface parametrisation: per day the importer pipeline up to the retained quotes (no SSVI fit), a local quadratic `iv(k) = a + b k + c k²` by unweighted least squares on the raw mid vols inside `|k| ≤ 0.10` of every retained slice with ≥ 5 points (raw ATMF vol `a`, raw ATMF skew `b`), pillars interpolated from the bracketing slices (skew linear in `T`, ATM vol linear in total variance); `vs_vol` copied from the SSVI history (the SSR estimator does not read it).  All 127 days imported (41–50 slices and 2 450–4 040 quotes in the band per day, none dropped; 272 s under concurrent load, recalibrated: no); the same `ssr_hist` on both frames over the common dates.  Raw vs SSVI SSR, 60-day window (z from the independent-SE approximation, conservative since both share the spot increments): 1m 0.94 ± 0.15 vs 0.81 ± 0.11 (z +0.71), 3m 0.80 ± 0.07 vs 0.82 ± 0.08 (−0.16), 6m 0.79 ± 0.06 vs 0.82 ± 0.07 (−0.35), 1y 0.77 ± 0.06 vs 0.84 ± 0.07 (−0.73); 100-day window 1.01 ± 0.11 / 0.90 ± 0.07 / 0.85 ± 0.06 / 0.81 ± 0.05 raw against 0.92 / 0.92 / 0.87 / 0.86 SSVI (|z| ≤ 0.8 everywhere).  The regression slopes agree to 1–6% at every pillar; the difference sits in the mean skew at 1m (raw −0.43 vs SSVI −0.52: the SSVI power-law φ steepens the short end, expiries under 3m being outside its acceptance region, §13), under 1–3% elsewhere.  **Verdict by the owner's rule** (every pillar ≤ 1y: `|SSR_raw − SSR_ssvi| ≤ 2 √(se² + se²)` and `SSR_raw + 2 se_raw < 1` on 60 days): **surface artefact** — the agreement leg holds at all four pillars but the below-one leg fails at 1m only (0.94 + 2 × 0.15 = 1.25); on the pillars the historical fit uses (3m, 6m, 1y; MatMin 3M) the same rule reads *real* (0.95, 0.91, 0.88 < 1) — reported as a sensitivity, not as the gate, for the owner to decide whether the 1m pillar (which no fit reads) belongs in it.  Historical P1 fit with the FINAL fitter for world (ii) (`scripts/m7_fit_2f_hdn.py --skew-mode soft --skew-weight 10 --no-stage3`, `outputs/m7/fit_2f_hdn_historical.yaml`, `configs/studies/m8b/world_historical.yaml` with `n_particles` 800 000): status interior, no messages; ν 3.12, θ 0.077, k1 20.0 (on the upper bound), k2 0.2, ρ_SX1 −0.70, ρ_SX2 −0.95, ρ12 +0.45 (χ at its lower bound −0.99); soft skew gaps +26% (3m), −2% (6m), −19% (1y) against the market — the naked kernel is steeper than the market at the short end and flatter at 1y; SSR targets 0.82 / 0.82 / 0.84; 0.3 s, recalibrated: no.

**Status.** Parts 1–5 built and tested (fast suite: `test_smile_dynamics.py` 5, `test_history.py` 4, `test_fit_2f.py` 4, `test_vix.py` 3; slow: the short-horizon SSR, the LSV decomposition on the cached ω = 3 model, the three-year identifiability and recovery tests, the real-data run). Open questions for the owner: the SSR target's weight in stage 2 (diagnostic only, or fitted with `max(0.1, SE)` scaling as now); the pillar set for real data (the sample supports 1m–1y; a 2y pillar needs quotes beyond 2y on every day); whether the VIX targets should be wired into stage 1 for SPX (Part 5 gives the quadrature; the placeholder in `Fit2FConfig.vix_targets` accepts `(T, implied vol of vol, se)` triples).

---

## 16. Desk conventions recorded
- "P1 deco" = this LSV two-factor Bergomi model. ω = 2ν; the earlier studies' ω = 3 is ν = 150%.
- Surfaces: the desk marks with SABRW (in-house); the library keeps SSVI/eSSVI (public, arbitrage-free by construction, analytic Dupire); `ImpliedSurface` is an ABC so another parametrisation can be added without touching calibration.
- Vega by maturity is by cumulative "waves" (§7.4). Delta regimes include sticky skew (§7.2).
- Up-var indicator "T−1" or "T & T−1"; down-var "T" or "T & T−1"; KO var is up-and-out, close-to-close, variant (b) (§6.1).
